  DejaVu fontconfig. Unit tests pin the `tr` parameters (`clusterHrPrint.test.ts`, `lightCurvePrint.test.ts`,
  `clusterDialog.test.ts`): literals translated, date, numbers and line breaks unchanged.
- **ES7 review fixes:** «16 campos de referencia» (`db.boundary`); `ownHr.limits` names the records that Registrar/Revisar
  [Record/Review] creates; quoted VIREO messages and help headings read Spanish first with the English after them
  (“Temas relacionados” (Related Topics), “¡Error de velocidad!” (“Velocity Error!”), “¡No se encontraron estrellas en el
  campo!” (“No Stars Found in Field!”)); `memberRemoveNote` gives the English name; ID #: › «N.º de ID:» (no longer in
  identical.json); Show Help Topics... › «Mostrar los temas de ayuda...» with an `except` on the glossary's Topics... row;
  Verify Removal from Plot › «Confirmar que se quita de la gráfica»; «V de referencia desplazada»; «El archivo supera los
  1000 registros»; retry buttons «Reintentar la carga…»; scrollable regions named «… (desplazable)».

## Spanish: radio telescope (ES8a)

ES8a translates the radio telescope route (`#radio-control`): the route page and its web scenario, the pulsar exercise card,
the radio clock scenario, VIREO's Set Observation Date/Time, ID dialog and its error box, the main-window radio controls (the
exercise and access menus, the scenario inputs, the access timer, the Radio switch), VIREO's Radio Telescope Control Panel
(Tracking, Slew Rate, the N/S/E/W hold buttons, Slew ▸ Set Coordinates with the coordinate dialog and its J2000.0/B1950.0 entry,
the slew questions, Slew ▸ Hot List with its list and Find dialog, the horizon-limit boxes), the radio configuration card with
VIREO's Optical and Radio Simulation Options, the radio Site dialog and the USER OPTIONS! box, Pulsar Parameters with Find Pulsar
Data and its notices, and the receiver monitor (the windows, controls, marks, the web audition, Record with VIREO's questions,
Identify Recorded Object, the data properties, its own texts of the verified web Saves and the Cannot Close Receiver! box). The
pulsar analysis
route (`#pulsar-analysis`) belongs to ES8b; ES8a translates only what its own routes render.

- **Catalog:** 645 keys (about 9,800 English words): `radio.*` (72), `radioRoute.*` (131), `radioAccess.*` (141),
  `radioReceiver.*` (200), `radioSimOptions.*` (20) and `pulsarParameters.*` (82, the 37 keys the pulsar guide also reads
  included). Every radio file and prefix is at 100 %.
- **Keys that left en.json** because they rendered VIREO box buttons (the rule of ES3): `radioRoute.date.dialog.ok`,
  `radioRoute.date.dialog.cancel`, `radioRoute.date.error.ok`, `radioAccess.refusal.ok`, `radioSimOptions.failure.ok`,
  `pulsarParameters.notice.ok`, `radioReceiver.record.ok`, `.cancel`, `.yes`, `.no` and `radioReceiver.box.ok`; and from the
  `radioAntenna` map `antenna.button.ok`, `.cancel`, `.yes`, `.no`. Their sites render `vireo()` of
  `OBSERVATORY_VIREO_CAPTIONS` (OK, Cancel, Yes, No), with the same English on screen. The receiver's Record indicator showed
  the web values `radioAccess.values.on/off`; it now renders VIREO's RcrdDisp literals (`RECEIVER_DISPLAY_LITERALS.on/off`),
  the same "On"/"Off" in English.
- **Maps, complete:** `radioAntenna` (324 strings), `radioR1`, `radioRecordSave` and `radioFinishSave`
  (`src/i18n/es/maps/radio*.ts`). `RADIO_MESSAGES_EN` stays catalog-backed (its Spanish is the `radio.*` keys). Review: the
  `radioR1` value `saveData` ("File ▸ Save Recorded Data...", VIREO's TRadioLab menu path) left the map; the route renders
  `menuCaptionText(vireo(RADIO_R1_LITERALS.file))` ▸ `vireo(RADIO_R1_LITERALS.saveRecordedData)`, so View original applies.
- **Verified web Saves, partly waiting for ES8b:** the receiver's two Save dialogs (Record's Yes and the finish question's Yes,
  `RecordSaveBox` in `RadioReceiverWindows.tsx`) render their heading, intro, Back, pending note, status, abandoned and busy
  texts from `radioRecordSave`/`radioFinishSave` (Spanish here), but their recording summary, format, losses, how-to, warning,
  file rule, origin, Choose button and stale refusal come from ES8b's `pulsar` map through `createPulsarTranslator`:
  `pulsar.rawExport.scope`, `.format`, `.inactive`, `.inactiveNone`, `.loss.heading`, `.loss.header`, `.loss.headerNone`,
  `.loss.samples`, `.loss.memory`, `.field.*`, `.problem.*`, and `pulsar.save.how`, `.extensionRule`, `.origin`, `.warning`,
  `.choose`, `.chooseAgain`, `.refusal.stale` and the save notice texts. They stay English until ES8b merges; `es-radio.spec.ts`
  opens the Record Save dialog and lists them as `TODO_ES8B_PULSAR_KEYS`, to be removed at that merge. `radioSave.scope` quotes
  the Pulsar Analysis buttons as “Guardar captura como .PLR (verificada)…” and “Descargar captura como .PLR…”, which ES8b's
  `pulsar.save.open` and `pulsar.rawExport.open` must match.
- **VIREO literals:** 98 new originals (589 in all): `NATIVE_CAPTIONS`, the switch's On/Off, `RADIO_LAB_CAPTIONS`,
  `RADIO_HOT_LIST_FORM_CAPTIONS`, `RADIO_OPTIONS_CAPTIONS`, `RADIO_ACCESS_DFM`, `RADIO_SITE_DFM`, `SET_DATE_MENU_CAPTION`,
  `PULSAR_PARAMETERS_CAPTIONS`, `PULSAR_FIND_CAPTIONS`, `RECEIVER_DFM_CAPTIONS`, `RECEIVER_DISPLAY_LITERALS`,
  `RADIO_RECORD_LITERALS`, `RADIO_RECEIVER_LITERALS`, `NATIVE_ID_DEFAULT` and `PULSAR_DATA_NOT_WRITTEN` (the data properties'
  file field). Every radio render site wraps them in `vireo()`, `vireoList()` or `menuCaptionText(vireo(…))`; the rest of the
  audit's sites are `identifier` receipts (lengths, limits, intervals, the dome bytes, option field ids, the epoch names, the
  message-box answer ids, a file name, the reserved data name, and the numeric-error literal that a web note quotes in English).
  After ES8a: 225 receipts. `UNRESOLVED_SITES` is 133 (+27: sites whose literal is chosen at run time, such as
  `D.labels[f]`, `manualButtonCaption(a,d)`, `flagLabel(k)` and the receiver close box); `es-radio.spec.ts` covers them on
  screen.
  Spanish identical to VIREO (identical.json, not in originals.json): Radio, Radio... and PEpoch (MJD):; the N, S, E buttons
  (chosen at run time) fall back to the literal, as `vireo()` does for any literal without an original.
  The hint "Set location & time parameters." is keyed without its `&` (the key rule), as "Set location  time parameters.".
- **Texts the core composes:** `radioIdCaptionText`, `idCaptionText` and `radioIdCaptionLabel` take `tr` (default: the literal).
  The screen passes `vireo(…,'fragment')`; the fragment «Radiotelescopio de {diameter} metros» places the number itself
  (Spanish word order), and English is "100 Meter Radio Telescope" as before. `receiverScreenText(text,tr)`
  (`src/core/radioReceiverDisplay.ts`) translates only VIREO's literal pieces of a receiver caption or drawn annotation (title
  prefixes, the fixed titles, Aperture/Meters, Effective FOV, Beam (HPBW), Deg, Sec): pulsar names and numbers stay as composed.
  `radioHotListMissText` and `hotListEntryAddedText` already took `tr` (ES2).
- **Canvas:** the receiver monitor draws "4.0 Sec " and "600.00 MHz " with `fillText`; the screen passes
  `receiverScreenText(op.text,vireo)`, so only "Sec" becomes «Seg». A mark's time label ("  12.0 Sec", `RECEIVER_MARK_LITERALS`)
  is translated by `receiverMarkScreenText` (`src/core/radioReceiverMarks.ts`, which imports the display module, so it cannot
  live in `receiverScreenText`) on the overlay canvas and in the DOM label. The display ops (core) and every file are unchanged.
- **Kept as recorded (data, not translated):** pulsar names (B/J and the legacy names), the Pulsars.dat values shown in Pulsar
  Parameters and the hot list rows, the profile source name ("No Object Identified" when the beam holds none), the stored ID
  UNKNOWN, file names (Pulsars.dat, Pulsars.HTL, .PLR), hashes, and the English VIREO name that `radioSimOptions.failure.nativeNote`
  quotes. The receiver's data properties show the recording's values as recorded; only the "Data Not Written to File" state is a
  VIREO literal (`vireo()`).
- **Glossary:** new `names` Radio Astronomy of Pulsars, Radio Telescope Control Panel, Radio Simulation Options, Pulsar
  Parameters, Access Pulsar List, Pulsar Identification, Telescope Type, Slew Rate (except where VIREO's Base Slew Rate is meant),
  Find Pulsar Data, Identify Recorded Object, Add Channel, Antenna Aperture, Sound Features; new `terms` sweep › «barrido», beam ›
  «haz», seed › «semilla», timer › «temporizador». Other forms used throughout: Record › «Registrar», a recording › «registro»,
  recorded data › «datos registrados», capture › «captura», audition › «audición»/«escuchar», channel › «canal», tick › «tic»,
  press and hold › «mantener presionado» (the hold itself › «presión sostenida»), programmed slew › «movimiento programado»,
  radio clock › «reloj de radio», altitude limit › «límite de altitud» (a `terms` row), the panel's Drive › «Accionamiento»
  (a `terms` row), live options › «opciones activas», deliver a tick › «Entregar», the radio telescope route › «página del radiotelescopio», options record › «conjunto de
  opciones», Match case › «Distinguir mayúsculas y minúsculas» (as VIREO's Case Dependent), master/slave receiver › «receptor
  maestro»/«(esclavo)» (VIREO's words), Enter a value › «Escribe». «registro» covers both a catalog record (Pulsars.dat rows: «Registro {position} de {count}», «el
  primer registro») and a recording, as in VIREO's own usage; context tells them apart. The `tabs` term no longer requires «pestaña» after
  "reachable with " (the Tab key: «la tecla Tab»). The forbidden-word lint is case-insensitive, so the review replaced 19
  per-string allowances with one rule in `es.test.ts` (`VIREO_PULSAR_NAMES`): only the exact, case-sensitive English VIREO
  names Access Pulsar List, Plot Pulsar Positions, Pulsar Analysis, Pulsar Parameters, Pulsar List and Pulsar Identification
  are removed before the check, so «pulsar el botón» or a lower-case «pulsar» in the same string still fails (self-test).
- **English-visible changes:** none intended. The box buttons, the Record indicator and the menu items read the same English.
- **Files:** `exports-identity.spec.ts` adds one ES8a family run in English, qps and Spanish: the raw capture `.PLR` of a
  model-generated recording, VIREO's Save Recorded Measurements text (with its US-format date and time header) after one manual
  reading, and the saved investigation with the radio configuration, clock and capture. The page clock is paused before the
  capture and only `runFor` moves it, so nothing is masked; proven stable with `--repeat-each=10 --workers=4`.
- **Checks:** `tests/i18n/es-radio.spec.ts` walks the route in English, Spanish and qps (39 steps, with a placed mark and the Record Save dialog). Spanish may repeat an English
  text only when it is in an exact identifier list (units, formats, the S400/S600/S1400 captions, Radio, N/S/E, No, Min,
  Hrs, Bytes, the stored “UNKNOWN”) or matches a narrow pattern (pulsar names, hashes, file names, numbers with units, ISO times); qps
  must mark every other text, `<option>` texts, accessible names, titles and SVG titles included. The VIREO captions of the walk
  (panel and dialog captions, the box buttons, the options dialogs, Pulsar Parameters and Find Pulsar Data, Set Date/Time, ID,
  the receiver controls, Identify, the Save and unsaved-data questions and the Cannot Close Receiver! box) are asserted
  explicitly in Spanish, in English, and in English under View original (English), where the web text stays Spanish. A drawn
  annotation check spies on `fillText`: «Seg», never "Sec", on the receiver annotation and on a placed mark's label (also in its DOM
  label), with the numbers unchanged. Layout at 320 px and 1440 px with the
  DejaVu fontconfig: no page overflow, dialogs inside the viewport, no clipped or crossing control.
- **Not translated here:** the pulsar analysis route and its Save, Load, review and print texts (ES8b); the help files behind
  the panel's Help menu and PLSLSHLP (ES-HELP); the Object X radio card (ES9).

## Spanish: exercise E06, pulsar analysis (ES8b)

The whole recorded pulsar analysis flow (`#pulsar-analysis`) and the Pulsar guide (E06) read in neutral Latin American Spanish on
a `?lang=es-419` preview. Scope: `PulsarLabGuide.tsx`, the `pulsarLab.*` and `plr.*` catalog keys, `PulsarMessages.ts`,
`PulsarSavedRowsMessages.ts` and `PulsarLoadSaveMessages.ts` (the maps `pulsar`, `pulsarSavedRows`, `pulsarLoadSave`), and every
VIREO literal rendered on those screens.

- **Catalog:** 90 keys (`pulsarLab.*` 83, `plr.*` 7 more: the route and the saved-file disclosure); both prefixes are at 100 %.
  Catalog coverage 61.4 % → 63.9 % on ES8b's base; after merging main (ES6, ES8a; 6eecc09), 87.8 % → 90.3 %. The Spanish chunk (`npm run build` on ES8b's base, main
  8367e99) grows from 409 KB raw / 118.3 KB gzip (ES5 measurement) to 494.8 KB raw / about 141 KB gzip (Vite's report); English loads do not fetch it. After merging main (ES6, ES8a) it is
  717.2 KB raw / 194.7 KB gzip (`scripts/check-i18n-dist.mjs` warns over 150 KB; the split decided at ES5 applies). The 36 `pulsarParameters.*` keys that the batch tool also
  lists for these files, `pulsarParameters.reference.sha` among them (shown in the guide's source note), belong to ES8a.
- **Maps:** `pulsar` (443 strings), `pulsarSavedRows` (83), `pulsarLoadSave` (25), typed `Translation<…>` (complete). Registered in
  `src/i18n/es/es.test.ts` (parity and glossary lint). 26 of 31 maps are started after merging main.
- **VIREO text out of the web maps.** The Review dialog caption, its button captions (new `PULSAR_REVIEW_BUTTONS`: Find...,
  Edit..., Delete, Ok, Cancel), its MessageBox texts, the comment editor's caption, field labels and buttons, and the Find
  dialog's captions left `PULSAR_MESSAGES_EN` (22 keys) and `SAVED_ROWS_MESSAGES_EN` (8 keys) and render through `vireo()` of
  their core literals (`PULSAR_REVIEW_LITERALS`, `PULSAR_REVIEW_EDIT_LITERALS`, `PULSAR_REVIEW_EDIT_DFM`, `SAVED_FIND_LITERALS`).
  The three unit tests that pinned the map text to the literals now pin the literals themselves. Also through `vireo()`:
  the "No Current Data to Analyze" and "Recorded Pulsar Data Not Saved!" boxes, VIREO's Save texts and questions
  (`PULSAR_NATIVE_SAVE_TEXT`, the Clear questions included), the Print Window menu item and its preview caption, the printed
  header, and the properties' "Data Not Written to File". 61 new originals on ES8b's base (552 in all); after merging main, 58 beyond
  ES8a's (659 in all). The 13 VIREO MessageBox button keys left the maps in the review (see below).
- **VIREO's MessageBox buttons** (Review's Yes/No/OK, the Clear and Load questions' No/Cancel and Yes/No/Cancel, VIREO's Save
  boxes and file-name form, the not-found OK) render `vireo(OBSERVATORY_VIREO_CAPTIONS.ok|yes|no|cancel)`, not map keys; the
  web-reworded buttons ("Yes, clear", "Yes, save first", "Continue without saving") stay web text.
- **The saved-measurement viewer's file mirrors** (its metadata lines, column header, column names, cells and the selected row's
  raw text) carry `lang="en"`, with a `NativeMirrorCaption` under the raw row (F2).
- **Texts with a file name inside** (the Save "already exists" box and the "Writing/Appending Data to" box): the core builders take
  an optional `tr` and translate their fixed pieces as `fragment|…` originals around the name, which stays as typed. The
  printed header takes an optional `tr` too (`pulsarPrintPage(…, vireo)`), so prints follow the screen language; its date is the
  browser's, as before.
- **Receipts:** the 3 files' `vireo-literals.json` entries are now identifiers (plot geometry, step ids, limits, download names),
  native text mirrored in a file (the saved-measurement viewer's header columns) or, for `NS.existsText` / `NS.verifyText`, the
  new category "translated in pieces" (`src/i18n/vireoAudit.test.ts` accepts it). ES8b adds four `vireo()` sites the guard cannot
  resolve statically (the `piece` translator, a conditional and an indexed DFM caption, and VIREO's Save box buttons
  `vireo(OV[a])`), all covered by `tests/i18n/es-e06.spec.ts`: `UNRESOLVED_SITES` is 137 after merging main (133 with ES8a).
- **Glossary:** names Pulsar guide and Pulsar timing guide, the term dispersion measure; the `tab` pattern also accepts «tabulación»
  after "for example a" and "accented letter, a" (a tab character, not a browser tab). Terms settled by the ES6 review
  apply (Escribe, lectura completada, valor esperado, Datos a partir de observaciones vs Datos de observación). Press is
  «presiona» (the lint forbids «pulsa»). Table Number is «Número de mesa de laboratorio».
- **One term per concept (ES8b review, aligned with ES8a):** a recording is «registro» («registro del receptor» for a receiver
  recording, «captura» only where the English says capture) and recorded signal data is «registrado(s)», as on the radio page;
  the Record button is «Registrar» and its measurements «registrada(s)»; the current pulsar data is «los datos de púlsar
  actuales», which a receiver recording «ocupa»; delay is «retardo»; Record armed is «armado»; Off is «Apagado»; Review's Delete
  is «Eliminar» / «eliminación» and Clear is «Borrar»; read back is «volver a leer» / «leído de nuevo»; a measurement number is
  «#{seq}»; VIREO's "Sec" is «Seg»; the Shift key keeps its name; the interval unit in Properties is «(ms)». A catalog that is "the
  recorded original" is «el original registrado» (documented with its hash). `pulsar.props.*` reads like ES8a's
  `radioReceiver.record.properties.*`.
- **Names on the radio page (ES8a's):** the guide names the radio controls the student must find as ES8a's merged Spanish shows them
  (checked after merging main), with the English after the first mention: Parámetros de púlsares (Pulsar Parameters),
  Buscar púlsar… (Find Pulsar), Mover (Slew) ▸ Fijar coordenadas… (Set Coordinates), Agregar canal (Add Channel),
  Frec. MHz (Freq MHz), Registrar (Record), Modo (Mode), Identificar el objeto registrado (Identify Recorded Object), Seguimiento (Tracking), the indicator «Apagado»,
  the card «Escenario del reloj de radio» and the notice «Se necesita un UT de radio». The three originals both fronts added ("Ok",
  "Receiver", "Data Not Written to File") are kept once, with the same Spanish.
- **Recorded data stays English:** the pulsar name in the recording, the file names, the Review table's cells, the saved
  measurement file's header line and rows, and the printed header's values. Files written by this flow are unchanged in every
  language (see Tests).
- **Not ES8b's:** the radio telescope route, `RadioPulsarParameters`, `RadioR1Messages` (the Find note under Review, `R1.findNote`,
  ES8a's Spanish), the guide's `pulsarParameters.reference.sha` label ("SHA-256", the same in Spanish; ES8a's key)
  and the help text behind the `?` button (ES-HELP).
- **Core builders with `tr`:** `PULSAR_NATIVE_SAVE_TEXT.existsText` / `verifyText` and `pulsarPrintLines` / `pulsarPrintPage` take an
  optional translator that defaults to the literal, so `src/core` stays free of i18n; unit tests pin that the file name and the
  values never pass through it.

**English-visible changes:** none in text. One layout fix: the data properties' receiver table wraps (`white-space:normal`
in `.plr-props-table`) instead of overflowing a 320 px screen (the "stored 0 replaced by 1" note made it 475 px wide in English).

**Tests**
- `tests/i18n/es-e06.spec.ts`: a 35-step walk in English, Spanish and qps (route without data, guide, saved-measurement viewer
  with candidate, Find, not found and refusal, a loaded .PLR with properties, print in both orientations, a measurement with the
  record dialog and its comment refusal, Review with Find, the comment editor, the delete question and "No Item Selected!",
  VIREO's Save with its name refusal, verify, exists and extension questions, export and append with refusals, the Clear
  questions). The texts allowed to read the same are an exact list (product names, units, data of the synthetic files, six
  words identical in Spanish), a SHA-256
  hash, numbers with units and "Panel {n}"; the identical words and symbols are allowed only in the Spanish comparison, and the
  qps walk exempts only recorded data. The saved-measurement viewer's file mirrors are `lang="en"` and skipped. Explicit VIREO caption asserts in Spanish, English
  and View original, and layout at 320 and 1440 px (run with the DejaVu fontconfig). A second test follows a receiver capture into the page (properties with
  "Data Not Written to File", the raw .PLR download dialog, the verified Save dialog, the Load question and its Yes flow).
  Not reached in a browser: the Load-after-Save chooser (it needs the browser's save picker) and VIREO's "No Current Data to
  Analyze" box (an empty capture); both are covered by `originals.json` and the originals checks.
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: one family in en, qps and es-419 (the original recording, VIREO's Save and its
  append, the export, the append file and the viewer's original download), fixed clock, nothing masked; the raw .PLR download
  of a capture and the investigation that holds it are ES8a's family.

## Spanish: exercise E08, Object X and cameras (ES9)

ES9 translates the E08 flow and the camera screens: the Object X guide; the `#object-x` lab (ID, object code, date and clock,
orbit file and asteroid list, the radio and spectrometer branches with kept spectra, the images, the measuring tool, the answer
and the reveal); the Object X card on the radio route (the route itself is ES8a's); the CCD and IR camera on the observatory
(its exposure, preparation, Information window, overlay and palette were complete since ES2); and the saved-images route
(`#images`, `FitsImageLab`).

- **Maps, complete:** `objectXLab` (ES1 had only its two menu names), `objectXGuide`, `objectXMeasure` and `objectXSession`
  (`src/i18n/es/maps/`). The verdict after the reveal puts the article that matches the kind (`una estrella`, `una galaxia`,
  `un cuásar (QSO)`, `un púlsar`, `un asteroide`).
- **Catalog:** the 44 `fits.*` keys (the 45th, `fits.nav`, was ES1's). `FitsImageLab.tsx` 2/33 → 33/33 keys, `fits` 1/45 →
  45/45; en.json 2201 → 2245 of 3585 keys (61.4 % → 62.6 %); maps 19 → 22 of 31 started. The remaining `OpenVireo.tsx` keys are the
  radio, pulsar and cluster fronts'.
- **VIREO captions:** the camera's Information button rendered VIREO's File › "&Information..." item (DFM `Information1` of TCCDCam
  and TIRCam) from the camera map's `information` key; that key left the map and the button renders `vireo()` of the new core
  literal `CAMERA_INFORMATION_MENU` (`src/core/cameraInformation.ts`), so View original applies. No new originals were needed
  (VIREO's "Information..." was translated in ES2). The camera's Minutes, Seconds and Stop labels are the web's own (TCCDCam has
  a Min/Sec switch and a "&Cancel" button) and stay in the map.
- **Receipts:** the four Object X receipts are `identifier` (the ID input length, the answer kind ids, the guide's step count and
  step ids). `UNRESOLVED_SITES` is unchanged (the new site resolves).
- **Glossary:** new `terms` blink › «parpadeo» («Iniciar el parpadeo», «Compara con el parpadeo y mide»), reveal › «revel…» (not in "Reveal the catalog sky", which means show), orbit file ›
  «archivo de órbitas», limiting magnitude › «magnitud límite», reference star › «estrella de referencia», plate constants ›
  «constantes de placa»; new `names` Asteroid List › «Lista de asteroides». The `counts` row skips Object X's "the count shows
  after the reveal", a number of asteroids («el número»), not detector counts (ES9 review). Other forms used throughout: object code › «código
  del objeto», near-Earth › «cercano a la Tierra», field box › «recuadro del campo», trail › «estela», low/high cut › «corte
  bajo/alto», kept spectrum › «espectro conservado», Classify (the route) › «Clasificación estelar», quasar › «cuásar», UT stays
  UT, "ID # of your unknown object" › «N.º de ID de tu objeto desconocido» (as VIREO's "ID # of Unknown Object" in ES2).
- **Hidden identity:** no Spanish string names or describes a target, and every string reads the same for every target (the
  neutral unavailable message, the hidden asteroid count «incluye los asteroides de la lista actual (el número se muestra…)»).
  `es-e08.spec.ts` checks, at every step before the reveal, in English, Spanish and qps:
  - that no ObjectX.HTL identifier appears in the Object X route, its guide, the camera, the observatory's sky view, the
    spectrometer and photometer ID readouts, or the radio route's Object X card and hot list (the remarks are English data, so in
    Spanish the remark check adds nothing; the observatory's target picker legitimately lists catalogue galaxies and is not read);
  - that the lab shows no verdict sentence («Es una…», «Tu respuesta es correcta», "It is a…", "Your answer is right");
  - and, in a separate test, that the lab's and the guide's texts and the sky view's toolbar are identical for a star target and an
    asteroid target once the object code and the coordinates line are removed. The verdict's article is chosen from the kind id
    (`verdict(right, kind, kindId)`; English ignores the id), and the spec checks all five sentences.
- **Kept as recorded (data, not translated):** the Object X codes (`X-NTFB6`…), the student's ObjX labels and file names
  (`ObjX-42-01.FIT`, `ObjX-42-S01`), the orbit file names (ObjX_MPC.CMO.sav, ObjX_MPC.CMO, ObjX_AST.CMO), the ObjectX.HTL identifier
  and remark shown after the reveal, the asteroid list's designations and the MPC/Lowell note codes, the recorded VizieR (NOMAD)
  rows and query, catalogue and telescope names, the FITS header cards (the saved-images header view is `lang="en"`), hashes and
  the synthetic file's name.
- **English-visible changes:** none in text. Two invisible ones: the saved-images FITS header `<pre>` has `lang="en"` (F2), and a
  camera button may wrap (`max-width:100%` on `.camera-control .button-row>button`): at 320 px «Guardar imagen (encabezado de
  VIREO)» was wider than the card; the English buttons fit and are unchanged.
- **Files:** `exports-identity.spec.ts` adds the E08 family, run in English, qps and Spanish: the kept spectrum CSV and `.SSP`, the
  camera's FITS and VIREO-header FITS, both Object X images (`ObjX-42-01.FIT`, `-02.FIT`), the saved-images download of the first
  (equal to it), and the investigation before and after the reveal. It replaces the ES0 Object X family (English and qps, with the
  counts and replay seeds masked). The page clock is installed at a fixed instant and paused before every step that records a time;
  it runs only while the spectrometer data loads and the asteroid list is computed, then is paused again at a later fixed instant.
  After the second image the observatory clock is paused (its committed elapsed time is saved) and the page clock runs again,
  because Save investigation recomputes the asteroid lists in slices that yield with `setTimeout(0)`; nothing after that records a
  time.
  `crypto.getRandomValues` (Uint32Array only) is replaced by a fixed xorshift stream in the page, so the camera seed and the spectrum
  seed are fixed. One value is masked: the observatory clock's committed time in the two investigations
  (`clockDrive.elapsedMS`), because the clock runs in 200 ms steps during each exposure, as many as the camera worker's real
  latency needs; the images' JD and UT are those of Start and do not depend on it. A FITS file is compared whole, as its header
  text plus the SHA-256 of its data, so the Spanish-letter check reads only the header.
- **CCD camera family (the known flake):** it ran on the real clock, and the FITS file name embeds a time (…-120001-V.fit vs
  …-120000-V.fit), so it failed when a save crossed a second boundary. It now installs the clock, pauses it before Start
  observatory clock and advances it in 200 ms steps only until the image is ready (up to 90 real seconds for the worker and the
  routed catalogue answer), with the same fixed random stream; the header
  masks (`DATE-OBS`, `MJD-OBS`, `RA`, `DEC`, `CRVAL1`, `CRVAL2`, `CAMSEED`) are gone and the whole files are compared. Both families
  were proven with `--repeat-each=10 --workers=4`.
- **Checks:** `tests/i18n/es-e08.spec.ts` walks the flow in English, Spanish and qps (25 steps, the recorded VizieR response, no
  live network). Spanish may repeat an English text only when it is an exact identifier or matches a narrow pattern (Object X codes,
  ObjX labels and file names, image and kept-spectrum rows, the cursor readout, the observatory's galaxy options, hashes, numbers
  with units); qps must mark every other text, `<option>` texts, accessible names and SVG titles included. The VIREO captions of the
  walk (the camera's Information... item, the Information window's caption, Print... and Close in both camera windows, the
  spectrometer branch's Spectral Range... and Help) are asserted explicitly in Spanish, in English, and in English under View
  original (English), where the web text around them stays Spanish. Layout at 320 px and 1440 px with the DejaVu fontconfig.
- **The radio route (ES8a):** the Object X card and the strings that point to the radio page use ES8a's names: «Iniciar ejercicio
  de púlsares», «Fijar coordenadas (Set Coordinates)», «Receptor (Receiver)», «Radiotelescopio».
- **Not translated here:** the help files behind the spectrometer's Help button
  (ES-HELP).
- **Spanish chunk:** 409,001 → 429,980 B raw, 117,974 → 124,943 B gzip (level 9), 96,449 → 102,056 B brotli (`npm run build`;
  `check-i18n-dist.mjs` reports 125.3 KB), under the 150 KB warning. After merging main with ES6 and ES8a: 652,506 B raw,
  180,384 B gzip, 144,399 B brotli (`check-i18n-dist.mjs`: 180.7 KB), above the 150 KB warning, so the per-area split ES5
  recommended is now due.

## Script groundwork: test fonts (SC1)

- **Why.** Checks for Japanese, Chinese, Hindi and Russian depend on font metrics: Noto Sans CJK's normal line is 1.448 em, and Devanagari ink reaches about 1.9 em. So they run under pinned fonts in their own Playwright config, `playwright.scripts.config.ts` (`npm run test:scripts`; specs `tests/scripts/*.scripts.ts`; loopback port `OPENVIREO_SCRIPTS_PORT`, 6400–6498, default 6420). The main suite keeps its own fonts and metrics.
- **The fonts.** `scripts/test-fonts.mjs` writes `~/.cache/openvireo-test-fonts/scripts/fonts.conf`. Its only folder holds symlinks to DejaVu Sans, Serif and Sans Mono, Noto Sans CJK, and Noto Sans Devanagari, Regular and Bold only (what Ubuntu 24.04 ships).
  - Generic families (including `system-ui` and fontconfig 2.17+'s `sans`/`mono`) resolve to DejaVu.
  - A text's language adds its fallback face: JP for `ja`, SC for `zh-Hans`/`zh-CN`, TC for `zh-Hant`/`zh-TW`/`zh-HK`, Devanagari for `hi`. Russian stays in DejaVu.
  - The script checks the config with `fc-list` and `fc-match -s`. It fails if a profile family is missing, if any other family is listed, or if the folder holds anything but its own symlinks.
  - It prints each file's SHA-256, and keeps fontconfig's cache in a folder named after those hashes, so a font package update cannot leave stale metadata behind.
  - If a font is missing it fails with a coded message and the install command: Ubuntu `fonts-dejavu-core fonts-dejavu-mono fonts-noto-cjk fonts-noto-core`, Arch `ttf-dejavu noto-fonts-cjk noto-fonts`. It never installs anything.
  - `npm run test-fonts -- --profile=dejavu` writes the DejaVu-only fontconfig that the Spanish layout checks above refer to, at `~/.cache/openvireo-test-fonts/dejavu/fonts.conf`; use it with `FONTCONFIG_FILE=…`.
- **Same faces, not the same bytes.** Arch and CI resolve the same faces by name, but only the two Noto Sans CJK collections are byte-identical. On Ubuntu 24.04 the six DejaVu files hash differently, and Noto Sans Devanagari is another version (229,336 and 237,660 bytes there; 243,520 and 250,176 on Arch). SC3 must take the hash list printed by the CI job `scripts` as its reference before setting 1 px tolerances, and re-check any tolerance measured locally against a CI run.
- **What is checked.** `tests/scripts/fonts.scripts.ts` asks Chromium which platform font drew each glyph (DevTools `CSS.getPlatformFontsForNode`), for Japanese, Simplified and Traditional Chinese, Hindi and Russian under the site's sans and mono stacks and the generic families.
  - Latin, Cyrillic, digits and general punctuation must come from DejaVu and everything else from the right regional face: no tofu, and no IPA Gothic, WenQuanYi or Unifont.
  - The zh-Hans “ ” therefore come from DejaVu because the stacks put Latin fonts first; SC3 may choose full-width quotes instead.
  - A named family must reach the Bold files.
  - `npm run test:test-fonts` covers the generator.
- **Chromium behaviour pinned by characterization tests** (Chromium of `@playwright/test` 1.64.1, Linux, test fonts; measured 2026-11-09, stable over repeated runs). These tests pin today's browser so that an update that changes it shows up. Each is a reason SC3's `:lang()` stacks must name the regional family:
  - (a) Under `font-family:sans-serif`, Chromium remembers the fallback face per character and ignores `lang`. A later paragraph of another Han region that shares a character with an earlier one gets the earlier region's face for its whole run:
    - zh-Hans then zh-Hant: both SC.
    - zh-Hant then zh-Hans: both TC.
    - ja then zh-Hant: both JP.
    - Paragraphs that share no character keep their own faces.
    - The memory survives `page.setContent` in the same page; a new page starts fresh.
  - (b) Plain `zh` and `ko` fall back to the JP face.
  - (c) Through generic per-character fallback, weights 550, 600 and 700 all get the Regular CJK and Devanagari files, with `font-synthesis` none or auto, while Latin reaches DejaVuSans-Bold. Only a named family reaches the Bold files: 550 with "Noto Sans CJK SC" named gives NotoSansCJKsc-Bold. The site's buttons are 550.
- **CI.** The job `scripts` in `.github/workflows/ci-cd.yml` runs on `ubuntu-24.04` (pinned, so the font packages do not follow `ubuntu-latest`). It installs the fonts from the Ubuntu archive, the same source Playwright's `--with-deps` uses, so this is no new download source, then runs both checks. `deploy` needs it (plan §5). No pixel screenshots: the checks are geometric or ask the browser which font it used.

## Dev-only script-stress pseudo-locales (qps-cjk, qps-deva, qps-cyrl)

Front SC2 (plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-scripts.md` §4). Open `?lang=qps-cjk`, `?lang=qps-deva` or `?lang=qps-cyrl`
in the dev server. Like qps, every catalog, map and `vireo()` string is wrapped as `⟦…⟧` and map functions have their result
marked only; `{params}`, `<tag>`-like placeholders, digits, the decimal point, line breaks, leading or trailing spacing and
non-ASCII letters (Å, Δ) are kept. Unlike qps, the English letters are replaced by another script, and `<html lang>` is that
script's real tag, so the `:lang()` rules of the script groundwork apply.

| Id | `<html lang>` | Transform | Measured over catalog, map leaves and VIREO originals (Chromium) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `qps-cjk` | `ja` | each word of n letters → ⌈0.6 n⌉ full-width kanji/kana (small kana and ー only inside a word; some Chinese-only Han); spaces next to them removed; `. , : ? ! ( )` → `。、：？！（）` | 1.24× (Noto stack) to 1.31× (app stack) the English width |
| `qps-deva` | `hi` | each word → ⌈0.8 n⌉ syllables with vowel signs above and below; about one word in three carries a conjunct that stacks in Noto Sans Devanagari (र्ड्ढूँ, ट्ठू, ड्डू, ट्टूँ, ड्ढुं, ष्ट्रैं, ह्मूँ, र्द्धि, क़्र्यूँ); `.` → `।` | 1.17×–1.23× width; ink at 100 px: mean 0.90 em above and 0.37 em below the baseline (English 0.77 and 0.20), max 0.90 above, 0.40 below, 1.30 em in all (English 1.12) |
| `qps-cyrl` | `ru` | transliteration, each word about 25 % longer; about one word of eight letters or more in six becomes one unbreakable word of min(24, 1.7 n) letters | 1.24× the characters, 1.44×–1.46× the width (real Russian is about 1.3–1.45×) |

- The Devanagari stress is mostly **below** the line (vowel signs ु ू ृ, nukta, stacked conjuncts). Above the line it adds
  little over Latin, and the total ink does not come near the 1.9 em of Noto Sans Devanagari's font-wide (win) metrics given
  in the plan. Real Hindi can still need more room than `qps-deva` shows.
- The transforms (`src/i18n/pseudoScripts.ts`) are pure, deterministic, idempotent and word-stable. Specs import
  `PSEUDO_SCRIPT[id]` to compute expected text, as the Spanish specs use `L()`. `pseudo.ts` and `pseudoScripts.ts` import
  `en.json` with a JSON attribute so Playwright can load them.
- They are accepted only from the `?lang=` link parameter in the dev server and tests, never from storage or the browser
  language, and show no language selector. `src/i18n/locale.ts` gates them with a constant-folded `import.meta.env.DEV`
  check (guarded by `typeof`, because Playwright specs load app modules outside Vite). `src/boot.ts` loads the bundle only
  in its `import.meta.env.DEV` branch. The production build is byte-identical without them, and `scripts/check-i18n-dist.mjs`
  still fails on `⟦` anywhere in `dist/`. A dist check for the `qps-*` names arrives with SC8.
- Numbers keep the `en-US` locale and files the site writes stay English (`pseudoScripts.test.ts` checks the number vector
  and three export hashes; the exports-identity flows join in SC10).
- Checks: `src/i18n/pseudoScripts.test.ts` (properties over the catalog strings, the English map leaves and the VIREO
  originals; ratio bands; locale decision; settle; maps; `vireo()`) and `tests/scripts-pseudo.smoke.spec.ts` (each variant
  renders the home page with its `<html lang>`, title and script, with no page or console error). The 320/1440 px route walk
  under these variants is SC3.

**Known gaps.**
- **Function-valued map entries stay Latin.** 91 of them (counted at SC2) are only marked, under all three variants:
  objectXLab 25, camera 11, clusterHr 9, wedge 9, observatory 8, and others. Their result may carry data, so they are marked
  and not transformed, as in qps.
- **Machine-looking text on screen is transformed too**: a timestamp such as `2000-01-01T12:00:00`, file names such as
  `Hubble.HTL`, key names. Download names and `accept` lists are constants and stay unchanged.
- **`qps-*` ids are not valid BCP 47 tags.** `new Intl.*('qps-cjk')` throws `RangeError`, so no `Intl` constructor may be given
  a `qps-*` id. Pass the `HTML_LANG` tag or English rules instead; SC5's plural helper maps them to English rules.
- The CJK variant is a width stress, not realistic Japanese: `;` and quotes stay ASCII.

## Script groundwork: fonts, line breaking and the route walk (SC3)

- **The sheet.** `src/theme/scripts.css`, imported once by `src/main.tsx` after `base.css`. Every selector is scoped by
  `:lang()` (ja; zh with zh-Hans/CN/SG as Simplified and zh-Hant/TW/HK/MO as Traditional, which wins by order; hi; ru), so
  English and Spanish do not change. It sets the regional script fonts after the Latin ones and before `system-ui`
  (`--ov-font-script`, `--ov-font-script-mono`, and the two token stacks), repeats each hard-coded English `font-family` list
  with that fallback, lets weight be synthesised for ja/zh/hi, turns letter-spacing and upper case off there,
  `line-break:strict` for ja, `text-autospace` for ja/zh, `overflow-wrap:break-word` for the four languages, `hyphens:auto` on
  Russian prose (no effect in Playwright's Chromium, which has no hyphenation dictionary: nothing depends on it), the line-height
  floors (CJK 1.5; Hindi 1.7 on text, 1.5 on controls), `min-height` for the two fixed text heights, and wrapping for the phone
  header and the theme switch. System fonts only. `src/theme/scripts.test.ts` fails when an English `font-family` or a
  line-height below the floors appears or changes without its line in the sheet.
- **Popup menus.** `src/polish/PopupMenu.tsx` measures the rendered menu before paint and moves it back inside the viewport
  (4 px from the edge) only when it leaves it; a menu taller than the viewport is capped to it and scrolls. A menu that fits
  keeps the old placement. One English change, a fix: the hot list's menu carries a note that the old 40 px-per-item guess
  ignored, so opened near the bottom of the viewport it reached past it; it now ends 4 px inside.
- **The walk.** `tests/scripts/routes.scripts.ts` (config of SC1) opens, under `qps-cjk`, `qps-deva` and `qps-cyrl` at 320 and
  1440 px, the home page, the 16 routes, the header menu, the options dialog, the hot list's popup menu, both help viewers and
  About, and asserts the `⟦` marker, `layoutProblems()` and `verticalInkProblems()` of `tests/fixtures/layoutProblems.ts` (the
  Spanish specs' copy-pasted check, extracted for SC9, plus a check of glyph ink cut by clipping boxes, itself tested on
  controlled boxes). It also checks the computed stacks and faces per language, nested autonyms, Han unification, the popup
  placement in English, and that English and Spanish compute the same value of every property the sheet declares with and
  without it, on every route.
- **Known problems for SC11** (listed in `KNOWN` in the spec; an entry that stops appearing fails the walk): the rotated y-axis
  titles of `PleiadesAnalysis.tsx` and `NativeClusterAnalysis.tsx` cross the plot's left edge by 1–2 px under `qps-cjk`, and the
  spectrum x-axis title of `SpectrumInstrument.tsx` and `ClassificationLab.tsx` reaches below the plot under `qps-deva`
  (font-dependent: tolerated, not required). With the tolerances measured on Arch, the CI job `scripts` on Ubuntu 24.04 found
  the two required entries and no other problem (run 42733470800, 2026-11-10); it takes about 5 minutes, beside the e2e shards.

## Script groundwork: availability in China (SC6)

**What was measured.** Nothing has been measured from inside mainland China yet. On 2026-11-09 the live page
(https://openvireo.space) was read from outside China. Its script tags, in order, are:
1. an inline script;
2. the ld+json block;
3. the app's `<script type="module" crossorigin src="/assets/index-….js">`;
4. Cloudflare's Web Analytics tag, `<script defer src='https://static.cloudflareinsights.com/beacon.min.js'
   data-cf-beacon='{"token": "REDACTED"}'>`.

So the beacon is deferred and comes after the app's module script [M]. A slow or blocked beacon therefore cannot hold back
the app. While it is pending it does hold DOMContentLoaded and the load event, so the browser's loading indicator may keep
turning after the page is already usable.

**Honest assessment (plan §11).**
- openvireo.space is a custom domain on Cloudflare's global network. It is not blocked by design, but mainland visitors are
  served from edges outside the mainland: slower and less predictable, especially at school hours [I].
- The entry is about 1.0 MB gzip and the GSC sky tiles are 1–2 MB each. On a lossy international link a first load may take
  tens of seconds [I].
- No source confirms or denies blocking of `static.cloudflareinsights.com` or `cloudflareinsights.com` [U]. If they are
  blocked, only the visit counts from China are lost.
- The two live VizieR hosts (`vizier.idia.ac.za`, `vizier.cds.unistra.fr`) are untested from China [U]. The Beijing mirror
  (`vizier.china-vo.org`) timed out over HTTPS from outside China and lacks NOMAD (I/297). The exercise fields need none of
  them, because they use extracts bundled with the site.
- The user decided on 2026-11-09 that "works, slowly" is acceptable for the first Chinese release.

**The fail-soft contract (`tests/china-fail-soft.spec.ts`).**
- The spec serves the page with the same deferred beacon tag, placed after the app's module script as on the live page. A
  catch-all route aborts every other request that leaves the local origin.
- Each scenario has the beacon script, its report host (`cloudflareinsights.com/cdn-cgi/rum`) and/or the VizieR mirrors
  refused or hanging.
- The home page and the stellar classification exercise render with no uncaught error while the beacon is still pending. The
  browser's DOMContentLoaded is still held at that point; the test pins this as browser behaviour, not anything about
  Cloudflare's injection.
- A bundled camera field makes an image without contacting any of those hosts.
- The live camera query ends with its typed message:
  - refused mirrors: in under 10 s, after trying IDIA and then CDS once each;
  - hanging mirrors: after 30 s per mirror, with "No VizieR mirror answered in time.", while Stop stays available and the
    page stays usable.

**Checklist.** `docs/CHINA-CHECKLIST.md` is for the user's testers in mainland China. It is written in Simplified Chinese,
addressing the tester as 你, with English after each step. The tester turns off any VPN, proxy or "accelerator" app, spends
15–20 minutes and installs nothing. It covers:
- the first screen and whether the loading indicator keeps turning;
- the stellar classification exercise;
- the sky map (GSC tiles);
- the camera with a bundled extract and with the live VizieR query;
- optional WeChat and developer-tools checks;
- a plain-text result form.

Testers send results through the chat they already use; the user pastes them to the coordinator. The site adds no data
collection of any kind. The English preface tells the owner what each outcome would mean.

**Still needs the user's approval.**
- Any faster route for China: Cloudflare's China Network (Enterprise, with JD Cloud), an ICP filing (needs a mainland entity
  or host), or a mainland host or CDN. These would only be raised if the testers' results show the site blocked or too slow;
  none is proposed now.
- Adding the Beijing VizieR mirror (SC14) is covered by the 2026-10-29 mirror approval, but waits for evidence that it serves
  the catalogs over HTTPS.
- Loading the analytics beacon some other way, so a pending beacon no longer holds the load event, would be a deploy decision.

## Script groundwork: per-locale lints, plurals and IME (SC4, SC5)

- **Lint framework** (`src/i18n/lint/`). `rules.ts` holds pure rules that take a pair (`catalog:<key>`, `map:<id>.<path>`,
  `original:<key>` with its English) and a `LocaleProfile`, and return typed findings. Errors fail a batch; reports (the
  length ratio) go to the reviewer. Rules use Unicode classes only, never `\b` or `\w`, and a test enforces it.
  - **Rules:**
    - placeholders, as a multiset whose order may change;
    - no HTML;
    - identical-to-English, or no character of the script, only with a reason in `identical.json`;
    - left-over English: three words in a row, each with a lower-case Latin letter, outside brackets and quotes (acronyms and
      format tokens like `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS` pass);
    - glossary terms, names and forbidden words;
    - register;
    - claims to be CLEA's or official (denials pass);
    - glyph classes (`...`/`…` and, in ja/zh, `……` are one ellipsis);
    - mark pairs (a VIREO message quoted in English inside “…” keeps its own marks);
    - quote style and typography;
    - Western digits and the decimal point;
    - NFC, invisible and bidi controls;
    - script purity;
    - length band, in grapheme clusters.
  - `lint-allowed.json` entries may name `words`, `placeholders` the translation may add, or whole `rules`, always with a
    reason. Allowances that are no longer needed are flagged.
  - `pairs.ts` collects pairs from a catalog, maps and originals.
  - `pluralForms` requires each key marked plural to have the language's CLDR categories (optional ones may fall back to
    `other`) and none it never selects.
  - Structural checks (`createI18n`, `mapParity`, originals keys) stay as they are.
- **Profiles** (`profiles.ts`) for ja, hi, zh-Hans, zh-Hant and ru. The register is decided (user, 2026-11-09): Japanese
  です/ます, Hindi आप, Russian lower-case «вы», Chinese 你.
  - `REGISTER` holds these values. The alternatives stay expressible, and the fixtures prove each register pattern both ways.
  - Length bands are first proposals that each language's XX0 calibrates: ja/zh 0.3–1.0, hi 0.45–1.2, ru 0.8–1.8.
  - The Simplified/Traditional table in `hanVariants.ts` is a hand-curated seed. Generation from Unihan is not approved as a
    download.
  - `text-autospace` for `:lang(ja)` and `:lang(zh)` belongs to SC3's CSS; the strings carry no manual CJK–Latin spaces
    either way.
- **Mutation fixtures** (`fixtures.ts`): a good and a bad string per rule and language, plus a flipped register per language.
  The good one passes every rule; the bad one trips its rule. They test the rules, not the translations.
- **Plurals** (`src/i18n/plural.ts`): `plural(language, n, forms)` over `Intl.PluralRules`, with the rules cached per language
  and every option. Russian has one/few/many/other, ja and zh only other, and hi puts 0 and 1 in one. The dev pseudo-locales
  use English rules, because `qps-cjk` is not a valid tag for `Intl`.
- **IME** (`src/i18n/imeAudit.test.ts`): every Enter handler with what it is bound to and whether it is guarded.
  `ime-enter-sites.json` pins them, and the offenders (unguarded on a text field, on a container holding one, or testing for
  an input target) shrink as SC11 adds guards.
- **Refreshing pins:** lines are `file › enclosing places · …`, sorted, with no line numbers. After a merge, run
  `OPENVIREO_REFRESH_PINS=1 npx vitest run src/i18n/plural.test.ts src/i18n/imeAudit.test.ts` and review the diff. The
  refresh is refused when `CI` is set, and a missing list is an error.
- **For SC9 (Spanish migration):**
  - Migrate from main's current `es.test.ts`, not from an older copy.
  - Use the hooks:
    - `forbiddenSetAside` for `VIREO_PULSAR_NAMES`;
    - the quoted-English exemption, already in `markPairs`;
    - mark pairs ¿? and ¡! with `closeAfter`/`closeBefore`;
    - a `placeholders` allowance for `fragment|Meter Radio Telescope` (`{diameter}`);
    - optional `many` from `OPTIONAL_PLURAL_CATEGORIES.es`.
  - Lengths are now counted in grapheme clusters rather than UTF-16 units.
  - Placeholders are stricter: the full multiset of `{param}` and «An», where `es.test.ts` only checks that «An» are present.
    Expect a few new findings to settle.
  - `collectPairs` gives an original's English without its `context|` prefix.
- **For SC11 (fixes in files the Spanish fronts edit):**
  - en.json's `.many` keys mean "not one" and become CLDR `other`, never `many`.
  - `obsHr.kindWarning` counts two things (counts and others), so it needs forms for each.
  - `siteInformationLines` and `requestDeniedText` choose between VIREO's literal texts (hour/hours, Minute./Minutes.). The
    hour count is shown with one decimal, so its category needs `minimumFractionDigits:1`.
  - IME guards must be `e.nativeEvent.isComposing||e.keyCode===229`: Safari fires the confirming Enter after compositionend
    with isComposing false and keyCode 229. The existing isComposing-only guards need the second test too.

## Spanish: help files (ES-HELP)

VIREO's 38 bundled help files (the 35 approved on 2026-10-29, HRAnalHlp and VarTypeHlp: `*HLP.LST`/`*HLP.TXT`, PULSRANL.TXT,
PULSRLST.TXT, SPECLNS.TXT, SPECLNS.LNL, Reminder.obs, REMINDER.PLS) are translated into es-419 as web adaptations by OpenVireo.
They are never CLEA's text: the originals under `public/original`, their bytes, SHA-256 and provenance are unchanged.

**Format and storage.** Each translation is authored in VIREO's own file format, so `core/cleaHelp.ts` (and `spectralLines.ts`,
`mainHelp.ts`) parse it with the same code as the original:
- LST rows `'Título' 'CamHlp.txt'` with the same row count, order, kind and file token (an original defect such as the
  unterminated `'Classify Spectra' 'begin` is reproduced; HUBHLP.LST and LSSHLP.LST keep their blank lines and stay refused;
  MLTSPHLP.LST and SPLSTHLP.LST stay empty); a displayed title fits VIREO's 65 characters;
- TXT sections `**Título**` whose header is the Spanish row title, "Temas relacionados:" lines naming Spanish titles, and every
  line at 72 characters or fewer (the viewer cannot wrap an unbroken longer line);
- SPECLNS.LNL keeps VIREO's line names (`Ca II (K line)`, `CH & Metals (G Band)`), as ES5 decided for the classification
  page's line table (data kept in English; review S2), so the translated LNL equals the original and the `*3933.68   Ca II (K
  line)*` keys of SPECLNS.TXT stay VIREO's; only the explanatory lines are Spanish. The reminders are plain text.
- VIREO menu and dialog names in the text are the ones in `originals.json`, with VIREO's English in parentheses at their first
  mention in each topic.

`src/i18n/es/help/<NAME>.ts` (for example `CAMHLP.LST.ts`) exports `{sourceName, sourceSha256, text}`: `sourceSha256` is the hash
of the original it was translated from. They are not under `public/`, so `check-resources` and the manifest are untouched. Text is
Latin-1 and LF; the loader (`src/help/helpTranslation.ts`) refuses a module whose recorded source is not the bundled original and
feeds the text, as Latin-1 bytes, to the original parsers. Each module is its own chunk (`import.meta.glob`), fetched only when the
viewer opens that file (a topic list plus its text, 2 to 8 KB gzip each); none is in the main Spanish chunk.

**The viewer** (`clusters/CleaHelp.tsx`, shared by every window's Help, and the Exit Observatory reminder and the Spectral Line
Table):
- a Spanish load opens the translation, labelled "Traducción: adaptación web de OpenVireo (no es de CLEA) · Ver original
  (inglés)"; "Ver original (inglés)" loads VIREO's exact bundled file (verified by size and SHA-256 as before) and the same row
  stays open, since both indexes have the same rows. The choice is per viewer and not stored. English loads show neither label
  nor switch. In the original view the topic list and text carry `lang="en"` (`HelpListBox`'s `lang`, the same prop ES7 adds),
  and the Exit Observatory reminder shows VIREO's exact box text, its English Select line included; the viewer's chrome
  (captions, menus, message boxes) stays in the page's language.
- A translation that cannot be loaded, verified (`helpTranslationBytes`: source name, SHA-256, Latin-1) or parsed falls back to
  VIREO's original with a note saying so (`HELP_VIEWER.translationError`), in the topic list, a topic, the Spectral Line Table and
  the reminder (review S1); a failed switch back to the original reports the file error as before.
- "Related Topics" is found by the language's marker (`RELATED_MARKER`: `TEMAS RELACIONADOS` in the translation).
- Search: in the translation `foldLatin1Accents` folds Latin-1 case and ignores accents and the diaeresis ("orbita" finds
  "Órbita"; ñ stays its own letter), also for Find Topic and the related-topic links, and says so in the search dialog as a web
  adaptation. The original view keeps VIREO's rule (`foldAsciiCase`, A to Z only).
- VIREO's own viewer literals go through `vireo()`: `Help - `, `Select a Topic Under`, `" Not Found.`, `Spectral Line Help`,
  the reminder caption, its Select line (translation view only) and the quit question (new `originals.json` entries).
- The Spectral Line Table's missing-file box is VIREO's (0x5049f0: `SPECTRAL_LINE_MISSING_LITERALS`, through `vireo()`; "File
  Not Found!" is «¡Archivo no encontrado!», as ES7's). Its web text moved to the map `helpViewer` (`HelpViewerMessages.ts`, `maps/helpViewer.ts`), with the same
  switch; its rows and help come from the translated SPECLNS pair.
- OpenVireo's own help (`webHelp.ts`, Help › Temas de ayuda…): only the text is translated (map `webHelp`, `maps/webHelp.ts`);
  topic ids, block kinds and related links stay in code, so a translation cannot break a link.
- The viewer's other chrome is ES7's: VIREO's captions (`CLUSTER_HR_CAPTIONS`: Topic, Back, the topic-list menus, "Select Help
  Topic", Topic Not Found, Find, not-found boxes) through `vireo()`, and the map `clusterHr` for its web notes. In the translation
  view the two web notes are this front's (`HELP_VIEWER.helpAdaptationTranslation`, `linkNoteTranslation`: «Temas relacionados»);
  the Spectral Line Table's Topic Not Found box uses the same captions; Print Topic translates only the lab-table suffix
  (`helpPrintPage`'s `tr`), and its title prefix stays VIREO's English.

**Tests.** `src/help/helpTranslation.test.ts`: the recorded hash equals `HELP_FILES[name].sha256`; Latin-1; row, kind and
file-token parity per LST; each topic opens the section at the same index as in English and its related topics resolve to the
same rows; two empty LSTs; HUBHLP/LSSHLP refused in both languages; line widths; SPECLNS pair; reminders; the search fold; the
loader's refusals; and the Spanish lints of es.test.ts (glossary `forbidden`, usted, voseo) over every help text, with a
four-entry allow-list in the test (VIREO's English names in parentheses, «cursor móvil», «cuando se abra»; review S5).
`tests/i18n/es-help.spec.ts` (Playwright): the help of the telescope, camera, cluster HR, photometer, spectrometer,
classification, spectrum measuring, wedge and pulsar analysis windows and the radio Help menu opens in Spanish, and "Ver original"
shows the bundled file's exact rows (with `lang="en"`); the Exit Observatory reminder in both views; accent-insensitive search;
the Spectral Line Table; OpenVireo's own help; and layout at 320 and
1440 px with a DejaVu-only fontconfig (`~/.cache/openvireo-eshelp-fonts`: symlinks to `/usr/share/fonts/TTF/DejaVu{Sans,Serif}*`,
no DejaVuMath; `FONTCONFIG_FILE=…/fonts.conf`; `fc-match sans-serif` gives DejaVu Sans).

**Build check.** `check-i18n-dist.mjs` recognises the 38 help chunks by the original's SHA-256, requires all of them, requires that
`index.html` neither loads nor preloads one, and reports their total. Measured on `npm run build` (2026-11-09, after the ES6
merge): 38 chunks, 154.6 KB raw, 54.4 KB gzip in all (largest VIRMNHLP.TXT 23.0 KB raw / 7.1 KB gzip; PULSRHLP.TXT 21.4 / 6.9;
CLASSHLP.TXT 14.1 / 4.7). The main Spanish chunk (`es-*.js`, 472.8 KB raw, 135.9 KB gzip) holds no help text (no
`sourceSha256`, no «Temas relacionados», no help sentence): it grew only by the two small maps above (`webHelp` 5.9 KB gzip of
source, `helpViewer` 1.4 KB) and six originals. `tests/release/pages-smoke.pages.ts` checks under the release headers that the
viewer fetches only the chunks of the file it opens and that "Ver original" passes the policy.

Not driven in Playwright (their structure is covered by the unit tests): Variable Stars (VARTYPEHLP, needs the 31,147-record
catalog), Pulsar Parameters (PLSLSHLP, needs a loaded pulsar catalog), the other spectrometer routes (same SPECHLP as tested) and
the two empty indexes (their Help stays disabled, as in VIREO).

**Review.** The draft (Sonnet translation batches) was reviewed section by section against the English by six independent Opus
reviewers, one per group of coupled files (an LST, its TXT and any TXT its rows point into), who fixed it in place: meaning and
omission fixes, the ES6 terms, VIREO names with their English at first mention, «lectura de cielo», «control deslizante»,
«diagrama HR», «graficado automático», «Herramienta de análisis de radiopúlsares», Balmer identifiers kept as VIREO writes them
(`H I (H Epsilon)`), and lines rewrapped at 72 characters. In OpenVireo's own help map, the help-topic text now says that the
instrument Help is VIREO's help translated, with "Ver original (inglés)"; the glossary gains the key name "Tab and Shift+Tab" ›
«Tab y Mayús+Tab» (the keys, not browser tabs: the «pestaña» term no longer applies to it).

**Terms.** The coordinator's ES6 decisions apply: «Datos a partir de observaciones» (Data from Observations) is distinct from
«Datos de observación»; "Enter" a value is «Escribe»; «lectura completada»; «valor(es) esperado(s)». Interface names that no
earlier front had translated were aligned, read-only, with the wording of the fronts translating those windows in parallel (ES7
cluster HR, ES8a radio, ES8b pulsar analysis, ES9 camera) as of 2026-11-09, and rechecked against `main` after ES8a and ES9
merged (the radio names in PULSRHLP and PULSRLST match ES8a's originals), for example «Fijar fecha/hora de observación e ID...»,
«Parámetros de púlsares», «Densidad electrónica», «Analizar datos de púlsares», «Registrar mediciones actuales...», «Acercar» /
«Alejar», «Seleccionar datos de cúmulos»; rechecked against `main` after ES7 and ES8b merged. VIREO's radio menu caption
"Hot List" (with its quotes) is «Lista de objetos» in `originals.json`, as in the help titles. A later change to one of those
interface names must also change the help files that cite it (`src/i18n/es/help/PULSRHLP.TXT.ts`, `PULSRLST.TXT.ts`,
`PULSRANL.TXT.ts`, `HRANALHLP.TXT.ts`, `CAMHLP.TXT.ts`).

## Spanish: the chunk split by route area (ES-SPLIT)

The plan is `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-es-split.md` in the research root. Until this front the whole Spanish bundle
(`src/i18n/es/index.ts`) was one chunk that a Spanish load waited for before its first render. It is now split into a **core
module**, which boot waits for, and one **module per route area**, which the area's routes fetch together with their own
JavaScript. English is unchanged and fetches nothing new.

**Sources (unchanged).** `es.json`, `originals.json`, `maps/*.ts` and `es/index.ts` stay the single sources, edited add-only by
the translation fronts; `es/index.ts` is the manifest (map id → file and export) and the whole bundle that Vitest imports. No app
module imports it, the catalog or a map file (`split.test.ts` item 5); no generated file is committed. The split languages are
listed in `src/i18n/languages.json` (`[{"tag":"es-419","dir":"es","catalog":"es.json","budgetFactor":1}]`); a later language adds
one line and its own `src/i18n/<dir>/`.

**Areas come from the code.** A route opts into an area at its own lazy site: `lazyArea('classification',()=>import('./ClassificationLab'))`
(`src/components/lazyArea.ts`). A plain `lazy()` stays in the core, so a missed opt-in costs bytes and never shows English. The
eight areas are declared in `src/i18n/areas.ts` (`AREAS`) and have 16 roots: classification (ClassificationLab); cluster
(ClusterDatabaseLab, NativeClusterAnalysis, ClusterNativeObservationHr, and ClusterDataWindow for File › Cluster Data…);
photometer (Photometer); pulsar (PulsarAnalysis, PulsarSavedRows); radio (RadioTelescopeRoute); lss (LssSurvey, WedgeLab);
exposure (GalaxyExposure, StarExposure, SkyExposure, and FieldSpectrometer in `Spectrometer.tsx`); fits (FitsImageLab). Object X,
the variable stars, the language selector and the observatory windows stay plain `lazy()` (their text is read by the shell too,
or is tiny). There is no help area: ES-HELP's help files are already one chunk each.

**The planner** (`scripts/i18n-split.mjs`, `node scripts/i18n-split.mjs [--json] [--lang es-419]` prints the modules) parses
`src/**/*.ts(x)` with `rolldown/utils` and resolves relative imports as Vite does. Edges are static imports, value re-exports,
dynamic `import()` and plain `lazy()`; type-only imports and type positions are ignored. A file's contexts are `core` when
`src/main.tsx` reaches it without crossing a `lazyArea` boundary, otherwise the areas whose roots reach it. Then:
- **Keys.** The files of a key are those that name it literally (as `scripts/i18n-areas.mjs` finds them) plus those that hold
  any template or `+` head that matches it (`` `pulsar.export.row.${…}` ``, `'fits.error.'+…`), not only the longest (review:
  a shorter head in a core file can build the same key as a longer one in an area file; no key moves today). This is more
  conservative than the plan's "static first, then the longest head" order: a key named in an area file and built from a head
  in a core file goes to the core (5 keys, 0.4 KB). A key with no file, or named or built in the i18n runtime (`src/i18n/*.ts`, for
  example `fieldReasonText`'s head), is core.
- **Maps.** The files that import the picked export (`OBSERVATORY_MESSAGES`…), following re-exports; the defining
  `*Messages.ts` file when the pick is used there too, sits in a translator factory (`createPulsarTranslator`) or is not exported.
- **Originals.** All 748 stay in the core: `vireo()`, `vireoList()`, `vireoMenuItem()` and "View original (English)" are unchanged.
- **Module.** The core if any file is core (or none is reached); otherwise the one area, or a **shared module** named after the
  sorted set of areas (`cluster+photometer`) when files of several areas render it and no core file does. Each area module
  statically imports its shared modules, so Rolldown emits each once and no text is duplicated.

**The plugin** (`openVireoI18nSplit()` in the same file, registered in `vite.config.ts`) serves the plan as virtual modules:
`virtual:openvireo-i18n/loaders` (the only thing in the English entry: `{'es-419':()=>import('virtual:openvireo-i18n/es-419')}`,
used by `src/boot.ts`), `virtual:openvireo-i18n/es-419` (the core: its catalog keys inlined, its map files and `originals.json`
imported, `areas` loaders, `mapModules` and `complete`, and in the dev server and Vitest `keyModules`), `…/es-419/<area>` and
`…/es-419/shared/<a+b>`. Chunks come out as `i18n-es-419-<module>-<hash>.js` (`+` is written `_`). In the dev server a source
change that moves text between modules re-plans (scans are cached per file) and reloads the page. When it builds, the plugin
repeats the visibility check on Rolldown's own module graph (`getModuleInfo(...).importedIds` and `dynamicallyImportedIds`,
crossing only the planner's `lazyArea` boundaries) and fails the build on a mismatch, and checks that the entry's static graph
reaches no language module. `src/i18n/virtual.d.ts` types the virtual modules.

**Runtime.**
- `activateLocale` builds a split bundle always with the English fallback, since its catalog is never complete in one module.
  **ES-REL must keep `!!bundle.areas` there when it flips `SPANISH_RELEASED`** (it did).
- `loadArea(name)` (`src/i18n/areas.ts`) resolves at once without `areas` (English, the pseudo-locales, a failed boot), so English
  imports nothing. Otherwise it fetches the area once per activation, races `LANGUAGE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s) from its own start, adds
  the part with `extendLocale` (the holder is replaced: catalog rebuilt, maps merged, `areas` records `loaded`), or marks the area
  `failed`. It never rejects, and a part that arrives after the timeout is ignored. `lazyArea` runs it with the route's import in
  `Promise.all`, so both start together and a route JS failure still reaches `LazyBoundary`'s "site updated" notice.
- **Reads before an area arrives.** `t` (one null test in English) and `resolveMessages` call `notePendingText` when the active
  split language lacks a key or a map: text of a loaded or failed area is English without an error; text of an area still
  loading **throws in the dev server and Vitest** (`OpenVireo: text "<key>" was read before its area <m> loaded`, the tripwire,
  from `keyModules` and `mapModules`); in production it is answered in English, counted (`pendingTextRequests`) and logged once
  per area (keys: once per load, and only when `complete`, which ES-REL sets, since before it production cannot tell a pending key
  from an untranslated one).
- `lazyMessages` and `lazyTranslator` keep a built map or translator only when no read during the build was pending, so text read
  too early becomes Spanish once its area arrives. A read of `$$typeof` on a lazy map answers `undefined` without building it
  (review): React Refresh probes it on every capitalised module-level value as the module evaluates in the dev server
  (`RADIO_SAVE_TEXTS` in `RadioReceiverWindows.tsx`), which read the radio text before its area and was hidden by Refresh's own
  `try`. The dev tripwire logs an `Error` (with its stack) before it throws, so such a caught read is no longer silent.
- **An area that fails** (an error, a wrong part, or 10 s) leaves its route in English with one bilingual `role="status"` notice in
  the shell next to the boot notice (`AREA_FAILURE_NOTICE`, `data-testid="area-failure"`): «No se pudo cargar parte del texto en
  español; esa parte se muestra en inglés. Recarga para intentarlo de nuevo.» The rest of the site stays Spanish.
- After a deploy, `/assets/*` stays immutable; a tab opened earlier fails a lab it had not visited like any route chunk (the
  route error if its JavaScript is gone, English and the notice if only its area is). The core chunk names the area chunks, so
  any area's text change also changes the core's hash.

**Checks.**
- `src/i18n/split.test.ts`: the partition (every key, map and original in exactly one module, originals all core, no empty area);
  the union of the virtual modules equals `SPANISH` (messages, maps by identity, originals); visibility of every rendering file,
  and every file `i18n-areas.mjs` finds kept; the opt-ins (the 16 roots); no app import of the bundle's sources; the pinned list
  of run-time key sites `src/i18n/dynamic-key-sites.json` (`file › place · t(<argument>)` or `· head '<head>'`, with the file's
  contexts; `OPENVIREO_REFRESH_PINS=1 npx vitest run src/i18n/split.test.ts` rewrites it); `complete`.
- `src/i18n/areas.test.ts` (a synthetic split bundle): English and the pseudo-locales load nothing; a key or map of a loading area
  throws in Vitest and is Spanish after `extendLocale`; production answers English, counts, logs once, and lazy maps and
  translators turn Spanish; timeout, a failed chunk or a wrong part give `failed` with English and no retry; `vireo()` and View
  original are unchanged.
- `tests/i18n/es-areas.spec.ts` (dev server, tripwire on, console and page errors collected): English fetches no language module
  on any route; the Spanish home fetches only the core; each of the 11 area routes, File › Cluster Data… and the field
  spectrometer at the Coma – 1 pointing fetch their area and shared modules exactly once, starting before the route's own module
  has finished; an area's help viewer, the wedge print preview and a forced route error; an area chunk aborted, and one held past
  10 s on the page clock (English route, notice, Spanish shell, no route error, a late part ignored); View original on an area
  route. The existing Spanish specs (`es-e01`…`es-e08`, `es-help`, `es-observatory`, `es-radio`, `exports-identity`) are the
  deep walk. Every spec in `tests/i18n/` imports `test` and `expect` from `tests/fixtures/area-tripwire.ts` (review, plan §4.3):
  it watches every page of the worker (the `page` fixture's and those opened with `browser.newPage`, also in `beforeAll`) and
  fails a test when one of its pages logged "was read before its area" or raised it as a page error. The tripwire logs before it
  throws, so a read inside app code that catches the error is still seen.
- `scripts/check-i18n-dist.mjs` (part of `npm run build`), per language of `languages.json`: each module's marker texts (catalog
  values that differ from English, 16+ characters; map literals, 12+, and originals, 8+, with a letter outside ASCII that no
  English source names, so the rule does not depend on Spanish letters; a language written in ASCII alone, such as Indonesian,
  finds no marker for a module that holds only maps and fails loudly until it gets its own rule; a text of two modules is not
  used) are all in one chunk named after it, which holds no other module's text (except as part of a longer text of
  its own); no other file carries any (the help chunks keep ES-HELP's rule); `index.html` loads and preloads none, and the static
  imports of its scripts reach none; the core's name appears in English files only in the loader's `import()` and its preload
  list, an area's only in the core, a shared module's only in the core and its areas. Budgets (KB gzip, level 9, × `budgetFactor`):
  core warn 120 / fail 150; each area or shared module 60 / 90; the sum 300 / 360; each help chunk 25 / 40. It prints the
  modules and each route's extra download.
- `tests/release/pages-smoke.pages.ts`: every route of `dist/` under the release headers in `?lang=es-419` fetches the core and
  exactly its area's modules, the holder is activated once and extended once per area (same language), and nothing logs an
  error; English routes fetch no language chunk.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, 2026-11-09, gzip level 9 as `check-i18n-dist.mjs` measures; the plan's estimate in brackets). 13
chunks, 238.8 KB gzip and 824.5 KB raw in all, against one chunk of 225.6 KB gzip and 820.6 KB raw on main: the raw text grows
only 3.9 KB (each module's wrapper), and the other 9 KB of gzip is compression context that small files lose:

| Module | Keys | Maps | KB gzip (raw) |
|---|---|---|---|
| core | 1,274 | 23 (appShell, camera, clusterHr, engineView, fieldInfo, fieldReadDetails, helpMenu, helpViewer, languageChoice, nakedEye, objectXGuide/Lab/Measure/Session, observatory, polish, projectLinks, qsoList, sound, spectralRange, tour, variableStars, webHelp) + all 748 originals | **104.3** (340.0) [103.3] |
| radio | 632 | radioAntenna, radioFinishSave, radioRecordSave | 40.3 (166.9) [40.0] |
| classification | 691 | observationMetadata | 28.0 (102.6) [27.2] |
| pulsar+radio | 0 | pulsar, radioR1 | 14.3 (57.1) [14.4] |
| photometer | 271 | – | 13.5 (44.0) [13.6] |
| lss | 126 | wedge | 8.7 (23.9) [8.8] |
| cluster | 186 | clusterData | 8.2 (26.7) [8.2] |
| exposure | 173 | – | 8.1 (25.2) [8.1] |
| cluster+photometer | 107 | – | 5.0 (14.7) [5.0] |
| pulsar | 0 | pulsarLoadSave, pulsarSavedRows | 3.9 (12.3) [3.8] |
| fits | 67 | – | 2.8 (7.1) [2.7] |
| exposure+lss | 27 | – | 1.3 (3.1) [1.3] |
| classification+cluster | 9 | – | 0.4 (0.9) [0.3] |

Extra download of each route on top of the core: radio 54.6 KB, classification 28.4, photometer 18.5, pulsar 18.2, cluster
13.6, lss 10.0, exposure 9.4, fits 2.8; Object X, the variable stars and the observatory nothing (the spectrometer route fetches
the exposure area when the field spectrometer is shown). All within the plan's acceptance: core ≤ 110, largest module ≤ 45,
largest route extra ≤ 60, sum ≤ 245; the 38 help chunks are byte-identical to main's.

**English** against main ef9f7ac (`npm run build` of both): `index.html` lists the same 80 files (only hashes changed); every
CSS file is byte-identical; the files `index.html` loads grow by 2,420 B raw and 952 B gzip (the entry `index` +937 B raw:
the loaders module, the 16 `lazyArea` sites, `loadArea` and the area notice; the shared chunk with the i18n machinery +1,483 B:
`extendLocale`, `markAreaFailed`, the pending-read rule in `t` and `resolveMessages`, `keep`). The pages smoke and
`es-areas.spec.ts` check that English fetches no language chunk on any route.

**Measurement** (the ES5 method above: `dist/` served from loopback by a throwaway server with gzip level 6 and `no-cache`,
ports 6393 and 6394; Chromium with the cache disabled and CDP throttling; first render as defined there; five runs each,
medians, every run within 0.13 s of the others; every Spanish run rendered in Spanish). *Before* is main ef9f7ac (one
226.3 KB chunk on the wire), *after* this front:

| Profile | Route | English before / after | Spanish before | Spanish after | Language chunks after (transfer, duration) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Slow 4G | home | 5.91 / 5.90 s | 7.25 s (+1.34) | 6.57 s (**+0.67**) | core 104.7 KB, 0.67 s |
| Slow 4G | lab | 7.75 / 7.66 s | 8.95 s (+1.20) | 8.52 s (**+0.86**) | core 0.67 s; classification 28.3 KB, 1.57 s; classification+cluster 0.7 KB, 1.19 s |
| Fast 3G | home | 10.07 / 10.02 s | 11.84 s (+1.77) | 11.16 s (**+1.14**) | core 1.14 s |
| Fast 3G | lab | 12.29 / 12.38 s | 14.05 s (+1.76) | 13.57 s (**+1.19**) | core 1.14 s; classification 2.39 s; classification+cluster 2.23 s |

- Spanish home minus English: 0.67 s on Slow 4G (limit 0.8) and 1.14 s on Fast 3G (limit 1.3). The lab costs 0.19 s (Slow 4G)
  and 0.05 s (Fast 3G) more than the home page (limit 0.3): its area loads in parallel with the lab's own chunks (about 300 KB more JavaScript than the home page). So SPLIT-2
  (lazy-mounting the guides and the E01 analyser to shrink the core) is not needed by the plan's own rule.
- JS before first render, home: English 1,026,346 → 1,027,304 B; Spanish 1,252,633 → 1,131,973 B. Lab (Slow 4G): English
  1,328,208 → 1,329,179 B; Spanish 1,541,004 → 1,433,005 B. CSS is unchanged: 21,776 B home, 26,542 B lab in English and
  27,091 B in Spanish (the selector's stylesheet).

**For ES-REL and later languages.** Keep `fallback:!!bundle.areas||…` in `activateLocale` when `SPANISH_RELEASED` flips; the
plugin then emits `complete:true` and production starts logging a pending key. A new language adds its line to
`languages.json` and its `src/i18n/<dir>/` with the same files; areas, `lazyArea` sites and budgets (× `budgetFactor`) apply
unchanged. A new route opts in with `lazyArea('<area>',()=>import(…))`; a new area is added to `AREAS`.

## Spanish: the release (ES-REL)

The plan's ES-REL item (`analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-es.md`, §7) after every Spanish sub-front (ES0–ES9, ES-HELP) and the
chunk split (ES-SPLIT) merged. User decision 5 of 2026-11-09 (AGENTS.md): Spanish turns on once all of them are in.

**Detection and the selector.** `SPANISH_RELEASED` (`src/i18n/locale.ts`) is `true`. One load decides once, in this order: a
`?lang=en` or `?lang=es-419` link parameter (that load only, not stored; the ES-SEO links and testers use it), the stored choice
(`openvireo-language`, `en` or `es-419` exactly), then the first browser language (`es`, `es-*` in any case → es-419; anything
else, including `['en-US','es-MX']`, → English). The English / Español selector is shown on every load, so English visitors
now fetch its small chunk and stylesheet (no language chunk); the dev-only pseudo-locales (qps, qps-cjk, qps-deva, qps-cyrl) show
none, as Front SC2's smoke requires, since it offers real languages only. The Spanish shell spec waits for the selector before it
lists the header's texts, because the chunk arrives after first render. Since the ES-REL review the chunk is imported by an effect of
`LanguageChoiceSlot` (`src/OpenVireo.tsx`), not as a `lazy()` under `Suspense`: a header boundary that suspended during the first
commits made React hold back the route's own Suspense content on its fallback-throttle timer, which froze routes under a paused
Playwright clock (six English photometer and Object ID specs in CI) and could delay a route's first paint. A change is stored and the page reloads at the same address;
when the address already has `?lang=` it is updated too (the parameter would otherwise win), and when storage fails the choice
travels as `?lang=` and the dialog says it is not remembered. The pre-release preview note is no longer shown. Later languages
keep their own gate until their sub-fronts merge; front SC8 replaces this flag with a per-language `released` in a language
registry (`front-i18n-scripts.md` §7), and nothing here assumes two languages beyond what was already there.

**Strict catalog and maps.** A whole released bundle builds strictly: `activateLocale` passes
`fallback:!!bundle.areas||(es-419&&!SPANISH_RELEASED)`, so only a split bundle (every production Spanish load) keeps the English
fallback, which its per-module catalog needs. Strictness is therefore static:
- `src/i18n/es/es.test.ts`: `createI18n(es,'es-419')` without fallback (every en.json key, the same `{params}`, no extra key), and a
  complete Spanish map for every `pickMessages` id (`MAP_IDS`, 33), checked by `mapParity` without `partial`;
- `src/i18n/split.test.ts` item 7: the plan's `complete` is true, and the core module says so;
