- at run time `resolveMessages` no longer completes a partial Spanish map with English: a map that does not match stays English,
  throws in the dev server and tests, and is logged in production.

With `complete:true` the plugin's core module lets production log a key read before its area arrived (once per load; see
ES-SPLIT, "Reads before an area arrives"). The pages smoke opens every route of `dist/` in an es-MX browser and requires no
console error, so a pending read in normal navigation would fail it.

**Coverage 100 %.** `node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs`: en.json 3567/3567 keys; 33/33 maps; 748 originals; "0 render sites or
module literals still pending". What closed the gaps:
- four keys translated: `hubbleHtl.reason.photographic-image`, `…variable-merge` and `…spectrum-continuation` (core code renders
  them through the run-time head `hubbleHtl.reason.`), and `continuity.supportedTarget`, which is not dead: `src/core/targetCatalog.ts`
  names it as the first group of the observatory's target picker and `OpenVireo.tsx` renders it with `t(group.key)`, so the
  Spanish picker showed "Supported acquisition" until now («Adquisición compatible», as the route's other «compatible» texts);
- the one `pending-translation` receipt (112 module literals of `src/help/webHelp.ts`) was stale: since ES-HELP those literals are
  the English source of the map `webHelp`, whose Spanish is `src/i18n/es/maps/webHelp.ts`. `webHelp.ts` left the audit's
  `PRESENTATION_MODULES` as `tour.ts` did in ES1, and the receipt was removed. `vireoAudit.test.ts` now fails on any receipt
  whose reason is not `identifier`, `native text mirrored in a file` or `translated in pieces`, and the coverage report counts
  the remaining receipts by reason (170 identifier, 2 translated in pieces, 2 native text mirrored in a file: sites that render
  a VIREO value outside `vireo()` on purpose).

**Tests.** The Spanish specs open Spanish by the browser language alone: each sets `test.use({locale:'es-MX'})` and loads its
routes without `?lang=` (`tests/fixtures/language.ts`); English reference runs in the same specs ask for `?lang=en`, the
pseudo-locale for `?lang=qps`, and `exports-identity.spec.ts` makes each context in its own language (en-US or es-MX). English
specs keep Playwright's `locale:'en-US'` from the four configs; the English area test of `es-areas.spec.ts` runs in an en-US
browser. Only the tests that check the preview link keep `?lang=es-419` (the pages smoke's ES1/ES2/ES-HELP tests and the
selector spec). New or rewritten:
- `language-selector.spec.ts`: detection for es-MX, es, es-ES, es-419, en-US, `['en-US','es-MX']`, `['fr-FR','es-MX']` and none;
  the stored choice beats the browser and `?lang=` beats both; Cancel and Escape store nothing; Save, then Change and reload
  stores the choice with a clean address and back; a change on a `?lang=` load; blocked storage; View original;
- `es-narrow.spec.ts`: the full 320 px sweep in Spanish (home, the 16 routes, the header menu with the selector and its
  confirmation, OpenVireo's help and About) with `layoutProblems()`; the exercise specs keep their own 320/1440 px walks;
- `es-browsers.spec.ts`: a small engine smoke (English in en-US; Spanish in es-MX through boot, a route area, a lazy dialog, the
  help viewer and the selector both ways; the home and a lab at 320 px), run in Chromium by the suite and, for the release, with
  `--browser=firefox` and `--browser=webkit`;
- `pages-smoke.pages.ts`: every route of `dist/` in an es-MX browser without `?lang=`, an es-MX home with the selector under the
  release CSP, the English loads now with the selector, and the bilingual 404.

**Release text.** `public/404.html` is bilingual (English first, then Spanish under `lang="es-419"`). README and
THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt carry the line "Spanish translation: an OpenVireo web adaptation; CLEA's files and texts are unchanged." In
Spanish, Help › About OpenVireo's "This site" paragraph says the same («La traducción al español es una adaptación web de
OpenVireo; los archivos y textos de CLEA no cambian.»; `src/i18n/es/maps/polish.ts`, Spanish only).

**Search engines.** Nothing advertises a Spanish URL yet: no `hreflang`, no `/es/` page, and the sitemap, the lab pages
(`/labs/`) and their JSON-LD (`inLanguage:'en'`) are unchanged. The static `index.html` and lab pages stay English, and a crawler
that renders the app with an English browser language sees English. A crawler that sends a Spanish `Accept-Language` and renders
JavaScript with it would see Spanish at `/`; the Spanish pages and their `hreflang` are ES-SEO's (plan §4).

**Verification** (2026-11-09, on the merged tree of ES-SPLIT plus this front). Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`,
`src/theme/scripts.test.ts` and `src/core/cleaHelp.test.ts`: 572 tests. Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig):
every `tests/i18n/*.spec.ts`, 139 tests in 16 files (two failures in one batch came from editing a source file while the dev server ran,
which reloads its pages, and passed when rerun; one E03 test failed once under load and passed alone); English samples of the
shell, menu, tour, help, project links, instructor mode, focus, SEO lab pages and phone layouts, 70 tests;
`npm run test:scripts`, 59 tests; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/`, 50 tests. Firefox (native) and WebKit (the local
`mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble` image, as the WebKit sound check did, since the native WebKit lacks host libraries
here) passed `es-browsers.spec.ts` and `language-selector.spec.ts`, 10 tests each. In Firefox the 320 px sweep finds one problem:
the analyser's x-axis title («Longitud de onda · Å») crosses the bottom of its SVG by 1 px, the same fixed-y axis title that the
script walk lists for SC11 (`tests/scripts/routes.scripts.ts` KNOWN, `src/SpectrumInstrument.tsx`); Chromium passes.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`). 13 chunks, 238.9 KB gzip in all (ES-SPLIT: 238.8): core 104.4 KB gzip (340.7
raw; the four keys and the About sentence), the area modules unchanged (radio 40.3, classification 28.0, pulsar+radio 14.3,
photometer 13.5, lss 8.7, cluster 8.2, exposure 8.1, cluster+photometer 5.0, pulsar 3.9, fits 2.8, exposure+lss 1.3,
classification+cluster 0.4); 38 help chunks, 54.4 KB gzip. English loads now also fetch the selector: `LanguageChoice` JS and its
0.49 KB stylesheet (0.24 KB gzip), after the first render.

**Left for later fronts.** ES-SEO: `/es/`, `/es/labs/`, `hreflang` and the Spanish preview image. SC8: the language registry
(per-language `released`, the `<select>` selector, `matchLanguage`). SC11: the fixed-y plot axis titles above. OpenVireo's own
help (`webHelp.ts`) and the tour do not mention the language selector yet; a topic for it needs English and Spanish text.

## Spanish: search pages (ES-SEO)

User decision 8 of 2026-11-09 (AGENTS.md): after the release, a Spanish home page `/es/` and the Spanish lab pages `/es/labs/`,
with `hreflang` and a Spanish preview image. Plan: `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-es.md` §4. The pages themselves are described in
`docs/SEO.md` ("Front ES-SEO"); this section records what touches the language rules.

**`/es/` is a static page, not the app.** It is plain HTML like the lab pages (no script), in Spanish, and its buttons open the app
with `?lang=es-419`. A prerendered copy of `index.html` at `/es/` would have needed boot to read the path (a fourth input to
`decideLocale`, front SC8's file) or a redirect chain. With a link, the precedence of "The language of one load" is unchanged:
`?lang=es-419` applies to that load only and is never stored, so a visitor who prefers English is never kept in Spanish (the
address shows the parameter, the selector still changes it, and a later visit to `/` follows the stored choice or the browser).
Every Spanish page links its English version; the link to the English home is `/?lang=en`, so a Spanish browser that asks for
English gets it. The English lab pages link their Spanish version («Español»).

**Lab links.** «Abrir la práctica» is `/?lang=es-419#lab=<slug>`. The `#lab=` effect (`OpenVireo.tsx`) removes only the hash, so
`?lang=es-419` stays and a reload stays Spanish. The observatory footer's «Acerca de las siete prácticas» now links the labs index
of the page's language (`labsIndexUrl(uiLanguage())`, `src/seo/labSlugs.ts`): `/es/labs/` in Spanish, `/labs/` otherwise.

**A crawler that renders Spanish at `/` (the ES-REL note).** No extra measure: `/` declares itself English (`<html lang="en">`,
English static title, description and boot text, self canonical) and names `/es/` as its `es` alternate (`es-419` is not a Google `hreflang` region; docs/SEO.md) with `x-default`
`/`, and the sitemap repeats it. Google renders without a Spanish `Accept-Language` and uses the alternates to send Spanish
searchers to `/es/`; a crawler that ran the app in Spanish would still read the canonical and alternates of an English page. Turning
detection off for crawlers would need user-agent sniffing (serving crawlers something else), which is neither cheap nor standard.

**Text rules.** The Spanish texts (`src/seo/labs.es.ts`, the `es-419` row of `src/seo/languages.ts`) are OpenVireo's web adaptation
of its own English pages, in neutral Latin American Spanish with «tú». `src/seo/languages.test.ts` applies the glossary
(`src/i18n/es/glossary.json`: required terms, fixed names, forbidden and Spain-only words; "star cluster" may be «cúmulo estelar» as
§5.1's table says) to each English/Spanish pair, and forbids presenting the text as CLEA's. The footer says «Este texto en español es
una adaptación web hecha por OpenVireo, no un texto de CLEA.»

**The dist check and static Spanish HTML.** `scripts/check-i18n-dist.mjs` (front SC8's) fails when any file outside a language's
chunks contains one of its markers: a catalog value of 16+ characters that differs from English, a map literal of 12+ characters
with a letter outside ASCII, or an original of 8+. The static pages are such files, and the first draft hit six markers: the app's
own names «Clasificación estelar» (`class.nav`), «Fotometría de las Pléyades» (`ph.nav`), «Clasificación» (map `polish`),
«Radioastronomía de púlsares» (`radioRoute.exercise.step.exercise`), «Mide el corrimiento al rojo» (`lss.measure.title`) and «Decide
qué es» (map `objectXGuide`). The pages were reworded instead of changing the script («Estrellas: clasificación espectral»,
«Pléyades: fotometría», «La clasificación de los espectros estelares», «Radioastronomía de los púlsares»…), so their lab names differ
slightly from the app's menu names. **Resolved after SC8:** SC8's `pagePath` lets an `.html` file under `dist/es/` carry Spanish
text, so the two lab names are the app's again («Clasificación estelar», «Fotometría de las Pléyades»); the other rewordings stay,
as they read well. `languages.test.ts` now checks the reverse: no English static page carries a Spanish marker. PT-SEO uses its own
`pagePath` (`pt-br`).

**For later languages (PT-SEO).** A language adds data only: its lab texts (`src/seo/labs.<dir>.ts`, typed `LabText` by slug), one
row in `SEO_LANGUAGES` (tag, hreflang codes such as `['pt-BR','pt']`, ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 regions only, `og:locale`, prefix, app link, UI, index and home texts,
preview image and its text), its prefix in `SEO_PATH_PREFIX`, a `/<prefix>/*` rule in `public/_headers` (and in
`src/deployHeaders.test.ts`), and its image rendered with `node scripts/render-og-image.mjs <tag>`. The renderer, the hreflang sets,
the sitemap, the dev server and the emitted files follow the table. `languages.test.ts` is written for Spanish in places (the
glossary lint, the dist-marker guard, the expected alternates list): PT-SEO generalises those per language. The test that a SEO
language exists in the app reads `src/i18n/languages.json` as it is on main; front SC8's registry should replace it with "released".

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-es-seo` from main `ab23e7a`). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/seo`,
`src/deployHeaders.test.ts`, `src/i18n/es/es.test.ts` and `src/i18n` (the `dynamic-key-sites.json` pin refreshed: the two page and
sitemap heads of `src/seo/pages.ts` changed, both `[unreached]`); `npm run build` with every check (`check-i18n-dist.mjs` passes,
unchanged); Playwright (dev server, Chromium) `tests/i18n/es-seo.spec.ts`, `tests/seo-labs.spec.ts`, `es-shell`,
`language-selector` and `es-browsers`; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` (51 tests, one new). The English home head and every English
lab page were compared with main's output: only the `hreflang` links, `og:locale`/`og:locale:alternate` and the header's «Español»
link were added.

## Script groundwork: the language registry, detection and selector (SC8)

The plan is §7 and §9 of `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-scripts.md`, with the five additions of
`analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §1.3 that a second, partial language needs (research root). Until this front the
release gate was one flag, `SPANISH_RELEASED`, and about twenty places named es-419. Now every translated language is one line of
a registry, and Spanish behaves exactly as ES-REL released it.

**The registry.** `src/i18n/languages.json` stays the single list, one line per language, because the Node build scripts read it
without compiling TypeScript (`scripts/i18n-split.mjs` plans each language's chunks, `scripts/check-i18n-dist.mjs` checks them,
`i18n-batch.mjs` and `i18n-coverage.mjs` take `--lang <tag>`). `src/i18n/languages.ts` types it and holds the pure rules the app
needs. English is the source language and is not in the list. The fields:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `tag` | the `?lang=` value, the stored value and the bundle's `language`; exact and case-sensitive (`es-419`, `pt-BR`) |
| `dir`, `catalog` | `src/i18n/<dir>/` with `<catalog>`, `originals.json`, `maps/`, `index.ts` (the manifest) and, with `help`, `help/` |
| `budgetFactor` | multiplies the chunk budgets of `check-i18n-dist.mjs` (1 for Latin and Cyrillic; 1.4–1.6 planned for CJK and Devanagari) |
| `htmlLang` | `<html lang>`, and the `lang` of the language's option and failure notice |
| `pagePath` | the site path of the language's own static pages, `dist/<pagePath>/…` (`es`; pt-BR will use `pt-br`), which may carry its text |
| `autonym`, `englishName` | the selector's option (the autonym, plus the English name when it has no Latin letter: `日本語 (Japanese)`) and the English half of the failure notices |
| `match`, `except` | browser language ranges (below) |
| `released` | the release gate of decision 5 |
| `help` | `{"related":"…"}` when the language translates all 38 help files: its "RELATED TOPICS" marker |
| `notices` | the language's own sentences for "could not be loaded" and "part of the text could not be loaded" |
| `path` | tests only: a root-relative directory instead of `src/i18n/<dir>` (the fixture below) |

Spanish's line: `es-419`, dir `es`, page path `es`, «Español», match `es`, released, help «TEMAS RELACIONADOS», and the two notices that were
`LANGUAGE_FAILURE_NOTICE` and `AREA_FAILURE_NOTICE` (their English halves are built from `englishName` and read exactly as before).

**Detection** (`decideLocale`, `src/i18n/locale.ts`; `matchLanguage`, `src/i18n/languages.ts`). One load decides once:
1. `?lang=<tag>`: English or **any** registered language, released or not (the preview); that load only, never stored;
2. the stored choice: English or a **released** language;
3. the first browser language: the registered language whose `match` range covers it most specifically (RFC 4647 basic
   filtering, any case: a range covers a tag equal to it or starting with range + `-`), unless its `except` covers it at least as
   specifically; English when that language is not released, or when none matches. Only the first preference decides, and an
   unreleased first preference gives English, never the second preference.

So `es`, `es-MX`, `ES-ar` give Spanish exactly as before; `?lang=ES-419` and `?lang=es` are no tag (English); the dev-only
pseudo-locales are link values only. For the languages to come the ranges are those of the plans: `pt` (pt-PT and pt-AO get pt-BR,
`gl` does not), `fr`, `id`, `hi`, `ja`, `ru` (never `uk`, `be`, `kk`), `zh-Hans` with `zh-Hans`, `zh-CN`, `zh-SG`, `zh-MY` and bare `zh`
and `except` `zh-Hant`, `zh-TW`, `zh-HK`, `zh-MO`, and later `zh-Hant` with those four. The script subtag beats the region
(`zh-Hant-CN` is Traditional, `zh-Hans-TW` Simplified), and while zh-Hant does not exist a Traditional-Chinese browser opens
English, with Simplified offered in the selector once it is released (decision of 2026-11-09). `tests/fixtures/i18n/registry.ts`
holds those entries for the tests only; each language adds its own line in its first front. The plan's case-insensitive `?lang=`
and its `zh-CN`/`zh-TW` link aliases were not adopted: ES-REL's tests pin exact tags, and detection covers browsers anyway.

**The gate, per language.** `released` replaces `SPANISH_RELEASED` everywhere it acted:
- `activateLocale` (`src/i18n/active.ts`) builds with the English fallback a split bundle (as since ES-SPLIT) or a language before
  its release; only a whole released bundle (Vitest's `src/i18n/es/index.ts`) builds strictly;
- `resolveMessages` (`src/i18n/registry.ts`) completes a partial map with English for a language before its release (a map of the
  wrong shape still throws in the dev server and tests); a released language's maps must be complete;
- the planner reports a released language whose catalog does not cover en.json as a problem, which fails the build;
- `check-i18n-dist.mjs` requires marker text for every module of a released language.

**The selector** (`src/components/LanguageChoice.tsx`). A native `<select>` replaces the two-option pill, in the same place at the
foot of the header menu, drawn like the theme switch: one row for any number of languages, with keyboard, touch and screen-reader
behaviour from the platform. Its accessible name is the map's `legend` («Idioma / Language», visually hidden as before). It lists
English, every released language and, on a preview load, the previewed language, in registry order; each option is its autonym in
its own `lang`. It is shown when it offers a choice (`selectorShown`): on every load since ES-REL, never under the pseudo-locales,
and, if no language were released, only on a preview load. The confirmation, Save investigation, Cancel and Escape, the storage
rules and View original are unchanged; the dialog names the language by its option text. On a preview load nothing is stored (ES1
review 1, C2, now for every language before its release): the choice travels as `?lang=` and the dialog shows the preview note,
so a tester's choice cannot start applying once that language is released. The map `languageChoice` lost `english` and `spanish`
(the options come from the registry; `src/i18n/es/identical.json` lost their two entries). Test ids: `language-select`, and
`language-<tag>` on each option.

**Failure notices.** `failureNotice(kind, tag)` builds both from the registry; the shell renders the language's half with its
`htmlLang` (`lang="es-419"`, before `lang="es"`).

**Help files.** Each help module now names its `language` (the 38 Spanish ones gained `export const language='es-419'`). The loader
(`src/help/helpTranslation.ts`) looks in `src/i18n/<dir>/help/` of this load's language, offers the translation when the
language's entry has `help`, refuses a module of another language, and `relatedMarker(view)` gives the language's marker
(`RELATED_MARKER` is the original's). `check-i18n-dist.mjs` assigns each help chunk to the one registered language whose quoted tag
it holds and requires 38 chunks, one per help file, for each language with `help` and none for the others. Two languages' help
modules for an empty original (MLTSPHLP.LST, SPLSTHLP.LST) therefore stay distinct chunks.

**A partial language before its release.**
- *Planner* (`scripts/i18n-split.mjs`): `languageDir(language)` is `src/i18n/<dir>` (or a fixture's `path`). For a language before its
  release, an area with no keys, maps or shared modules is not a problem but `empty`: the core's `areas` entry resolves at once to
  an empty part (`Promise.resolve(...)`, no `import()`), so it has no chunk and `loadArea` marks it loaded. The gate is `released`,
  not a complete catalog (SC8 review): the `pulsar` area holds only maps, so a language whose catalog is complete before its maps
  has it empty. For a released language an empty area is a problem, as before.
- *Runtime*: a key the partial catalog lacks is English without the tripwire (its bundle is not `complete`, and `keyModules` lists
  only keys it has); a map it lacks is English; a partial map is completed with English.
- *Dist check*: a text that another registered language's sources also hold (catalog values, map literals, originals) is no
  marker, so a Portuguese word equal to a Spanish one is not reported as misplaced; an empty area must have no chunk; a module of
  an unreleased language with no marker yet (short or English-equal texts) is found by its chunk name, with a warning. A
  language's own static pages, an `.html` file under `dist/<pagePath>/` (ES-SEO's `dist/es/index.html`, `dist/es/labs/…`), may carry
  that language's text (its lab names, say), never another language's; an `.html` file elsewhere (`dist/labs/…`) and every `.js`
  file outside the language's chunks, also under its path, keep the rule (coordinator's addition for ES-SEO). The script
  is now a function, `checkI18nDist(dist, {root, languages, graph})`, with the same command line.
- *Tests*: `src/i18n/split.test.ts` loops over every registered language (its whole bundle from `src/i18n/<dir>/index.ts`, its core
  from the loaders) and plans the fixture.

**Adding a language** (what PT0 does for pt-BR):
1. Add its line to `src/i18n/languages.json` with `"released":false`, no `help`, its `notices`, `match` (pt-BR: `["pt"]`) and
   `budgetFactor`; dir in lower case (`pt-br`).
2. Create `src/i18n/<dir>/` like `src/i18n/es/`: `<catalog>.json` (any subset of en.json), `originals.json` (may be `{}`), `maps/`,
   and `index.ts` exporting the whole `LocaleBundle` with `language:'<tag>'` and `maps:Object.freeze({...})` (the manifest the planner
   parses); its lints, glossary and test as SC9's framework and the language's profile require.
3. `npm run build`: the planner gives it a core, its non-empty areas, and empty areas without chunks; `check-i18n-dist.mjs` lists
   them. `?lang=<tag>` previews it; nothing detects, stores or offers it otherwise. Vitest keeps a module cache
   (`node_modules/.vitest-cache`) in which an `import.meta.glob` keeps the directories it saw: after adding `src/i18n/<dir>/`, delete
   that cache once, or `split.test.ts` and `languages.test.ts` miss the new directory locally (CI starts without it).
4. Its help front adds `src/i18n/<dir>/help/<NAME>.ts` for all 38 files (each with `language`) and `help` in the registry line.
5. Its release front sets `"released":true` once the catalog and maps are complete (the planner, `split.test.ts` item 7 and
   `check-i18n-dist.mjs` enforce it), which turns on detection, storage and the option for everyone.

**Tests** (Vitest). `src/i18n/languages.test.ts`: the registry is valid (unique tags and directories, canonical `htmlLang`, names,
notices, no range claimed twice), each language's directory and help modules, Spanish's ES-REL behaviour, the detection table of
the plans for every language to come (before and after its release, zh-Hant absent and present), the selector's list and labels.
`src/i18n/locale.test.ts`: precedence with Spanish released and with Spanish before its release. `src/i18n/partialLanguage.test.ts`:
the test-only fixture `qaa-XA` (`tests/fixtures/i18n/qaa-xa/`, ISO 639's local-use range; three catalog keys, one partial map,
one original; registry entry `tests/fixtures/i18n/partial.ts`) settles a `?lang=qaa-XA` load with its title and `<html lang>`,
falls back to English, completes its partial map, resolves empty areas, never opens from the browser or storage, and shows its
notices. `src/i18n/split.test.ts`: the fixture's plan (keys where their files render them, seven empty areas, inline empty areas
in the core's code, released-while-incomplete refused), and a complete catalog with no maps before its release (`pulsar` empty, no
problem; released, a problem). `src/i18n/distCheck.test.ts`: `checkI18nDist` on synthetic dist trees
(the fixture accepted; misplaced text, a chunk for an empty area, a preloaded core and missing help refused; help chunks assigned by
their language; a text shared by two languages; a marker-less module found by name; a Spanish marker accepted in
`dist/es/x/index.html` and `dist/es/labs/hubble/index.html`, refused in `dist/labs/x/index.html`, in the English entry and in
`dist/es/x.js`, and a fixture marker refused on a Spanish page). Playwright: `language-selector.spec.ts` uses
the select and checks its options, their `lang` and keyboard use; `es-narrow`, `es-browsers`, `es-shell`, `es-areas` and the pages
smoke follow the select and `lang="es-419"`.

**Proof with a real build** (scratch, not committed). With the fixture copied to `src/i18n/qaa-xa/` and its line in the registry,
`npm run build` passed: `check-i18n-dist.mjs` listed qaa-XA with a 1.3 KB core, a 0.2 KB fits chunk and seven areas "empty: no text
yet, no chunk", and the Spanish modules unchanged; `split.test.ts` passed for both languages (the virtual modules, with their inline
empty areas, put together give the whole fixture bundle); in the dev server `?lang=qaa-XA` showed its title, `<html lang>` and text,
fetched only its core and, on the images route, its fits module (none on the radio route), offered English, Español and Testlingvo in
the selector at 320 px, and stored nothing when changed to Spanish; an en browser with `qaa` as its language and `qaa-XA` stored
opened English with only English and Español offered. The fixture was then removed.

**Verification** (2026-11-10). `npx tsc -b`; the whole Vitest suite, 5,125 tests (and, after the last changes, `src/i18n`, `src/help`,
`src/theme/scripts.test.ts`, `src/core/cleaHelp.test.ts`); Playwright in the dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig: every
`tests/i18n/*.spec.ts`, 140 tests in 16 files, the pseudo-locale smoke and English shell, project links, instructor mode, help and
focus specs, 32 tests; `npm run test:scripts`, 59 tests; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/`, 50 tests. No failures.

**Sizes** (`npm run build` against main ab23e7a). Spanish: the 12 area and shared chunks are byte-identical; the core is 37 bytes
smaller (the map `languageChoice` lost `english:'English'` and `spanish:'Español'`; 104.4 KB gzip as before); the 38 help chunks
grow by about 24 bytes each (`language`), 54.4 → 55.1 KB gzip in all. English: `index.html` lists the same 80 files; the files it
loads grow by 1,223 B raw and 594 B gzip (the shared chunk +1,090 B: the registry JSON and its rules; the entry +34 B; `CleaHelp` +99
B); every CSS file is byte-identical except the selector's own (492 → 1,078 B, 251 → 428 B gzip), and the selector's chunk is
4,332 B (+120 B).

**Left for later fronts.** The per-language rows of the pages smoke and the script route walk (each language's REL front); the
Spanish lints and the exports identity spec on the lint framework (SC9, SC10); Unicode help text (SC7); the budget factors for CJK
and Devanagari, measured on each language's first batch.

## Script groundwork: the Spanish lints on the framework (SC9)

- **The profile.** `src/i18n/lint/profiles.ts` has an `es-419` profile (`PROFILE_IDS`, `REGISTER.es`: `tu`, decision 6, with
  `usted` as the expressible alternative). It reproduces the checks `src/i18n/es/es.test.ts` used to hand-code:
  - no script, so `script` and `leftover-english` never apply; Unicode word edges and the `es` case fold;
  - the nine glyphs, each counted on its own, so a `…` that becomes `...` still fails. VIREO's `...` is not counted, as
    before: Spanish adds it where it glosses a VIREO name («Lista de púlsares... (Pulsar List...)», 12 strings);
  - ¿? and ¡! with the ES5 closer contexts (`closeAfter`/`closeBefore`). `fragment|` originals are skipped, and a VIREO
    message quoted in English inside “…” keeps its marks;
  - the register: `usted`/`ustedes` with the old test's ASCII word edges, so «ustedés» still fails as it did;
  - the claim `traducci[oó]n (oficial|de CLEA)`;
  - `SPANISH_VIREO_PULSAR_NAMES` as `forbiddenSetAside`;
  - the length band 0.7–1.6, now in grapheme clusters, reported only;
  - plural categories one, many and other, with `many` optional.
  - The data stays in `src/i18n/es/`: `glossary.json` through `glossaryFrom(…,'es')` (terms, names, the forbidden words
    with voseo and the usted imperatives, and the forbidden patterns), `identical.json` and `lint-allowed.json`.
- **New with SC9, all stricter.**
  - Placeholders are a multiset of `{param}` and «An», where only «An» presence was checked. One finding was settled:
    `original:fragment|Meter Radio Telescope` gains `{diameter}` («Radiotelescopio de {diameter} metros»). It is now a
    `placeholders` allowance in `lint-allowed.json`, with its reason.
  - An original's English is its literal without the `context|` prefix. The 15 context originals are now compared with their
    real English (identical, glossary names).
  - The framework's digits, NFC and invisible-character rules apply.
  - A typography rule: no space after ¿ or ¡.
  - Spanish forbids no quote mark: it writes both “ ” and « ».
  - The catalog had no finding under any of these except the one above.
- **es.test.ts now** loads the Spanish files, collects the pairs with `collectPairs` and runs `lintPairs` with the profile
  (6,316 pairs; errors must be empty, length reports are printed). It keeps the Spanish structural checks: the strict
  catalog, map parity and completeness, originals keys, and the shell at 100 %. It also keeps the ES1 examples ("the lint
  catches what it must"), now through `lintPair` with the real glossary.
  - One guard is new: a `words` allowance may only exempt a forbidden glossary word, never `usted`, a claim or a forbidden
    pattern. That was the old reach of `lint-allowed.json`; the framework would otherwise accept any finding whose matched
    text an allowance names. The review added that a Spanish entry holds only `words`, `placeholders` and `reason`: a
    whole-rule `rules` allowance, which the framework accepts for other languages, would exempt usted or a claim.
  - `forbiddenIn` and `unpaired` are gone (nothing imported them).
  - `vireoSites.test.ts` is unchanged. It is a structural check (every `vireo()` literal has an original or an
    `original:` reason) with no per-string lint to move. Run again, `UNRESOLVED_SITES` is still 140.
  - The help-text lint in `src/help/helpTranslation.test.ts` (forbidden words, usted, vos over the 35 help files) is
    outside SC9 and still has its own code.
- **Fixtures.** `fixtures.ts` has 31 es-419 fixtures for every rule the profile configures, including the register
  flipped to usted, the set-aside VIREO names, a bare `?`, quoted English, and `…` versus `...`. `rules.test.ts` now
  requires fixtures only for the rules a profile configures: `script` and `leftover-english` need a script, `quotes`
  forbidden marks, `typography` patterns, and `purity` a script check. This is the refactor that pt-BR needs too.
- **Equivalence evidence.** A scratch Vitest file, not committed, ran the old checks verbatim (main ce98d48's `es.test.ts`)
  and the framework side by side on the same pairs (6,316, identical ids and order):
  - on the current catalog;
  - on 140 broken copies:
    - each string set to its English;
    - each of the 47 forbidden words appended, in lower and upper case;
    - usted, ustedes and «ustedés»;
    - the forbidden patterns and the claims;
    - the VIREO pulsar names with and without the verb;
    - ¿ ¡ ? ! dropped, or added stray or inside quoted English;
    - each of the nine glyphs dropped or added, and `…`↔`...`;
    - a placeholder dropped or duplicated;
    - HTML added;
    - every required glossary term or fixed name removed.
  - For each copy and rule it compared the sets of string ids each side flags. The old side flagged 824,789 (copy, rule,
    id) entries, and **every one is flagged by the framework**: no copy lost a single id. The framework flags 1,519 more:
    - 791 duplicated and 703 dropped `{param}` placeholders;
    - 15 context originals equal to their literal;
    - 8 stray ¿ or ¡ put before a string that begins with a space (the new typography rule);
    - 2 term removals that also removed a placeholder.
  - On the current catalog, after the allowance, both sides find nothing and every allowance is still needed.
  - The independent review reproduced it with its own scratch file and its own 88 broken copies (each string set to its
    English, or the English set to the translation; «An» dropped or renumbered; ¿ ¡ ? ! dropped or added, and quoted English
    messages; each glyph replaced by its ASCII look-alike or added; forbidden words at the start, middle, end and inside
    brackets, quotes and ¿?, in three cases; the forbidden patterns, eight usted spellings including «ñusted» and «ustedés»,
    five claims, seven pulsar-name variants; glossary terms and names removed, terms and exact names added to the English;
    HTML; 30 random combinations of three and 20 fuzzed copies). The old side flagged 413,153 (copy, string) entries in
    650,202 (copy, string, check) hits; the framework flags every one under the matching rule, and 6,334 further entries
    the old side passed.
- **Layout fixture.** The nine Spanish specs with a local copy now import `tests/fixtures/layoutProblems.ts`:
  `es-e01`…`es-e06`, `es-e08`, `es-observatory` and `es-radio`. `es-narrow` and `es-browsers` already did. Each passes the
  options that keep its old copy's checks:
  - e03, e05 and e08: the defaults;
  - e01: `dialogs:'dialog[open]'`;
  - e02: `crossingExempt:'.wedge-table-wrap'`;
  - e04: the native cluster and Pleiades plot skips and `.photometer-table`;
  - e06: `explainOverflow`;
  - radio: modal alert dialogs, `topDialog` and `dt`.
  - Two options are new:
    - `crossingInScrollers`, for e01, e02 and e04, whose copies reported a crossing inside a sideways scroller too;
    - `eachDialog`, for es-observatory, whose copy was a different check: every open dialog, menu and sub-menu, with `p`,
      `small`, `dt` and `dd`, clipped or crossing the dialog that clips. It is reproduced line for line.
  - Where the fixture checks more than a copy did, the spec gets the extra checks:
    - box roles for e01;
    - plot text for radio;
    - the page's own controls and plot text for the observatory.
  - Defaults are unchanged for `routes.scripts.ts`, es-narrow and es-browsers.
  - **A real problem found.** The plot-text check is new to es-observatory, and it fails the Multi-Spec walk at 320 and
    1440 px, in English as well as Spanish, so the cause is not the translation:
    - the observatory sky map (`src/OpenVireo.tsx`, the `sky-object` label at `x+13`) places the Coma-1 target labels
      («NGC 4874 · Coma - 1», «… · supported galaxy») at x ≈ 705 of its 700-unit view, 43–148 px past the SVG's right
      edge; the `sky-clip` hides them, so the student sees the target ring at the edge with no label;
    - the Multi-Spec field view (`src/multispec/MultiSpecFieldOverlay.tsx`, the number's baseline at `y+4`, 11 px font)
      cuts the top 3 px of the selection number «3» for a selection at y=4.
  - These are recorded in `MULTISPEC_KNOWN` rather than skipped: anything else still fails, and an entry that stops
    appearing fails too, so the fix removes it. The review split the label entry in two (one per label, only the walk steps
    that show the sky map), so fixing one label fails its own entry. The fix belongs to the sky map and Multi-Spec
    components, outside SC9: flip a sky-map label to the left of its ring (`text-anchor:end` at `x-13`) when it would leave
    the view, as the Multi-Spec overlay already does sideways; keep the Multi-Spec number's baseline between the font's
    ascent and the bottom edge (or place it under the box near the top). Check first whether VIREO's own drawing places
    them that way (REVIEW.md).
  - Fixed by Front SKY-LABELS (2026-11-10) as web adaptations: the sky-map labels are a web overlay, and the vertical
    placement of VIREO's Multi-Spec number (0x53d6f0) is not recovered
    (docs/SKY-FIELD-VIEW.md, docs/MULTI-SPEC.md, src/core/overlayLabels.ts); `MULTISPEC_KNOWN` is empty.
- **Verification** (2026-11-10):
  - `npx tsc -b` and `npm run build`.
  - Vitest:
    - `src/i18n`: 466 tests in 18 files, of which the lint framework is 362 with the 31 Spanish fixtures;
    - `src/help` and `vireoSites.test.ts`;
    - the pins refreshed with no diff;
    - the whole suite: 5,198 passed. `cameraParameters.test.ts` timed out under load (5 s), passed run alone and is unrelated
      to SC9.
  - Playwright, Chromium, dev server, DejaVu fontconfig: `es-e01`…`es-e06`, `es-e08`, `es-observatory`, `es-radio`, `es-narrow`
    and `es-browsers`, 72 tests in 11 files.
    - Two E01/E02 `qps` walks timed out in the first batch and passed run alone. They were load flakes, and they do not use
      the layout check.
    - The Multi-Spec layout tests found the problem above.
  - `npm run test:scripts`: 59 tests.
- **Independent review** (Claude Opus 5.5, high effort, 2026-11-10). Raw findings:
  1. Low: `MULTISPEC_KNOWN` matched both sky-map labels with one pattern (any step, any suffix), so fixing one label would
     not have failed. Fixed: one entry per label, built from `sky.supportedGalaxyLabel` for the second (the Spanish walk
     reads «… · galaxia compatible»), limited to the three walk steps that show the sky map.
  2. Low: the word-allowance guard did not cover the framework's whole-rule `rules` allowances, which would exempt usted or a
     claim. Fixed: a Spanish entry may hold only `words`, `placeholders` and `reason` (es.test.ts).
  3. Docs: the sky-map labels are not drawn visibly past the SVG; they are placed outside the 700-unit view, where the
     `sky-clip` hides them. Fixed in the problem description above, with the files and a suggested fix.
  - Checked without a finding: the equivalence (reproduced independently, above); `{diameter}` (radioIdCaptionText places
    the number where the translation holds `{diameter}`, so «Radiotelescopio de 40 metros» renders); the usted pattern
    (`[A-Za-z0-9_]` under the `iu` flags matches the same characters as `\b`'s word characters, including ſ and K); every
    switched spec's options against its old copy (e01's added box roles: by a source search, only the radio and wedge
    components render `role=dialog`); the `rules.test.ts` filter (every SC4 profile configures a script, forbidden marks
    and typography, so none lost a required fixture).
  - Review verification: `npx tsc -b`, `npm run build`; Vitest `src/i18n src/help` 601 passed in 26 files; the pins refreshed
    with no diff; Playwright (Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig) es-e01…e06, e08, es-observatory, es-radio, es-narrow and
    es-browsers, 72 passed; `npm run test:scripts` 59 passed.

## Brazilian Portuguese: the folder, lints and shell (PT0)

The first front of Brazilian Portuguese (pt-BR), plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT0" in the research root,
following SC8's "Adding a language". Portuguese stays hidden (user decision 5 of 2026-11-09): only a `?lang=pt-BR` preview reaches it;
no browser, stored choice or selector offers it until PT-REL. Its translations are web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's.

**The registry line.** `{"tag":"pt-BR","dir":"pt-br","catalog":"pt-br.json","budgetFactor":1,"htmlLang":"pt-BR","pagePath":"pt-br",
"autonym":"Português (Brasil)","englishName":"Portuguese","match":["pt"],"released":false,"notices":{…}}`, no `help` yet. Once released,
every `pt` and `pt-*` browser (pt-PT, pt-AO…) opens pt-BR, Galician does not (plan §4, D1); `?lang=pt`, `?lang=pt-br` and `?lang=pt-PT` are
no tag. The notices read «Não foi possível carregar o português; o site é exibido em inglês. Recarregue para tentar de novo.» and «Não foi
possível carregar parte do texto em português; essa parte é exibida em inglês. Recarregue para tentar de novo.»; their English halves say
"Portuguese". `tests/fixtures/i18n/registry.ts` now takes this line instead of its stand-in.

**The folder** `src/i18n/pt-br/`, mirroring `src/i18n/es/`: `index.ts` (`PORTUGUESE`, the manifest the planner parses), `pt-br.json`,
`originals.json`, `maps/`, `glossary.json`, `identical.json`, `lint-allowed.json` and `pt-br.test.ts`.
- **Translated:** ES1's shell slice, the 130 catalog keys of `app.*`, `project.*`, `session.*`, `error.*`, `instructorMode.*`, every
  `*.nav`, the home and first steps, `demonstrationMode.banner`, `spectrum.tools`, `classLink.cancel` and `app.documentTitle` (1,452 English
  words); the complete maps `appShell`, `helpMenu`, `languageChoice` (the dialog; the options are SC8's autonyms), `projectLinks` and `tour`;
  the partial maps `objectXLab` (`nav`, `guideNav`), `polish` (`clusterGuide.nav`, `results.help`) and `variableStars` (`nav`), which the
  header menu shows; and the 10 VIREO captions the shell shows through `vireo()` (Help «Ajuda», Topics... «Tópicos...», Websites «Sites»,
  2MASS Catalog, About This Exercise..., Results Editors, Observational Results, Pulsar Results; Project CLEA and NVO kept). PT1 translates
  all 748 originals and fixes the VIREO menu glossary.
- **Style** (plan §3.1): «você» with the subjunctive-form commands («Clique», «Escolha», «Salve»), infinitive buttons («Salvar
  investigação», «Cancelar»), sentence case, “ ” quotes, Brazilian words and AO 1990 spelling (tela, arquivo, navegador, aba, celular,
  espectrômetro), the gerund («Carregando…»), the IAU OAE pt-BR astronomy terms (aglomerado, pulsares, desvio para o vermelho), VIREO's
  English name in brackets at its first mention in prose («Modo de demonstração (Demonstration Mode)»). The phone menu button is «Menu»
  (English "Tools"; the glossary's `Tools` row excepts it); the analyser's own sidebar nav (`spectrum.tools`) is «Ferramentas». English-style
  thousands separators stay on screen («até 1,000 medições»), decision 4 as the plan keeps it (D5).
- **How it was translated:** three Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`, from the English, the reviewed Spanish shown only as meaning
  context): the 130 keys (`scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge --lang pt-BR`), the eight maps with the 10 originals, and the glossary seed. The
  Opus implementer read every row and changed 11 strings (sentence case of «Prévia do observatório» and «Observatório virtual», the glossary
  names «Resultados de observação» and «Dados de observação», `spectrum.tools`, the radio group «Observatório de rádio» instead of
  «Radiobservatório», a fuller «diagrama HR de observação») and trimmed the glossary's forbidden list (below).
- **Glossary** (`glossary.json`, `pt` field, read by `glossaryFrom(…,'pt')`): the 122 terms and 144 names of the Spanish glossary
  re-targeted to Portuguese from the English (plan §3.2–3.3). Rows for areas still in English wait for their front; PT1 reviews the VIREO
  menu names before using them. Its `forbidden` rows are the term forms the glossary rejects («desvio ao vermelho», «enxame/cúmulo
  estelar», «carregue em»); the language-wide rules are the profile's.

**The `pt-BR` lint profile** (`src/i18n/lint/profiles.ts`, added beside SC4's five; SC9 adds Spanish's beside it). Latin script
(`script:null`), letter boundaries, Portuguese case folding, plural categories one/many/other with `many` optional.
- **Register «você»** (`REGISTER.pt`, «tu» expressible): the pronouns tu, teu, tua, contigo, vós, vosso, te and the enclitic «-te»; «o
  senhor»/«a senhora»; and **pair-aware tu-commands**: a sentence of the translation that starts with «Clica, Escolhe, Abre, Salva,
  Fecha…» is flagged only when the English sentence it translates (same index, or any when the counts differ) starts with a bare
  imperative, so «Salva a investigação.» for "Saves the investigation." passes. This needed one additive hook in `rules.ts`:
  `register.paired` (`PairedPattern`, a pattern read at sentence starts with an `english` condition).
- **Typography errors:** European Portuguese words (ecrã, ficheiro, utilizador, rato, telemóvel, definições, palavra-passe, aceder,
  equipa, facto, contacto, registo, descarregar, premir, sítio…), pre-1990 spellings (acção, objecto, actual, exacto…), European accents
  (astronómico, espectrómetro, fotómetro, génio, protão, electrão…), «estar a» + infinitive and a label starting «A» + infinitive (the
  gerund instead), Spanish words and characters left from the reference (usted, también, pantalla, ventana, botón, archivo, cúmulo,
  estrella, medición, púlsar, el, con, del, una; «los»/«las» only as words, since «salvá-las» is Portuguese; ñ ¿ ¡), « » outside the lint's
  own «A1» samples, and title case (three capitalised words in a row outside brackets, so VIREO's English names in brackets keep theirs).
  Straight `"` is a wrong quote. Claims: «tradução oficial», «tradução do CLEA», «texto é do CLEA»; denials («não é uma tradução oficial»,
  «não é do CLEA») pass.
- **Not checked for a Latin language:** the `script` and `leftover-english` rules read the language's own script, so they never fire for
  pt-BR (rules.test.ts now skips their fixtures for a `script:null` profile, as it skipped `purity`). In their place (PT0 review) the
  profile's typography has an **English word** pattern: English function words that are no Portuguese word (the, and, with, your, this,
  is, not, of, to…) outside brackets and “ ” quotes. The European-word pattern also sets brackets and quotes aside, so a VIREO English
  name in brackets («Fator de criação do campo (Field Creation Factor)») is not read as European «factor».
- **Fixtures** (`fixtures.ts`, `ptBR`): 37 (35, plus the English word and the bracketed gloss of the PT0 review), a good and a bad string per rule, the register both ways (tu-command paired with an English
  command, «Salva a investigação» as a description, the flipped «tu» register), each European, Spanish and title-case pattern.
- **Calibration** on the 130 shell keys (grapheme ratio, English over 12 graphemes, 113 strings): minimum 0.84, 5th percentile 0.94, median
  1.13, 95th percentile 1.54, maximum 1.81 (`db.nav` «Banco de dados de aglomerados»); against Spanish, median 0.96. The band stays 0.7–1.6
  (reported, not failed); the strings above it are short menu names and messages that carry VIREO's English names in brackets.
- `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`: the registry line, a valid partial catalog covering the 130 shell keys, map shapes (complete for the
  five shell maps, partial for the three), normalised originals that Spanish also translates, and `lintPairs` over every string with the
  profile, the glossary, `identical.json` and `lint-allowed.json` (no errors; reports printed).

**The selector's keyboard trap** (SC8 review). On Chromium and Firefox for Windows and Linux an arrow key on the closed `<select>` changes
its value and fires `change` at once, so the confirmation opened on the first arrow and a keyboard user could not reach the option after
the next one; with three options on the pt preview that mattered. `src/components/LanguageChoice.tsx` now treats a `change` that follows
an arrow, Home, End, Page Up/Down or a typed letter on the select (within 400 ms; a pointer press, leaving the select and every dialog
reset it) as a step: the select shows the option as pending and nothing opens. Enter asks for it, leaving the select (Tab, a click
elsewhere, while the page has focus) asks for it, and Escape returns to the current language. A pick from the open list (mouse, touch,
macOS's keyboard list) asks at once, as before. The dialog, the not-stored and preview notes, and the effect-based loading in
`LanguageChoiceSlot` are unchanged; no text was added (so no Spanish map changed). The IME audit pin lists the select's Enter handler
("no text field"). Selector chunk: 4,332 → 5,006 B raw (5,130 after the review's focus return).

**Tests.**
- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: each translated language's catalog, originals and shell maps from its own folder, its letters, its preview
  query, its notices from the registry, and `text(english)` (English for a partial language); it throws when a registered language has no
  entry. Later PT fronts add the maps of their areas.
- `es-shell.spec.ts` → `shell.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over the languages: the Spanish tests keep their titles and assertions (their
  hard-coded Spanish replaced by the same strings read from the maps and originals), and pt-BR runs the same ten under its own titles in
  its preview. The language select's option texts (autonyms, never translated; a preview lists one more) are left out of the "differs from
  English" comparison; every other option in the shell regions is still compared.
- `es-areas.spec.ts` → `areas.spec.ts`: the Spanish tests unchanged, plus "a partial language fetches only its core, and no chunk for an
  empty area": in an en-US browser, `?lang=pt-BR` on the home page fetches only `core`, and each of the 11 area routes fetches exactly the
  non-empty modules of pt-BR's plan, nothing for an empty area, with English route text, no notice, no route error and no read before its
  area. With PT0 the `photometer` area holds two keys (`project.photometryCleared`, `project.photometryRestored`, rendered only by the
  photometer) and the seven others are empty.
- `language-selector.spec.ts`: pt-BR, pt and pt-PT browsers and a stored `pt-BR` get English with English and Español only; `?lang=pt-BR`
  gets Portuguese and the three options with their `lang`; a change there stores nothing and travels as `?lang=`; and the keyboard test
  (arrows, Home and End across all three options without the dialog, Enter, Escape, Tab, a list pick).
- `exports-identity.spec.ts`, the one-time generalisation (plan §1.2 #15): `Locale` gains `pt-BR`, `<html lang>` and the "the language's
  letters reached the file" check come from the language (`/[ãõçâêôàáéíóú]/i` for pt), `N()` reads a pt text from its catalog (English
  where the preview has none). The toolbar Save investigation family now runs in English, qps, Spanish and pt-BR (its title says so):
  the same name and SHA-256, and no Spanish or Portuguese letter in any of the files.
- `tests/fixtures/language.ts`: `contextLocale`/`contextQuery` follow `released` (a language before its release: en-US and `?lang=<tag>`).
- `locale.test.ts`: pt rows before and after release; `languages.test.ts` pins the registry as `[es-419 released, pt-BR not]`.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`). pt-BR: 2 chunks, 8.3 KB gzip — core 8.0 KB gzip (23.5 KB raw), photometer 0.3 KB, seven
areas "empty: no text yet, no chunk". Spanish: the core, the 12 area and shared chunks and the 38 help chunks are byte-identical to main
ce98d48's. English against ce98d48: `index.html` lists the same 80 files and every CSS file is byte-identical; the files it loads grow by
581 B raw and 186 B gzip (the shared chunk with the registry JSON +432 B for the pt-BR line, the entry +149 B for its loader); the other
changed files differ only in the imported chunk names.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pt0` from main ce98d48). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n` (with the pt test and
the lint fixtures), `src/help`, `src/seo`, `src/components` and `src/theme/scripts.test.ts`: 712 tests; the pins refreshed (only the IME
audit's new select line); `npm run build` with every check; Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig): `shell` 20 tests,
`areas` 20, `language-selector` 10, `exports-identity` 21, `es-observatory` 11, and the two keyboard tests of `language-selector` in
Firefox too; `npm run test:scripts`, 59 tests; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/`, 51 tests. On `dist/` served from loopback, an es-MX browser
without `?lang=` opened Spanish, a pt-BR and an en-US browser English (English and Español offered), and `?lang=pt-BR` Portuguese with
the three options. No failures.

**Independent review** (2026-11-10). Eight strings corrected: `error.configure` «antes de registrar» (a measurement is recorded, not
written), `error.storedDomain` «é arredondado … não pode manter», `classLink.cancel` «Cancelar o salvamento ou a restauração pendente»,
`session.restoreError` «… ou tamanho inválidos», `project.help` «da semente e dos eventos», `instructorMode.banner.leaveNote` «o VIREO não
tem essa ação», the dialog's `lost` «… se perde» and the tour's «o menu fica no botão Menu»; the stale `identical.json` row for
`appShell.toolsMenu` (its English is "Tools", so it never equalled it) removed. The lint gained the English-word pattern and the bracket
set-aside above. The shell spec's exclusion is narrowed to the language select's options. The selector's dialog, when it opened because
the select was left (Tab), returned the focus to the body in Chromium on Cancel or Escape (it opened while the focus was between
elements); closing it now puts the focus back on the select whenever it would be lost, and the keyboard test checks it.

**Left for later fronts.** PT1: the observatory and all 748 originals, `vireoSites` strict for pt-BR, the VIREO menu names of the glossary.
PT2–PT6: their areas, specs (`es-e0x` → `e0x.spec.ts`) and export families in pt-BR. PT-HELP: help files and `help`. PT-REL: `released`,
pages smoke and script walk rows, the trilingual 404. `tests/fixtures/layoutProblems.ts` still names `es-shell` in a comment (SC9's file).

## Brazilian Portuguese: the observatory and VIREO's names (PT1)

The second Portuguese front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT1" in the research root, with ES2 as its Spanish
model. Portuguese stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=pt-BR` reaches it, and pt-BR, pt and pt-PT browsers still open English.
The translations are web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** ES2's 546 observatory keys (`sky`, `optical`, `motion`, `horizon`, `siteClock`, `runningClock`, `scopeAccess`,
  `instrumentAccess`, `instrumentParameters`, `cameraParameters`, `cameraInfo`, `cameraPrepare`, `palette`, `testMode`,
  `demonstrationMode`, `optionsFile`, `photometerTab`, `htl`, `multispec`, `nativeMirror`, `notes.summary`, and the guide keys the
  observatory files render: `classLab`, `pleiadesLab`, `pleiadesPoint`, `ph`…) and seven observatory keys added since ES2
  (`sky.m31Adaptation`, `hubblePoint.elsewhere/exact/htl`, the three `hubbleHtl.reason.*`): 553 keys, about 8,000 English words.
  `pt-br.json` now holds 683 keys.
- **Maps, complete:** `observatory`, `fieldInfo`, `fieldReadDetails`, `nakedEye`, `qsoList`, `sound`, `observationMetadata`, `polish`
  (partial since PT0) and `camera`. The plan put `camera` in PT6, but the observatory route shows the CCD/IR card, and the walk below
  found its text in English; ES2 had completed it for the same reason.
- **All of VIREO's originals at once:** the 748 keys of Spanish's `originals.json` and the 14 that Spanish keeps identical with an
  `original:` reason (760 distinct texts: NVO and Project CLEA are in both). 742 of them are in `src/i18n/pt-br/originals.json`, now
  744 entries with the two below; 18 equal the English in Portuguese and carry an `identical.json` reason instead (OK, Longitude:, Latitude:, Altitude:, Zoom, Pulsar, Item, Cursor, Angstroms, Dec:, Min, Min:, HH:MM:SS,
  PEpoch (MJD):, V - Mv =…). Two originals have no Spanish entry because Spanish's word equals VIREO's and their `vireo()` site is
  one the source guard cannot resolve: the options dialog's `&General` tab («Geral») and the Site Information longitude letter `E`
  («L», Leste). `pt-br.test.ts` lists them (`PT_ONLY_ORIGINALS`) and the observatory spec no longer accepts them as identical in pt-BR.
- **Fragments** keep the join spaces VIREO's composition needs («O arquivo », « já existe.»); `fragment|Meter Radio Telescope` carries
  `{diameter}` («Radiotelescópio de {diameter} metros», as Spanish, with a `placeholders` allowance). Two quote fragments keep a straight
  `"` because VIREO joins them with an untranslated straight quote (`" Not Found.` and the photometer's "readings for \""); they have a
  `quotes` allowance with that reason. Every other VIREO message uses “ ”.

**How it was translated.** Ten Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English, with the reviewed Spanish only as a meaning
reference and a style brief (register, Brazilian vocabulary and spelling, the IAU OAE pt-BR terms, the fixed VIREO names below): three
catalog batches (243, 216 and 94 keys, merged with `scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge --lang pt-BR`), three originals batches (250 each),
and four map batches (observatory; fieldInfo and qsoList; polish; the four small maps with the third catalog batch; then camera). The
Opus implementer read every row and corrected 13 catalog strings, 22 originals and 14 map strings (49 in all), plus the two added
originals and three glossary rows. The corrections: one term for "telescope view" («visão do telescópio», never «vista»); CLEA is
masculine («do CLEA», as PT0 wrote it); Comma Delimit is «Separado por vírgulas» everywhere (the menu item, the file-type filter and
the web notes); VIREO's quoted button names in “ ”; «Parâmetros do pulsar» (one pulsar's dialog); «(secundário)»/«receptor principal»
for VIREO's slave/master receivers; «Aluno» for the student listing; «mesa de laboratório» for VIREO's Lab Table everywhere; the GCVS
item without a title-case catalogue name; «Imprimir campo de seleção»; VIREO's quoted English messages in the Multi-Spec reason
translated; «Efeito imediato aqui» for every live instructor note; the camera prep's «Filtro {filter}: catálogo {catalog}, banda {band}»
and «Câmera CCD/IR»; and three title-case or doubled-glyph glosses.

**The VIREO menu glossary (pt-BR).** PT0's Sonnet-seeded names were reviewed against Windows pt-BR and the Spanish ES2 decisions; the
`glossary.json` `names` (now 154) hold one Portuguese form per menu, dialog caption and button of the observatory, and the later
fronts reuse them. Main forms: File › Arquivo, Slew › Mover, Tools › Ferramentas, Help › Ajuda, Set Coordinates › Definir coordenadas,
Hot List › lista de objetos, View/Select from List › Ver/selecionar da lista, Add Current Item/Position to List › Adicionar o objeto ou
a posição atual à lista, Slew to Selection › Mover para a seleção, Field Information › Informações do campo, Print Sky Image ›
Imprimir imagem do céu, Exit Observatory › Sair do observatório, Set Date/Time › Definir data/hora, Site Parameters › Parâmetros do
local, Request Time › Solicitar tempo, Optical Simulation Options › Opções de simulação óptica, Limit Slewing › Limitar movimento,
Restrict Scope Access › Restringir acesso aos telescópios, Edit Mode › Modo de edição, Virtual Observatory › Observatório virtual,
Multi-Channel Spectrometer › Espectrômetro multicanal, OK › OK (Windows pt-BR), Cancel › Cancelar, Close › Fechar, Reset › Redefinir.
- **Changed after review:** Multi-Spec Init › «Preparar» (was «Inicializar», too close to Go › «Iniciar»; Spanish chose
  «Preparar»/«Comenzar» for the same reason); Pulsar Parameters › «Parâmetros do pulsar»; the term "standard pair" accepts the singular
  («par de padrões»). Kept after review: Scan › «Varrer» (the Multi-Spec spectra list's Scan steps through the spectra at Scan Rate
  › «Taxa de varredura»; Brazilian technical usage «varrer a lista», see the review below), Display ›
  «Exibir», Search › «Pesquisar», Find › «Localizar», Finder › «buscador», hot list › «lista de objetos».
- **Added:** Spectral Range › Faixa espectral, Spectrum Information › Informações do espectro, View/Save Spectra › Ver/salvar espectros,
  Select Objects › Selecionar objetos, Print Selection Field › Imprimir campo de seleção, Save Text › Salvar texto, Comma Delimit ›
  Separado por vírgulas, Exposure Timer › Temporizador de exposição, Find Text String › Localizar texto, Close Multi-Spec › Fechar
  Multi-Spec.
- **In prose**, as in Spanish: the Portuguese name with VIREO's English in parentheses at its first mention («Limitar movimento (Limit
  Slewing)»), menu paths glossed per segment, square brackets for a gloss inside parentheses.
- Two lint allowances keep proper names in English: the National Science Foundation (About credits) and the Two Micron All Sky Survey
  (the IR camera citation), each a `typography` allowance with its reason.

**`vireoSites` strict for pt-BR.** `src/i18n/es/vireoSites.test.ts` moved to `src/i18n/vireoSites.test.ts` (`git mv`) and runs once per
strict language, Spanish and pt-BR (`STRICT`): every resolvable `vireo()` literal needs an original or an `original:` reason in that
language. The site scan is shared; `UNRESOLVED_SITES` is still 140 (rerun, not edited). A later language joins once it has
translated VIREO's originals.

**Tests.**
- `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`: the observatory catalog keys present (by their prefixes), the nine maps complete, every VIREO original
  translated or kept with a reason (the 760, plus the two pt-only), and the pt-BR profile lint over all 683 keys, 15 maps and 744 originals
  (no errors; length reports printed for the reviewer, mostly short VIREO captions such as «Diferenciar maiúsculas de minúsculas»).
- `tests/i18n/es-observatory.spec.ts` → `tests/i18n/observatory.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over `TRANSLATED`. The 11 Spanish tests keep
  their titles and assertions (the Spanish literals now read from `languageText.ts`; `npx playwright test --list` shows the same 11
  titles); pt-BR runs the same nine checks under its own titles in its preview; English and qps run once. Portuguese words that equal
  English (OK, Longitude, Zoom, Multi-Spec…) are a per-language `SAME_IN`, and `NOT_SAME_IN` removes General and E from the shared SAME
  for pt-BR. `MULTISPEC_KNOWN` is unchanged; `knownFor(L)` rebuilds its entries for another language by replacing the Spanish
  `sky.supportedGalaxyLabel` with the language's, so removing an entry (Front SKY-LABELS) removes it for every language.
- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: each language's `observatoryMaps` (the View original test's web text).
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the HTL save, the options file (`VEO_OPTS.json`) and File › Field Information (Star Data List
  Save Text and Comma Delimit, QSO List Save Text) also run in the pt-BR preview: same names and SHA-256 as English, no Portuguese
  letter in the files. `N()` for pt-BR handles a button whose English ends in “ ↗” as Spanish's does.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main a00cf1b built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). pt-BR: 4
chunks, 63.4 KB gzip — core 60.1 KB gzip (192.3 KB raw; 8.0 KB in PT0), photometer 1.8 KB, fits 1.1 KB, classification 0.5 KB; five
areas still empty. Spanish: every chunk byte-identical, including the core (`i18n-es-419-core-CSIJ8K8S.js`, 104.4 KB gzip). English:
every file identical except the entry `index.js`, +2 bytes: the pt-BR core's preload list gains the `observatoryMenuLiterals` chunk it now
imports (as Spanish's does); nothing changes for an English load.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pt1` from main a00cf1b). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`
and `src/components`: 754 tests in 33 files; the pins refreshed (only `dynamic-key-sites.json`, two pt-BR map heads like Spanish's);
`npm run build` with every check; Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig): `observatory` 20, `shell`, `areas` and
`language-selector` 50, `exports-identity` 21; `npm run test:scripts` 59; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` 51. On `dist/` served from
loopback, pt-BR, pt-PT and en-US browsers without `?lang=` opened English with English and Español offered, es-MX Spanish, and
`?lang=pt-BR` Portuguese with three options. The 320 px screenshots of the options dialog, Site Parameters and the Multi-Spec windows
were inspected. No failures.

**Independent review (PT1).** Every string this front adds was read against the English (the 553 catalog keys, the nine maps and
the 744 originals, with Spanish beside them). The translation holds: você throughout, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990 spelling,
the IAU OAE terms, “ ” quotes, VIREO's `...` and the fragments' join spaces kept, no claim to be CLEA's. Corrected: 15 strings (8
originals, 4 catalog keys, 3 map strings) and 3 glossary rows:
- **One form per VIREO name.** Find › «Localizar» everywhere: Find Pulsar... › «Localizar pulsar...» and Find Pulsar Data › «Localizar
  dados de pulsar» (were «Buscar…», the only two; glossary row changed). Show › «Mostrar» as in Show Object ID and Show Help Topics:
  Show Spectrum › «Mostrar espectro» (was «Exibir», the Display menu's word). VIREO's Telescope Display is «exibição do telescópio»
  in the Multi-Spec catalog keys, so the Multi-Spec click hint says so too (was «tela do telescópio»).
- **Program List** › «Lista do programa...» (was «Lista de programas», a list of software programs; VIREO's list holds the program
  objects of the classification exercise), with the glossary row, the classification guide step and the toolbar help.
- **Sentence case.** Record/Review › «Registrar/revisar», as «Ver/salvar», «Adicionar/editar» and «Exibir/imprimir» (glossary, the
  original, two observatory option notes and the session limit message); the photometer's results hint «Veja, imprima ou salve a lista
  em…» (was «Ver/Imprimir/Salvar lista em…», title case mid-sentence); the Edit Mode warning writes «Modo de edição» as every other
  string does (was lower case twice); the QSO print note quotes VIREO's «(não registrado)» as its original translation reads.
- The CCD parameters note says «Singles» as the photometer note does.
- **Allowances narrowed:** the two `typography` allowances (National Science Foundation, Two Micron All Sky Survey) were whole-rule,
  which also switched off the European, Spanish and English word checks in those strings; they now allow only that title-case run
  (`words`). The two `quotes` allowances stay (that rule's only pt-BR finding is the straight quote they need).
- **Spec:** the Multi-Spec walk reads its labels by key again (`lang.catalog[key]`, as the Spanish walk read `es[key]`), not by the
  reverse English lookup, which picks the last of two keys that share an English text.

Glossary judgments kept: Init › «Preparar» (Go is «Iniciar»; Spanish chose the same pair), Display › «Exibir» (Windows pt-BR's View
menu), Scan › «Varrer» (it steps through the spectra list; «varrer a lista» is ordinary Brazilian technical usage and matches Scan
Rate › «Taxa de varredura»), the longitude letter E › «L» (Brazilian compass letters N, S, L, O; W is «O»), Hot List › «lista de
objetos», Pulsar Parameters › «Parâmetros do pulsar». The 18 identical originals are the same words in Portuguese or formats; the two
pt-only originals (General › «Geral», E › «L») are on screen in the observatory walk. The `N()` change in the identity spec applies to
pt-BR only and keeps file names and SHA-256 equal to English; Spanish's branch is unchanged. The 11 Spanish observatory tests keep
their titles, steps and assertions; `knownFor(L)` only swaps the label text inside each entry, so a missing or extra problem still
fails. Review verification: the commands above on the reviewed head, with the same counts and no failures; on the built `dist/`,
pt-BR, pt-PT, pt and en-US browsers opened English and fetched no pt chunk, es-MX opened Spanish, and `?lang=pt-BR` Portuguese with
only the pt-BR core fetched.

**Left for later fronts.** PT2–PT6: the exercise areas and their specs; their guide keys in the core. PT-HELP: the help files, including
the exit reminder (Reminder.obs). `tests/i18n/es-e04.spec.ts` still names `es-observatory.spec.ts` in a comment (PT4 converts it).

## Brazilian Portuguese: E01 Hubble and E02 large-scale structure (PT2)

The third Portuguese front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT2" in the research root, with ES3 and ES4 as its
Spanish models. Portuguese stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=pt-BR` reaches it. The translations are web adaptations by
OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English. VIREO's own captions in these screens (Observational Data,
Measured Redshift Data, the Spectral Range dialog, the engine's Zoom, the wedge window and LSS Galaxy Data) were already translated
in PT1's originals, so this front adds web text only.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** 781 keys (about 10,300 English words), every key of the prefixes `lab`, `lssLab`, `hubble`, `velocity`, `ownResults`,
  `spectrum`, `spectrometer`, `galaxyExposure`, `skyExposure`, `starExposure`, `ssp`, `exposureIO`, `drift`, `skyProfiles`,
  `fieldSpec`, `lss`, `wedge` and `continuity` (the acquisition archive and stellar alignment the analyser shows), and `classLink.open`
  («Classificar estrela adquirida», the star exposure's button that the E01 walk shows). `pt-br.json` now holds 1,464 keys. The pt-BR
  `exposure`, `lss` and `exposure+lss` modules are complete; the rest of these keys are core (the guides and the analyser).
- **Maps, complete:** `engineView`, `spectralRange` and `wedge` (`src/i18n/pt-br/maps/`).

**How it was translated.** Five Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`) for the catalog (94, 149, 121, 197 and 186 keys: the two
guides; Hubble, velocities and Observational Results; the analyser; the exposures, `.SSP`, drift and sky profiles; the field
spectrometer, the survey and the wedge) and one for the three maps, from the English, with the reviewed Spanish shown only as a
meaning reference and the PT1 style brief (você, infinitive buttons, sentence case, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990 spelling,
the IAU OAE pt-BR terms, VIREO's names from `originals.json` with the English in parentheses at their first mention). The Opus
implementer read every row and translated the 34 `continuity.*`/`classLink.open` keys itself (the walk found them in English).
Corrected after reading, 20 strings:
- VIREO's messages quoted in a reason are given in Portuguese in “ ”, as PT1's Multi-Spec reason does («o VIREO diz “Nenhuma estrela
  encontrada no campo!”», «“O índice espectral é inválido!” … “Você não pode registrar um espectro desta estrela!”»), not English
  with a gloss (four `lss.reason.*`/`fieldSpec.reason.*` strings).
- Download as a verb («Não foi possível baixar o arquivo», «os arquivos de texto/CSV que ela baixa», «os arquivos baixados»), reload
  as «recarregar» (`drift.cache-boundary`), status as «situação» everywhere (the LSS column, its help and the two guide lines that
  name the Hubble status column), provenance as «procedência» (`spectrum.scope`), Display level as «Nível de exibição», unsupported
  as «sem suporte» (`velocity.zero`), «medição feita com» (`fieldSpec.uncertified`), the full and H/K views as «visões» in the engine
  note (the analyser's buttons say «Visão»), the guide's «o painel Diagrama de Hubble e distâncias» (title case), one ellipsis in
  `lss.region.help` and `spectralRange.locked`, and a clearer `spectrum.observationNoTimeNote`.
- Term choices: survey › «levantamento», wedge › «cunha»/«diagrama em cunha», snapshot › «instantâneo», acquisition archive ›
  «acervo», template › «modelo», bin › «canal», tick (the z step's) › «marca», journal › «diário», random draws › «sorteios»,
  committed time and counts › «acumulado», workbook › «planilha», recipe › «receita», fit through zero › «ajuste pela origem»,
  pointing › «apontamento», raw › «bruto/brutas», Sp.Type › «Tipo esp.».

**Glossary** (`glossary.json`, add-only except one row): six terms (survey › levantamento, except the "Two Micron All Sky Survey"
citation; snapshot › instantâne-; analyser › analisador; template › modelo; journal › diário; bin › canal/canais) and eight VIREO names
(Measured Redshift Data, Record Z, Save Data, Plot Data, LSS Wedge Plot, Load Saved Data, LSS Galaxy Data, Time-Dependent Data), with
Save Data's two other VIREO captions excepted. **Changed:** the existing `raw` row now reads `brut[oa]s?` (was `bruto`), so «contagens
brutas» agrees in gender. `identical.json` gains 23 rows (symbols, units, formats; «quasar», «bits» and «Pixel (x, y)» are the same
words in Portuguese).

**Tests.**
- `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`: the three maps registered and complete, a PT2 coverage check (every key of the prefixes above), and
  the profile lint over 1,464 keys, 22 maps and the originals (no errors).
- `tests/i18n/es-e01.spec.ts` → `e01.spec.ts` and `es-e02.spec.ts` → `e02.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over `TRANSLATED` as PT1 did:
  the 18 Spanish tests keep their titles, steps and assertions (`npx playwright test --list` shows the same 18 titles), with their
  Spanish read from `languageText.ts` (`L.catalog`, `L.originals`, `e01e02Maps(tag)`); pt-BR runs the same 15 checks under its own
  titles in its preview; English and qps run once. Per-language identicals: `SAME_IN` (pt-BR: OK, Zoom, `v · km/s · float32`, Pixel (x,
  y); Spanish's Observable and No moved there from E02's shared list) and one pt pattern for the measurement log's «bits 3c23d70a». The
  field selector check reads « (N galáxias)» from `lss.fieldOption`; the M31 test takes the telescope button and VIREO's message from
  the language's originals. The layout tests' `ES3_LAYOUT_LANG`/`ES4_LAYOUT_LANG` still switch the walk to `en` or `qps`.
- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: `E01_E02_MAPS`/`e01e02Maps(tag)` (engineView, spectralRange, wedge, observatory, polish per language).
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the E01 and E02 families (measurement CSV, velocities CSV and session; Observational Data with
  results text/CSV, acquired SSP and analysis spectrum; Hubble CSV and session; star `.SSP`; galaxy, sky and star counts CSV and
  session JSON; field spectrometer CSV and session; the LSS counts, session, SSP, results CSV and TXT, investigation, wedge CSV and
  LSS Galaxy Data TXT) also run in pt-BR: same names and SHA-256 as English, no Portuguese letter in the files. `N()` for pt-BR knows the
  observatory map's Save spectrum (.SSP); `ptStart()` matches Record measurement and its glyph.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`; compared with main 62f8763 built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). pt-BR: 7 chunks, 100.6 KB gzip —
core 79.2 KB gzip (253.8 KB raw; 60.1 KB in PT1), exposure 8.1 KB, lss 8.7 KB, exposure+lss 1.3 KB, photometer 1.8 KB, fits 1.1 KB,
classification 0.5 KB; cluster, pulsar and radio still empty. Spanish: all 13 chunks byte-identical. English: every other file
identical after normalising chunk hashes (only names change, as the pt chunk names enter the loaders).

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pt2` from main 62f8763). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`,
`src/seo` and `src/components`: 755 tests in 33 files; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check; Playwright
(dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig): `e01` and `e02` 33, `observatory` and `shell` 40, `exports-identity` 21; `npm run
test:scripts` 59; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` 51. No failures. The 320 px screenshots of the guides, Observational Data, Measured Redshift Data and
the wedge in pt-BR were inspected.

**Overlap with PT3 (E03).** `continuity.*` and `classLink.open` are core keys that the E03 screens may render too; PT2 translated them
because the E01 walk shows them. The other analyser keys of the classification module (`classLink`, `classImport`, `probe`…) are PT3's.

**Independent review (PT2).** Every string this front adds was read against the English (the 781 catalog keys and the three maps,
with Spanish beside them). The translation holds: você throughout, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990 spelling, «desvio para o
vermelho» as PT1 fixed it, VIREO's names as `originals.json` gives them (Registrar Z, Dados de desvio para o vermelho medidos, Diagrama
em cunha LSS, Mostrar ID do objeto…), “ ” quotes, every placeholder, the decimal point, no claim to be CLEA's. Corrected: 4 strings:
- `spectrum.observationNoTimeNote`: «o registro reutilizado por este registro (Record)» read as a record reusing itself; now «o registro
  reutilizado não tem hora de observação…», as the Spanish says it.
- `galaxyExposure.engineAdaptation`: «mesclagem» › «fusão com o catálogo de QSOs», the word every other merge string uses.
- `ssp.exportHelp`: «equivalência … do tempo de execução original» (runtime as a duration) › «com a execução original», as
  `lab.next.interpretSupported` says it.
- `lss.adaptation.raster`: «um raster de navegador declarado, de centro mais próximo» › «uma rasterização declarada do navegador
  (centro de pixel mais próximo)».

Judged and kept: `wedge.routeTitle` «Cunha da estrutura em grande escala» (the route's title; «cunha» is the front's word for the
wedge, as Spanish «Cuña»); «tique» for the 1-second timer tick (PT1's word in `testMode.adaptation` and the instrument parameters;
the analyser's z-step tick is «marca», a different thing); the literal technical notes (`lab.limitsExtended`, `lss.adaptation.*`)
read as Portuguese and keep every claim; «Access violation … in module ‘CLEA_VEO.exe’» stays English with a gloss, since it is the
Windows runtime's text, not one of VIREO's strings (`originals.json` has no entry for it). The `\braw\b` row now accepts only the four
forms of «bruto»; each new `identical.json` row is a symbol, unit, format or the same word. Spec: the LSS fields file error test
again requires a translated original (or a word the language keeps, SAME_IN) instead of accepting vireo()'s English fallback; the
other e01/e02 changes keep every Spanish assertion. Hidden: a built `dist/` served on loopback gives English and fetches no pt chunk
for pt-BR, pt-PT and en-US browsers; `?lang=pt-BR` fetches only the pt core and the route's modules. PT3 (worktree at ae086c6) also
adds `continuity.restoreExposure`, `ssp.header` and `classLink.open`, with the same Portuguese; both fronts add a `snapshot` glossary
row (keep one when merging).

## Brazilian Portuguese: E03 spectral classification (PT3)

The third Portuguese front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT3" in the research root, with ES5 as its Spanish
model; it ran beside PT2 (E01 and E02). Portuguese stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=pt-BR` reaches it. The translations are
web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English.

**What is translated.** 742 catalog keys (about 10,860 English words), chosen from the Spanish split plan rather than by prefix: every
key of the `classification` module (691) and of `classification+cluster` (9), and the 42 core keys E03 renders (`classLab.*` left by
PT1, `class.*`, `classLink.*`, `classImport.*`, `combined.regionPolicy`, `probe.*`, `continuity.restoreExposure` and `ssp.header`,
which `ClassificationLab.tsx` renders). They cover ES5's list: the classification route and its guide, the classification tools (atlas
and standard pairs, Standard − practice, the line table, the wavelength window and range dialog, the four renderings with their reverse
options, the normalized sampled difference, the sampled flux readout, the comparison cursor, the manual equivalent width and its cursor,
comparison marks, the Program List dialog and the local `.LST` import, an imported spectrum, an acquired star), the observation
