notebook with its edit, delete and recovery dialogs, and the DAT and TXT/CSV inspectors. Every E03 file and prefix is at 100 %
(`node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang pt-BR`); `pt-br.json` now holds 1,425 keys. No map was needed: `observationMetadata`, the only
map of the classification module, was completed by PT1, and VIREO's E03 names (Program List..., Next on List, No More Spectra, Spectral
Line Table, Observational Data and its six field captions) were among PT1's 744 originals.
- `nativeResults.number.infinity` is rendered by the cluster HR tools too, so the `cluster` area of pt-BR now has a 0.2 KB chunk (found by
  its name only, with the build's warning, as a partial language's module without a marker).
- **Identical to English** (`identical.json`, the Spanish reasons): the placeholder-only formats `classLines.option`, `photo.caption`,
  `viewport.range`, `nativeResults.typeValue`, `nativeResults.kindValue`, `program.row`, `nativeText.choice`, the hash name
  `probe.shaLabel`, and the Delphi type names "Single · 32 bits" and "Double · 64 bits" ("Extended · 80 bits armazenados" is
  translated, as in Spanish).

**How it was translated.** Four Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English with the reviewed Spanish as a meaning reference
only, under PT1's style brief (você and its commands, infinitive buttons, sentence case, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990 spelling,
the IAU OAE pt-BR terms, “ ” quotes, decimal point) and the glossary with the pt-BR strings already on screen: the lab, its guide and
line table (193 keys); the DAT and TXT/CSV inspectors (202); the notebook and the shared keys (175); the width measurement, its cursor,
the Program List, the sampled flux, the trace, range, photo and difference displays (172). They were merged with `scripts/i18n-batch.mjs
merge --lang pt-BR`. The Opus implementer read every row against the English and corrected 47 strings (6 %):
- **VIREO names in prose** with VIREO's English at the first mention, as ES5 does: the guide's intro (Mostrar ID do objeto (Show Object
  ID), Mostrar mag. do objeto (Show Object Mag), Opções de simulação óptica (Optical Simulation Options), the boxes Objeto (Object) and
  Mag. aparente (V) [App. Mag (V)], Classificar (Classify), Dados de observação (Observational Data)), `class.limit` and
  `class.guide.compare` (Classificar (Classify), where the batch had «a classificação»), Opções (Options) in the two Time-Dependent Data
  notes, Próximo da lista (Next on List) and Lista do programa (Program List) in `programImport.unbound` (the batch had «Próximo»), and
  the menu path in `obs.reuse` in square brackets inside the sentence, as Spanish writes it.
- **One form each:** the notebook's row buttons «Editar/Excluir resultado de observação {id}» (as Spanish's «resultado de observación»;
  the card stays «Resultados do caderno»); «Instante da observação (JD)» for `obs.time`, VIREO's caption (the batch had «Horário»,
  ES5's review made the same alignment); «visualização combinada» (never «vista», PT1's rule); «Inspecionar painéis de comparação»
  for the button and the help that names it; snapshot › «instantâneo» (one batch had «captura»); bins › «canais» (Spanish «canales»;
  the batch kept «bins»); a fresh bracket › «um novo conjunto de marcas» in both strings; «Definir faixa de comprimento de onda da
  classificação» for the range dialog and the guide that names it; grade › «nota» (`feature.limit` had «classe»); AR for right
  ascension in the TXT field names and the DAT ordering messages (PT1: VIREO "RA:" › «AR:»); «Esc» for the key, as PT0 writes it;
  «cópia de segurança» (two strings had «backups»); «Objeto X» in `range.help`; «Carga útil NaN».
- **Wording:** «o site não preenche / não seleciona / não escolhe … por você» for "for you" (three strings read as "by you");
  «histórico de classificação» for the full classification log (it was «registro», the same word as each record); «0–1» kept as the
  English glyph in `classImport.error`; «A inspeção em modo bruto»; «valores padrão do projeto»; «Uma entrada formada só por
  dois-pontos … Digite um tipo»; `reverseTrace.readOnly` names the checkbox «Inverter exibição dos traçados».

**Glossary** (`terms`, added): snapshot › instantâneo, combined view › visualização combinada, pane › painel, the E03 traces (sampled,
printed, printed-coordinate, intensity, unknown) › traçado (narrowed so the pulsar print's "trace" is not caught), readout › leitura.
ES5's E03 rows (standard › padrão, shoulder › ombro, grayscale › cinza, notebook › caderno, luminosity, atlas, standard pair, raw,
merge, endpoint grid) were already in PT0's glossary and hold for every new string. Other choices used in one form: practice ›
prática, unknown › desconhecido, feature › característica, trace › traçado, range › faixa, window › janela, zoom in/out › aproximar/
afastar, pan › deslocar, fallback › alternativa, owned bytes › retidos, replay › reprodução, Undo › Desfazer.

**The dist check across languages.** The Portuguese classification chunk holds «Campo de velocidade», «Importar espectro para
comparação» and «Cópia editada: {count} registros.», which contain the Spanish markers «Campo de velocidad», «Importar espectro» and
«{count} registros»; `check-i18n-dist.mjs` took them for Spanish text outside its chunks. Its rule that a marker may appear inside a
longer text of the chunk's own module held within one language only. It now holds across languages: another language's chunk (named
`i18n-<tag>-<module>-`) is never taken for this language's module, and carries this language's markers only inside its own module's
text; elsewhere (an English chunk, `index.html`) a marker still fails. `src/i18n/distCheck.test.ts` covers both ways with two fixture
languages. Later languages close to Spanish (and Portuguese itself, once French or Spanish share phrases with it) need this. The
review made the rule occurrence-based (below): each occurrence of the marker in the file must lie inside an occurrence of a text of that
chunk's module, not merely somewhere in the module's text.

**Tests.**
- `tests/i18n/es-e03.spec.ts` → `tests/i18n/e03.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over `TRANSLATED`. The six Spanish tests keep their titles,
  steps and assertions (`npx playwright test --list` shows the same six titles; the Spanish literals now read from `languageText.ts`);
  pt-BR runs the same five checks under its own titles in its preview; qps runs once. A partial language's catalog falls back to the
  English for keys of a later front (the star exposure buttons the walk passes through), a released one must have them. `SAME_IN` is
  empty for pt-BR: no Portuguese text of the walk equals the English outside the shared identifiers. The screenshots are named
  `<tag>-e03-<width>-<step>.png`.
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the E03 family (classification session, CSV, complete investigations, observation notebook and
  its edit recovery, unchanged and edited DAT, unchanged TXT, the generated native CSV and the imported spectrum) also runs in pt-BR: the
  same names and SHA-256 as English, no Portuguese letter in any file, and the flow's `h1` is the Portuguese title, so the family cannot
  pass by comparing English with English. Two existing lines changed: the notebook's Edit button for pt-BR and the family's locale list.
- `src/core/classificationProgram.test.ts` and `tests/i18n/classifyProgramLiterals.ts` name the renamed spec.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main 62f8763 built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). pt-BR: 6 chunks,
94.0 KB gzip — core 62.4 KB gzip (199.1 KB raw; 60.1 KB after PT1), classification 28.1 KB gzip (101.9 KB raw; Spanish's is 28.0),
classification+cluster 0.4, cluster 0.2, photometer 1.8, fits 1.1; pulsar, radio, LSS and exposure still empty. Spanish: every chunk
byte-identical, names included. English: every file identical once chunk hashes are normalised (the hashes move with the pt-BR chunk
names the loader imports).

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pt3` from main 62f8763). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`,
`src/components` and `src/core/classificationProgram.test.ts`: 762 tests in 34 files; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build`
with every check; Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig): `e03` 11; `observatory`, `shell` and `exports-identity`
61; `areas` and `language-selector` 30; `npm run test:scripts` 59; `npm run test:pages` on `dist/` 51. The pt-BR 320 px screenshots of the guide, the
width review and the notebook were inspected.

**Independent review (PT3).** Every string this front adds was read against the English, with Spanish beside it (the 742 keys, in two
halves by two Opus readers, then checked by the reviewer). The translation holds: você throughout, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990
spelling, the IAU OAE terms (tipo espectral, classe de luminosidade, sequência principal, linhas de absorção, largura equivalente),
PT1's VIREO names with VIREO's English at the first mention, “ ” quotes, placeholders and decimal points, no claim to be CLEA's; the
686 keys PT2 also holds read the same in both fronts. Corrected: 37 strings:
- **Meaning:** `combined.layout` "the lower atlas reference" › «a referência inferior do atlas» (was «o atlas de referência inferior»);
  "acquired journals / classification drafts" › «diários de aquisição», «… das aquisições» (three strings had them «adquiridos»);
  `combined.tracePolicy` round half to even › «ao inteiro mais próximo, com empate para o par» (as `sampledTrace.policy`; it read "to the
  nearest even"); "held input" › «a entrada em andamento» (was «mantida»); rescale › «reescalam» (was «redimensionam»); the DAT and TXT
  policies describe what is read («São lidos…», were commands «Leia…»); «Registro {index} selecionado no arquivo», «campos de texto
  selecionados do DAT»; the export error's «sem suporte» covers the encoding again; «A conversão do espaçamento para o CSV nativo».
- **One form each:** runtime › «ambiente de execução» (the DAT ordering, the MJD boundary, the JD unit; «tempo de execução» read as a time
  next to the time fields) and native-runtime equivalence › «equivalência com a execução nativa» (two strings; PT2's review chose
  «execução original»); source inventory › «inventário de origem» (one had «de fontes»); source identities › «identidades de origem» in
  `program.unavailable`; owned › «retenção» in the download error (was «posse»); the remarks field › «comentários» in the undo message
  (was «anotações»); «instante da observação» in `obs.metadataPolicy` and the DAT time field («Valor nativo…», was «Palavra nativa do
  horário»); previewing › «Pré-visualizar»; RA › «AR» in `nativeList.policy` (the last «RA»).
- **Grammar and wording:** «Texto das horas de AR» (was «dos»), «eram esperados exatamente dois campos», the undo messages end
  «… anteriores foram restaurados exatamente», «Nenhum … nota é calculado automaticamente», «Posição {position} de {total} na lista do
  programa», «para baixo da borda», «marcadores na borda ou fora dela», «exibidos como escapes», «começa na cauda», «As setas ou as teclas
  2/4/6/8 do teclado numérico», «Por padrão, deslizar o dedo rola a página», «fluxo amostrado da foto», «Inspecionar resultados nativos
  salvos em texto», «reimporte-o», «Carga útil de NaN … medição».

Judgments kept: «Impressão digital do inventário de origem» (fingerprint: «impressão digital» is pt-BR's established word for a hash or
key fingerprint, as GnuPG, OpenSSH and browser certificate viewers use it); «Carga útil de NaN» (payload › «carga útil», the standard
rendering; «de» makes it a NaN's payload); «perfil de cursor de cauda» (tail › «cauda», the usual Brazilian data-structure word, and it
matches «começa na cauda»).

**The dist check, reviewed.** The front's rule accepted a marker of another language anywhere in a foreign chunk whose module's text
contained it, so a stray Spanish «Campo de velocidad» or «{count} registros» written as a string of its own into the Portuguese chunk
passed. Now every occurrence of the marker must lie inside an occurrence of a text of the file's module (its catalog values, every
string its map sources write, and in the core the originals), in any of the minifier's three quoted forms; the same rule holds within
one language (it used the module's whole source text). `stringsOf` reads '…', "…" and the fixed parts of `…${x}…` templates, skipping
comments; the old whole-text match was needed only because the single-quoted literals missed the double-quoted and template maps.
`src/i18n/distCheck.test.ts` adds the stray copy beside its longer text, a file merely named like another language's chunk, and
`stringsOf`. Mutations on a copy of the real `dist/` (each one alone; the front's rule › the reviewed rule): a Spanish marker that is a
substring of a Portuguese text, as its own string in the Portuguese chunk, pass › fail (two cases); a whole Spanish string in the
Portuguese chunk, Spanish or Portuguese text in the English entry chunk or in `index.html`, Portuguese text in a Spanish chunk,
`index.html` preloading a Portuguese chunk, the entry importing one statically, the Spanish classification chunk appended to the
Portuguese one, and a fake `i18n-pt-BR-…` file holding Spanish: fail › fail; the unmutated build: pass › pass. The check takes the same
time (about 90 s). Review verification on the reviewed head: `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over the same files, 763 tests in 34 files; the pins
refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check (pt-BR 94.0 KB gzip in 6 chunks, `released:!1` in the bundle, no pt-BR page,
sitemap or hreflang entry); Playwright `e03` 11, `observatory`, `shell` and `exports-identity` 61; `npm run test:scripts` 59.

**Left for later fronts.** PT2: E01 and E02 (the star exposure buttons of the E03 walk are E01's). PT4–PT6 and PT-HELP: their areas, and
the classification help files behind the lab's Help button (CLASSHLP, SPECLNS) and the Spectral Line Table window's own text, as ES-HELP
did for Spanish.

## Brazilian Portuguese: E04 Pleiades and E05 clusters (PT4)

The fifth Portuguese front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT4" in the research root, with ES6 and ES7 as its
Spanish models; it ran beside PT5 (radio telescope). Portuguese stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=pt-BR` reaches it. The
translations are web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English. VIREO's own captions in these
screens (Record/Review and its boxes, Observational Data, Display/Print Data, Possible Cluster Member, Select Cluster Data...,
Clusters for Study, Load/Plot, View/Print Parameters, Variable Star Data, Simulated Light Curve, the Cluster Data dialog and its boxes,
the help viewer's captions) were already among PT1's originals, so this front adds web text only.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** 585 keys (about 8,600 English words), every key of the prefixes `ph` (the photometer route), `pa` (the Pleiades
  colour–magnitude analysis), `pleiadesLab` (the rest of the Pleiades guide), `nc` (the native cluster model fit), `db`, `ce` and
  `clusterLab` (the cluster reference database, its exclusions and guide), `ownHr` and `obsHr` (Data from Observations and the
  saved-observation HR tool), chosen as ES6 and ES7 scoped them; with the Spanish split plan they are the `photometer` (249),
  `cluster` (186) and `cluster+photometer` (107) modules and 43 core keys. Every E04 and E05 file and prefix is at 100 %
  (`node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang pt-BR`); `pt-br.json` now holds 2,788 keys.
- **Maps, complete:** `clusterHr` and `clusterData` (new, `src/i18n/pt-br/maps/`), and `variableStars` (partial since PT0, only `nav`).
- **Identical to English** (`identical.json`, Spanish's reasons where they apply): the band letters and symbols (`obsHr.blockedField.*`,
  `obsHr.ebv`, `obsHr.modulus`, `obsHr.colBV`, `obsHr.colIndex`, `nc.axisColor`, `db.columnEbv`), placeholder-only formats
  (`ph.fixed`, `ph.seriesSecondsOption`, `ce.inspectedInfo`, `pleiadesLab.sourceFile`, `variableStars.typeOption`), «magnitudes»,
  «Pixel (x, y)», SHA-256, `Dec (°)`, the empty action header, the em dash for no period and the help viewer's OK.
- **Lint allowances:** `ph.apertureReadout` (the placeholder `{factor}` is not the European word; the text says «fator») and the
  variable-star guide's search example «del Cep» (a GCVS star name, not the Spanish word).

**Glossary** (`glossary.json`). Terms added: metallicity › metalicidade, slider › controle deslizante, trial › teste («ajuste de
teste»), preset › predefini-, apparent modulus › módulo aparente, fresh sky › nova leitura do céu, integration series › série de
integração, zero-age main sequence › sequência principal de idade zero, turn-off › ponto de saída da sequência principal (the review's
choice, below), ly › anos-luz (or «a.l.»),
colour index › índice de cor. Names added (one Portuguese form per VIREO caption, as PT1's originals give them): Load/Plot ›
Carregar/plotar, View/Print Parameters › Ver/imprimir parâmetros, Display/Print Data › Exibir/imprimir dados, Select Cluster Data ›
Selecionar dados de aglomerados, Possible Cluster Member › Possível membro do aglomerado, Clusters for Study › Aglomerados para estudo,
Variable Star Data › Dados da estrela variável, Simulated Light Curve › Curva de luz simulada, Remove from HR Diagram › Remover do
diagrama HR, Recorded Results › Resultados registrados, Telescope has Moved! › O telescópio foi movido!, Reading Sky! › Leitura do céu!.
**Changed:** Data from Observations › «Dados a partir das observações» (PT0's seed «Dados das observações» read too close to
Observational Data › «Dados de observação», the reason ES6's review gave for «Datos a partir de observaciones»; no string used the old
form). Two candidate rows were tried and left out because they also matched other areas' good text: report (a verb as often as the
noun «relatório») and committed (PT1's motion strings). Other choices used in one form: plot › «plotar» (verb) and «gráfico» (noun),
report › «relatório», membership › «pertinência ao aglomerado», catalogue tiles › «blocos do catálogo», "port" › «versão web»,
pointing › «apontamento» for the bench button Use the telescope pointing (as PT2's field spectrometer) and «posição do telescópio» in
the photometer's descriptions (as PT1's access lines), press › «pressione» (the glossary's `press` row).

**How it was translated.** Five Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`) for the catalog (113 and 112 `ph` keys; `pa`, `pleiadesLab`
and `nc`, 107; `db`, `ce` and `clusterLab`, 127; `ownHr` and `obsHr`, 126) and one for the three maps, from the English, with the
reviewed Spanish shown only as a meaning reference, under PT1's style brief with this front's glossary rows and the Portuguese
already on screen for these areas (PT1's originals and the 63 `pleiadesLab` and `ph` keys PT1 translated). They were merged with
`scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge --lang pt-BR`. The Opus implementer read every row against the English and corrected 48 catalog
strings (8 %) and 11 map strings:
- **One form each:** fresh sky › «nova leitura do céu» everywhere (the batches mixed «céu novo», «um novo céu»; this also changed
  four earlier strings, below); the dataset button «Carregar o conjunto de dados selecionado» and every string that names it;
  «Usar o apontamento do telescópio» and its citations; one Portuguese for each repeated English («Cancelar leitura do arquivo»,
  «Tentar carregar de novo o modelo selecionado», «O que significa este progresso?»); "Hotlist {n}" › «Entrada {n} da lista de
  objetos»; membership › «pertinência ao aglomerado» (the batches had «participação», «pertencimento», «associação de membros»);
  merge › «fusão» for the catalogue merge (PT2's word); catalogue tiles › «blocos» (the map had «pedaços»).
- **VIREO names:** Limitar movimento (Limit Slewing) in `ph.tiledPointLimit` (left English), «catálogo de estrelas-guia (Guide Star
  Catalog)» in `ph.tiledHelp` and the Cluster Data report note (one had title case), «Resultados registrados (Recorded Results)» in
  round brackets outside a parenthesis, the Select Cluster Data gloss with one ellipsis, the `ownHr.adaptation` quote names the real
  button «Plotar meus registros de observação» (as ES7's Spanish does), the photometer's Data from Observations button «Plotar meus
  pares no diagrama HR de Dados a partir das observações».
- **Meaning and wording:** "port" › «versão web» (four strings kept the English loanword), «deixa um alvo indisponível» for
  "withholds" (was «retém»), «daria a volta» for a pixel position that would wrap (was «estouraria»), the observation HR intro «O site
  não ajusta, não preenche nem dá nota a nada por você» (it read "by you", as PT3's review found elsewhere), "Full investigation
  saves" as «Ao salvar a investigação completa…», «Exiba-a como a lista…» for "Show it as", «(0 dias)», «com empate para o par» for
  round half to even (PT3's wording), the bibliographic title of the 2008 manual in “ ” with its gloss, en dashes kept in ranges
  («1–5 repetições», «tamanhos 5–150»), «Diagrama cor-magnitude» with VIREO's hyphen in the print image name.

**Changed existing strings** (not add-only): `ph.saveHelp` and `pleiadesLab.next.measure` (PT1), `pleiadesLab.intro` (PT1) and
`project.photometryRestored` (PT0) now say «nova leitura do céu», the one form for "fresh sky"; the glossary's Data from Observations
row (above).

**Tests.**
- `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`: the two new maps and variableStars complete, a PT4 coverage check (every key of the nine prefixes),
  and the profile lint over 2,788 keys, 21 maps and the originals (no errors).
- `tests/i18n/es-e04.spec.ts` → `e04.spec.ts` and `es-e05.spec.ts` → `e05.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over `TRANSLATED` as PT1–PT3 did:
  the twelve Spanish tests keep their titles and assertions (`npx playwright test --list` shows the same twelve titles), with their
  Spanish read from `languageText.ts` (`L.catalog`, `L.originals`, `e04e05Maps(tag)`); pt-BR runs the same ten checks under its own

- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: `E04_E05_MAPS`/`e04e05Maps(tag)` (spectralRange, observatory, polish, camera, sound and clusterHr per

- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the E04 family (photometer session, Observational Results text and CSV, investigation) and the

  locale lists.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main 780ae37 built the same way). pt-BR: 10 chunks, 163.0 KB gzip —
core 88.3 KB gzip (283.5 KB raw; 82.1 KB on main: the 43 core keys, the clusterHr map and variableStars), photometer 13.5 KB (1.8 on
main), cluster 8.2 KB (0.2), cluster+photometer 5.1 KB (new; Spanish's three: 13.5, 8.2 and 5.0), classification 28.1, lss 8.7,
exposure 8.1, exposure+lss 1.3, fits 1.1, classification+cluster 0.4; pulsar and radio still empty. Spanish: all 13 chunks byte-identical, names included. English:
every other file identical once chunk hashes are normalised.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pt4` from main 780ae37). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`
and `src/components`: 758 tests in 33 files; the pins refreshed (only `dynamic-key-sites.json`: the pt-BR variableStars map's
`printCurveLayout` head, like Spanish's); `npm run build` with every check; Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig):
`e04` and `e05` 22 (a first run with two workers had two load timeouts, the Portuguese E04 comparison's English walk and the Spanish
E05 comparison, both of which passed when rerun alone, and showed Portuguese's identical «Item», «OK» and «Pixel (x, y)» in the E05
walk, now in `SAME_IN`), `exports-identity`, `observatory` and `shell` 61; `npm run test:scripts` 59 and `npm run test:pages` on
`dist/` 51 (three load timeouts with two workers, the English scripts.css route walks at 320 and 1440 px and the Spanish
pulsar-analysis smoke, passed when rerun alone). No other failures.

**Independent review (PT4).** Every string this front adds or changes was read against the English, with Spanish beside it (the 587
catalog rows and the three maps). The translation holds: você throughout, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990 spelling, the IAU OAE terms
(aglomerado, magnitude aparente, módulo de distância, índice de cor, avermelhamento, isócrona, metalicidade, sequência principal de
idade zero, curva de luz), PT1's VIREO names with VIREO's English beside them, “ ” quotes, placeholders, decimal points and English
thousands separators, no claim to be CLEA's. Corrected: 14 catalog strings, one map string and one glossary row:
- **Turn-off › «ponto de saída da sequência principal»** (was «ponto de desvio», after Spanish's «punto de desvío»): in this bundle
  «desvio» is redshift («desvio para o vermelho», about 40 strings), so «ponto de desvio» beside a colour–magnitude diagram reads as a
  shift; the descriptive form is unambiguous and is how Brazilian teaching texts explain the turn-off. `nc.limits`,
  `clusterLab.step.trial.help`, the Cluster Data map's `editModeColumns` and the glossary row.
- **Meaning and grammar:** `db.title` laboratory › «Laboratório…» (it said «Prática», the word for a lab exercise, while
  `clusterLab.return` and `clusterLab.progressHelp` call the same page «laboratório»); `db.catalogDraft`, `db.manualDraft` and
  `db.catalogConfirmed` «Origem do avermelhamento não aplicado/confirmado» (the participle belongs to the reddening, not to «origem»);
  `ph.uncertifiedReading` «leitura medida com…» (was a bare masculine «medido»); `obsHr.beforeBlock` and `obsHr.blockingRecord` "web plot
  withheld" › «gráfico web não exibido» (was «retido», the word the implementer had already replaced for "withholds");
  `ph.detectSkyLocked` names the button «o apontamento do telescópio» again; `pa.fitHelp` «aparecem dispersas»; `ph.domainCount` «em
  relação a» (was the calque «contra»).
- **One form each:** `ce.inspectedMarker` «classificação de pertinência ao aglomerado» (was «de membro»); `obsHr.ownRecordsHelp` «o
  registro do Hubble [Record]», as `ph.nativeHelp`, `ph.nativeRecordHelp` and PT2's `ownResults.sharedHelp`.

Judgments kept, on the implementer's uncertain items: press › «pressione» (the glossary's `press` row has required it since PT0, it is
device-neutral on touch screens, and the earlier fronts use it throughout; «clique em» stays for mouse-specific text such as a
right-click); membership › «pertinência ao aglomerado» (the Brazilian cluster-membership term), with «membros do aglomerado» kept where a
sentence speaks of the stars themselves; pointing › «apontamento» for the bench button and its citations (as PT2) and «posição do
telescópio» in descriptions (as PT1), both natural; the ~17 short labels over 1.6× English are ordinary Portuguese noun phrases
(«Controle deslizante: …», «Tentar carregar de novo») and pass the layout walks at 320 px. The specs keep every Spanish assertion (titles,
SAME lists, the target-option wording now read from the same catalog text, caption checks); two comments that still named
`es-observatory.spec.ts` now name `observatory.spec.ts`.

**Left for later fronts.** PT5: the radio telescope (its maps join `index.ts`, `pt-br.test.ts` and `languageText.ts` beside this front's
lines). PT6: E06 and E08, with the `fits` keys. PT-HELP: the help viewer's own map (`helpViewer`), its topic text and the cluster,
photometer and variable-star help files. `tests/fixtures/layoutProblems.ts` still names `es-e04`/`es-e05` in historical comments.

## Brazilian Portuguese: the radio telescope (PT5)

The fifth Portuguese front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT5" in the research root, with ES8a as its Spanish
model; it ran beside PT4 (E04 and E05). Portuguese stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=pt-BR` reaches it. The translations are
web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English. VIREO's own radio captions (the control panel,
Radio Simulation Options, Pulsar Parameters, the receiver windows, Identify Recorded Object, the Save and unsaved-data questions, the
Cannot Close Receiver! box, the drawn annotation's Sec and Deg) were among PT1's originals, so this front adds web text only.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** ES8a's 646 keys, every key of `radio.*` (72), `radioRoute.*` (131), `radioAccess.*` (141), `radioReceiver.*` (200),
  `radioSimOptions.*` (20) and `pulsarParameters.*` (82); 645 were new (`radioRoute.nav` came with PT0), about 9,700 English words.
  Every radio file and prefix is at 100 % (`node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang pt-BR`); `pt-br.json` now holds 2,848 keys. `plr.*`
  and `pulsarLab.*` are E06's (PT6), as they were ES8b's.
- **Maps, complete:** `radioAntenna` (324 strings), `radioR1` (11), `radioRecordSave` (17) and `radioFinishSave` (16)
  (`src/i18n/pt-br/maps/radio*.ts`), about 7,300 English words. `RADIO_MESSAGES_EN` stays catalog-backed (its Portuguese is `radio.*`).
- **Still English until PT6:** the receiver's Record Save dialog also renders texts of the `pulsar` map through
  `createPulsarTranslator` (`pulsar.rawExport.scope`, `.format`, `.inactive`, `.inactiveNone`, `.loss.*`, `pulsar.save.how`,
  `.extensionRule`, `.origin`, `.warning`, `.choose`, `.chooseAgain`, `.refusal.stale`), as ES8a waited for ES8b. `radio.spec.ts`
  lists them as `TODO_PT6_PULSAR_KEYS`, allowed only in pt-BR and only on that step; PT6 removes the list. PT6's `pulsar.save.open` and
  `pulsar.rawExport.open` must read «Salvar captura como .PLR (verificada)…» and «Baixar captura como .PLR…», which
  `radioReceiver.record.scope`, `.yesGated` and `radioSave.scope` quote, and `pulsar.save.choose` should start «Escolher», the word
  `radioSave.pendingNote` and the abandoned texts use for its Choose.
- **Canvas:** nothing new. `receiverScreenText` and `receiverMarkScreenText` translate only VIREO's pieces through `vireo()`
  (PT1's «Seg», «Graus», «Abertura:», «Metros», «Receptor», «Nenhum pulsar no feixe!»); numbers, MHz and pulsar names are drawn as
  composed. The spec's canvas test spies on `fillText` in pt-BR too.
- **Kept as recorded:** pulsar names, Pulsars.dat values, the stored ID “UNKNOWN”, file names and hashes, “No Data”, and the VIREO
  pulsar names the Spanish lint sets aside are Portuguese VIREO names here (Acesso à lista de pulsares, Parâmetros do pulsar…), so no
  set-aside was needed: Portuguese «pulsar» is the same word.

**How it was translated.** Seven Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English, with the reviewed Spanish shown only as a
meaning reference, under PT1's style brief extended with the radio terms below and the fixed VIREO names from `originals.json`: four
catalog batches (174 keys of `radio`, `radioSimOptions` and `pulsarParameters`; 130 of `radioRoute`; 141 of `radioAccess`; 200 of
`radioReceiver`, merged with `scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge --lang pt-BR`) and three map batches (the two halves of `radioAntenna`, 162
strings each; the three small maps, 44). The Opus implementer read every row against the English and corrected 42 strings (4 %):
- **The pt-BR glyph rule counts VIREO's `...` with `…`**, so a gloss that repeated a menu path or an ellipsis failed it (15 strings):
  a path is glossed per segment, «Mover (Slew) ▸ Definir coordenadas… (Set Coordinates)», «Arquivo (File) ▸ Lista de pulsares... (Pulsar
  List)», and the English in brackets drops its `...` («Local... (Site)»).
- **One form each:** the radio route is «a página do radiotelescópio» (two strings had «esta tela»); ported › «migrado» (three had
  «portado»); the key is «Esc» (five had «Escape»), as PT3 writes it; «responder à» (three); current pulsar data › «dados de pulsar
  atuais» (five had «dados atuais de pulsar»); receiver monitor › «monitor do receptor».
- **Meaning and wording:** a slew answered No is «não confirmado» (was «recusado», the word for a refused request); a dome state that
  holds access «bloqueia» it (was «retém»); the receiver «gera» a modelled signal (was «desenha», drawing); «O registro passou a ser os
  dados de pulsar atuais»; «um tique manual do temporizador de movimento»; «só a opção Somente trânsito (Transit Only)» (was «somente
  Somente trânsito»); «nenhum nome de local está implícito»; «(definido pela abertura do receptor [Receiver Open], …)»; «seu Cancelar
  (Cancel)»; «os IDs»; and `radioRoute.scope` glosses Definir coordenadas… [Set Coordinates] at its first mention.
- Term choices kept from the batches: Tracking › «Acompanhamento» (PT1's original); a held press › «pressionamento contínuo» and press
  and hold › «manter pressionado»; the panel's Drive › «Acionamento»; tick › «tique» (PT1, PT2); gate (the Preferences gate) › «acesso
  a Preferências», a browser gate › «barreira do navegador»; switch › «interruptor» (the Radio switch › «interruptor Rádio»); host ›
  «ambiente hospedeiro»; flags › «sinalizadores»; the finish question › «pergunta de conclusão»; reseed › «reinicialização da semente»;
  thread › «linha de execução»; Choose › «Escolher»; Back › «Voltar»; the receiver display › «tela»; Move up/down › «Subir»/«Descer»;
  Scenario inputs › «Entradas do cenário»; Radio clock scenario › «Cenário do relógio de rádio»; Use compiled defaults › «Usar padrões
  compilados»; built-in defaults › «padrões integrados»; Reset radio site › «Redefinir local de rádio»; Start pulsar exercise ›
  «Iniciar exercício de pulsares». The direction buttons show PT1's «N», «S», «L», «O», and the prose that names them or their
  directions says N/S/L/O too (the review's change: the batches kept VIREO's N/S/E/W, as Spanish does, where only W differs); the
  keyboard text keeps the letter keys E, N, S and W, which the keys are.

**Glossary** (`glossary.json`, add-only): five `terms` (audition › audição/ouvir, radio clock › relógio de rádio, press and hold ›
manter pressionado/pressionamento contínuo, slew-timer › temporizador de movimento, receiver monitor › monitor do receptor; PT0's seed
already had altitude limit, drive, sweep, beam, seed and timer) and five `names` (Radio clock scenario, Scenario inputs, Use compiled
defaults, Reset radio site, Start pulsar exercise). `identical.json` gains 19 rows, Spanish's ES8a reasons (placeholders, UT, the
coordinate formats, the dome byte 2, SHA-256, bytes) plus Longitude and OK, the same words in Portuguese. `lint-allowed.json` gains one:
`radioReceiver.marks.selectLabel` starts with the noun «Marca», which the pair-aware register rule reads as a tu-command of «marcar».

**Tests.**
- `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`: the four radio maps in `ENGLISH_MAPS` and `COMPLETE_MAPS`, a PT5 coverage check (every key of the six
  prefixes), and the profile lint over 2,848 keys, 23 maps and the originals (no errors; the length reports are short labels where
  slew › «movimento» and the infinitive buttons run long, such as «Cancelar o carregamento da lista de objetos»).
- `tests/i18n/es-radio.spec.ts` → `tests/i18n/radio.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over `TRANSLATED` as PT3 did: the seven Spanish tests
  keep their titles, steps and assertions (`npx playwright test --list` shows the same seven titles; 113 `expect(` calls on 85 lines
  before and after), with their Spanish read from `languageText.ts`; pt-BR runs the same six checks under its own titles in its preview; qps runs
  once. The canvas test's caption pattern is built from the originals (for Spanish it is the old literal pattern). Per-language
  identicals: `SAME_IN` (pt-BR: OK, Longitude, Latitude, Altitude, Pulsar) and `NOT_SAME_IN` (pt-BR must translate the shared
  Spanish-only Radio, Radio..., E, No, no and Hrs).
- `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`: `RADIO_MAPS`/`radioMaps(tag)` (the control panel and Record Save maps and identical.json, the View
  original test's web text).
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the ES8a family (raw capture `.PLR`, VIREO's Save Recorded Measurements text and the
  investigation) also runs in pt-BR: the same names and SHA-256 as English, no Portuguese letter in the files, and the route's
  Portuguese `h1` is checked. Its title, its `<html lang>` check and its locale list changed.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main 780ae37 built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). pt-BR: 12
chunks, 173.7 KB gzip after the review (173.6 before it) — radio 40.3 KB gzip (164.7 KB raw; Spanish's is 40.0), pulsar+radio 1.3 KB
(the `radioR1` map; Pulsar Analysis's File menu shows it too), pulsar 0.2 KB (a loader that only re-exports pulsar+radio, as Spanish's),
core 82.2 KB (263.5 KB raw; Vite's gzip figure 82.05 → 83.03 kB against main: the 13 radio keys the core holds, such as the route's
title, scope and loading text and the data-property labels). The radio budget is 60/90 KB, the sum 300/360. Spanish: all 13 chunks
byte-identical, names included. English: every file identical once chunk hashes are normalised.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pt5` from main 780ae37). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`
and `src/components`: 758 tests in 33 files; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check; Playwright (dev server,
Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig): `radio` 13, `observatory`, `shell` and `exports-identity` 61; `npm run test:scripts` 59; `npm run
test:pages` on `dist/` 51. No failures. The pt-BR 320 px screenshots of the walk (control panel, Radio Simulation Options, the receiver
with a placed mark, the Record Save dialog) were inspected.

**Independent review (PT5).** Every string this front adds was read against the English (the 646 catalog keys and the 368 strings of
the four maps). The translation holds: você throughout, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990 spelling, the radio terms (radiotelescópio,
receptor, frequência, ganho, feixe, largura do feixe, pulso, período, medida de dispersão, trânsito, varredura, traçado), PT1's VIREO
names with VIREO's English at the first mention, “ ” quotes, placeholders and decimal points, pulsar names and file names as recorded,
no claim to be CLEA's. Corrected: 17 strings:
- **The direction buttons:** 14 strings named buttons the Portuguese panel does not have («os botões … E e W ficam ocultos»,
  «Mantenha pressionado N, S, E ou W», «botão N/S/E/W»): the buttons read «N», «S», «L», «O» (and their labels say «L (botão original
  L)»), so that prose now says N/S/L/O (`radio.scope`, `radio.panel.gap`, `radioRoute.scenario.gates`, the two manual-hold refusals,
  and in `radioAntenna` the scope, the Slew-menu and transit notes, the button state and hint, the two unavailable reasons and the
  options guard). `antenna.manual.keys` keeps E, N, S and W: there they are the keyboard's letter keys.
- **Wording:** `antenna.rateLimit.stored` «ou faça com que o ambiente hospedeiro ative» (was «peça ao ambiente hospedeiro», asking an
  environment); `antenna.outcome.slew-axis-ra` «movendo-se em ascensão reta», as `antenna.status.slewing*` write it;
  `antenna.outcome.manual-idle` «Nenhum botão de movimento manual está pressionado» (a slew is not pressed).

Judgments kept: «acesso a Preferências» for the Preferences gate (it opens and closes naturally, «o acesso … está aberto», and is
always qualified, so it does not read as telescope access); «ambiente hospedeiro» for the host that supplies the options (Spanish's
«entorno anfitrión»; «computador hospedeiro» where the English means the classroom computer); «registro» for a recording beside
«Registro {position} de {count}» for a catalog record (gravar already means writing a file, and context separates them). The
`TODO_PT6_PULSAR_KEYS` exemption is narrow: 16 exact `pulsar.*` keys, each an anchored pattern of its English text, only for pt-BR and
only on the Record Save step. The specs keep every Spanish assertion (the sorted `expect(` calls of the old and new file differ only by
the language variable), and the radio export family adds pt-BR without loosening a check.

**Left for later fronts.** PT6: the `pulsar` map (and with it the Record Save dialog's pending texts and the removal of
`TODO_PT6_PULSAR_KEYS`), `plr.*` and `pulsarLab.*`. PT-HELP: the radio help files behind the panel's Help menu and PLSLSHLP.
`tests/fixtures/layoutProblems.ts` and `tests/i18n/es-narrow.spec.ts` still name `es-radio` in comments (SC9's and ES-REL's files).

## Front HEADER: the page bar (2026-11-10)

User decision of 2026-11-10 (AGENTS.md; plan `analysis/front-plans/front-header.md` in the research root): the sidebar's Help button
and the investigation backup card give way to a page bar at the top of the content area (`src/components/PageBar.tsx`). What it
means for languages:
- **Where the selector is.** The language `<select>` (`LanguageSelect`, with its reload dialog) is in the page bar beside Help, on
  every width (no Tools menu needed on phones). "Show VIREO's own texts in English" (`VireoOriginalsOption`) stays at the foot of the
  sidebar under the theme switch. Both come from the same lazily loaded module (`src/components/LanguageChoice.tsx`) with the same
  messages, storage keys and test ids (`language-choice` is the select's wrapper; the checkbox has its own wrapper
  `language-originals-choice`). `selectorShown` hides both, as before (the pseudo-locales show neither).
- **The place.** From 600 px up the bar names the current page as "section › page" with the sidebar's own labels (the new route
  table `src/components/routePages.ts`, which the sidebar's page groups now also read), so it is translated wherever the menu is.
- **New strings** (the `appShell` map, complete in every language; no catalog key added or removed): `pageBar.label` "Investigation,
  language and help" (the region's name; es «Investigación, idioma y ayuda», pt-BR «Investigação, idioma e ajuda»), `pageBar.more`
  "More" (es «Más», pt-BR «Mais») and `pageBar.moreName` "More investigation actions" (es «Más acciones de la investigación», pt-BR
  «Mais ações da investigação»). The visible word must start the accessible name (WCAG 2.5.3), checked for each language by
  `src/components/pageBar.test.ts`. Reused: `project.save`, `project.restore`, `project.title` (now the visually hidden name of the
  backup group), `project.toolbarHelp`, `classLink.cancel`, `appShell.backupHelpSummary`.
- **Changed English, translated at once.** The tour's save step (`tour` map, es and pt-BR) now says where the buttons are: "Save
  investigation, at the top of every page, downloads a file; Restore investigation JSON, in the menu beside it, loads it again later."
  The web help (`webHelp`, English and Spanish): the Saving topic's first item and the "Load Saved Data..." row of "Coming from VIREO"
  name the top of the page and the More menu instead of the backup bar. Brazilian Portuguese has no `webHelp` map yet: **PT-HELP
  translates these two English items** with the rest of the web help. Kept on purpose: the five "Save investigation in the toolbar"
  strings (the bar is the toolbar), "Help is always at the top of the page", the tour's help step and `toolsMenu`.
- **For later languages.** Each new language's `appShell` and `tour` maps carry the three `pageBar` strings and the new save-step
  text; the selector specs find the `<select>` in `.page-bar` and "View original" in the sidebar footer. Files are unchanged: Save
  investigation writes the same bytes in every language (`exports-identity.spec.ts`, unchanged and green).

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-header` from main 2dbb118). A step-0 spike first: in Chromium, Firefox and
WebKit (the local `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble` image), `getByLabel('Restore investigation JSON').setInputFiles` on a
`hidden` file input and `input.click()` from a menu item (the `filechooser` event, focus kept on the menu button) both work, so the
plan's fallback (a visually hidden input) was not needed. `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`,
`src/components` and `src/theme`: 792 tests in 37 files (the audited UI files go 142 → 144 with `PageBar` and
`InvestigationActions`); the pins refreshed (only `ime-enter-sites.json`: the select's component is now `LanguageSelect`); `npm run
build` with every check. Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig): `page-bar.spec.ts` 11; the changed specs (`shell`,
`project-links`, `clea-help`, `onboarding`, `recorded-analysis-navigation`, `es-narrow`, the four restore file-chooser specs,
`language-selector`, `scripts-pseudo.smoke`) 94; `exports-identity`'s Save investigation and E02 families 2; a broad English sample
of 23 more specs (cluster, areas, es-browsers, es-help, Object X identity, own results, pulsar restore, the radio app specs, instructor
mode, project naming, camera palette, app shell, focus, SEO lab pages, camera narrow layout) 195; `render-budget.spec.ts` 3;
`cloud-account.spec.ts` (cloud config, loopback) 1 passed, 1 skipped as before; `npm run test:scripts` 59; `npm run test:pages` on
`dist/` 51 (one test expected the removed «One investigation backup» heading and now finds Save in the bar's region). Firefox
(native) and WebKit (the image): `page-bar`, `language-selector`, `investigation-photometry` and `onboarding`, 30 each, except one
WebKit failure that main 2dbb118 has too (`language-selector`'s PT0 keyboard-stepping test, where an expected confirmation dialog does not
appear in WebKit; not investigated further, since it is independent of this front). No other failures.

**Independent review** (2026-11-10, Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort, over b3ff60a; fixes in the same worktree). The user, on seeing
the bar, asked for it pinned: from 600 px up it is sticky at the top of the viewport (below 600 px it scrolls with the page; see
AGENTS.md and `src/components/page-bar.css`). No string changed for it. Findings, all fixed: the place read as one word to screen
readers («Observatorio ópticoObservatorio»; a space now separates section and page, and the shell and page-bar specs expect it);
the hidden group name «Una copia de seguridad de la investigación» was announced twice (now `aria-hidden`, still the group's
label); a racy Cancel check in `page-bar.spec.ts`; two docs still naming the removed card. The new es-419 and pt-BR strings follow
the glossaries (tú, você). observatoryLoad 97 → 102 re-measured against main: real (PageBar, InvestigationActions and the second
language slot), budget 110 kept; pinning adds no render. The WebKit failure is pre-existing on main 2dbb118: in
`language-selector.spec.ts`'s PT0 keyboard test, Tab out of the select with a pending step does not open the confirmation in WebKit
(line 120), to be scheduled. Verification: `tsc -b`; Vitest 792 + pins 31 (unchanged); build; Playwright Chromium: page-bar (14 with
the three pinned cases) and shell 36, render budget, onboarding, focus-after-layout, comparison-inspection 18, a sample of 14 specs

failure; `test:scripts` 59; `test:pages` 51.
After the pinning, CI found the translation walks (`e01`–`e05`, `es-e06`, `es-e08`, `es-radio`, `observatory`) collecting shell text
that used to live only in the sidebar, which the walks skip: the selector's bilingual legend «Idioma / Language» and its autonyms

place's `<p>`), and each walk's skip list names it beside `.portal-header`; they stay checked where shell text is checked
(`shell.spec.ts` reads the place against each language's nav labels; `language-selector.spec.ts` the select). The reload dialog is
outside the label and is still walked. A later walk spec skips `[data-i18n-shell]` too.

## Brazilian Portuguese: E06 pulsar analysis and E08 Object X (PT6)

The sixth Portuguese front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT6" in the research root, with ES8b and ES9 as its
Spanish models. Portuguese stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=pt-BR` reaches it. The translations are web adaptations by
OpenVireo, never CLEA's; every file the site writes stays English, and E08's object stays hidden until the reveal in Portuguese too.
VIREO's own captions on these screens (Review and its Find, the comment editor, VIREO's Save and Clear questions, the print header,
the camera's Information window, the spectrometer's Spectral Range... and Help) were among PT1's originals, so this front adds web
text only.

**What is translated.**
- **Catalog:** the 134 keys left, every key of `pulsarLab.*` (82 new; `pulsarLab.nav` came with PT0), `plr.*` (8; `plr.nav` PT0's)
  and `fits.*` (44; `fits.nav` PT0's), about 2,100 English words. `pt-br.json` now holds every en.json key (3,567): every file and
  prefix of `node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang pt-BR` is at 100 %.
- **Maps, complete:** `pulsar` (443 strings), `pulsarSavedRows` (83), `pulsarLoadSave` (25), `objectXLab` (127 new; PT0 had its two
  menu names), `objectXGuide` (31), `objectXMeasure` (52) and `objectXSession` (1) (`src/i18n/pt-br/maps/`), about 9,500 English
  words. With them 31 of the 33 maps are started, every one complete; `webHelp` and `helpViewer` are PT-HELP's. `createPulsarTranslator`
  takes only a complete map, so the pulsar panel switched from English to Portuguese as a whole.
- **The verdict's article** follows the kind id, as in Spanish: «É uma estrela.», «É uma galáxia.», «É um quasar (QSO).», «É um
  pulsar.», «É um asteroide.»; «Sua resposta está certa: é …» when the answer is right. The answer legend is «Meu objeto é», so the
  bare kind names need no article.
- **Hidden identity:** no Portuguese string names or describes a target, and every string reads the same for every target (the
  neutral «Nenhum espectro mensurável nesta posição com este telescópio.», the hidden asteroid count «asteroides da lista atual
  incluídos (o número aparece após a revelação)»).
- **The receiver's Record Save dialog** (PT5) rendered 16 texts of the `pulsar` map in English; they are Portuguese now, and
  `TODO_PT6_PULSAR_KEYS` is gone from `tests/i18n/radio.spec.ts` (with its source reader and the `readFileSync` import). The
  buttons the radio strings quote read as PT5 wrote them: `pulsar.save.open` «Salvar captura como .PLR (verificada)…»,
  `pulsar.rawExport.open` «Baixar captura como .PLR…», `pulsar.save.choose` «Escolher arquivo e salvar…» (its Choose is «Escolher»,
  as `radioSave.pendingNote` and the abandoned texts say). A scan of every catalog and radio/pulsar map string that cites one of the
  panel's buttons found the Portuguese button text in each (28 citations).
- **Kept as recorded:** pulsar names, file names (.PLR, .CSV, ObjX-42-01.FIT, ObjX_MPC.CMO.sav…), the stored “No Data” and “Table
  Number:”, the Object X codes and ObjX labels, ObjectX.HTL's identifier and remark after the reveal, the asteroid designations and
  note codes, the recorded VizieR rows, FITS cards, hashes.

**How it was translated.** Five Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English, with the reviewed Spanish shown only as a
meaning reference, under PT1–PT5's style brief extended with this front's terms and the button wordings PT5 fixed: the catalog
(134 keys, merged with `scripts/i18n-batch.mjs merge --lang pt-BR`), the `pulsar` map in two halves (225 and 218 strings), the two
small pulsar maps (108) and the four Object X maps (written as TypeScript from the Spanish files' shape). The Opus implementer read
every row against the English and corrected 34 strings (4 %):
- **Download:** the glossary's `download` row wants «baix»; twelve pulsar strings said «o download» and now say «baixar» («Pedido
  para baixar {name} feito…», «Não foi possível começar a baixar o arquivo.», «O arquivo baixado é uma cópia…», «Baixar o arquivo não
  confirma que ele foi gravado.»), as PT5's radio strings do.
- **One form each:** line endings › «finais de linha» / «final de linha» (six strings had «fins»/«fim»); Dismiss › «Fechar» in both
  viewers (one said «Dispensar»), as the radio's «Feche primeiro a mensagem»; a copy «lida de volta igual» (two loadSave strings said
  «lida de volta e confere como igual»).
- **Glosses:** a gloss inside parentheses uses square brackets («(Registrar [Record] armado, recebendo ou concluindo)», four
  strings, and the guide's «Salvar medições registradas [Save recorded measurements]»); VIREO's “Dados não gravados em arquivo” keeps
  its English outside the quotes.
- **Wording:** «e esta marca fica então desmarcada» and «A configuração continua desmarcada» (were «sem seleção», «sem marca»);
  «Sortear um para mim» for "Assign me one at random"; «Confirme, mova o telescópio e abra o Receptor (Receiver)» (a slew is a
  move); the guide's «30–60 minutos depois» keeps the English en dash; the measuring tool's adaptation note says «um programa externo
  de astrometria» after the CLEA Astrometry Toolkit's English name (the `astrometry` term).
- Term choices kept from the batches: pan › «deslocar» / «Taxa de deslocamento»; zoom in/out › «Aumentar/Reduzir zoom»; display
  factor › «fator de exibição»; placeholder › «marcador»; headerless › «sem cabeçalho»; carriage return / line feed › «retorno de
  carro» / «avanço de linha»; viewer › «visualizador»; Append (VIREO's action) › «Acrescentar» while the web button that adds rows to a
  saved file reads «Adicionar medições registradas…»; the export's Table number › «Número da mesa de laboratório» (PT1's Lab Table);
  blink › «comparação por piscagem», «Iniciar/Parar a piscagem», «Compare por piscagem e meça»; low/high cut › «Corte
  inferior/superior»; trail › «rastro» (the camera's word); field box › «caixa do campo»; near-Earth › «próximo da Terra»; kept spectra
  › «Espectros mantidos»; Classify (the route) › «Classificação estelar»; reveal › «Revelar a identidade» / «revelação»;
  the observatory names in the guide are PT1's (Digite as coordenadas, Apontar para as coordenadas, Campo de visão › Telescópio · 15′,
  Mover para o objeto selecionado).
- Kept as the English says: `pulsar.save.fail.abandoned` «Salvar e Cancelar voltam a funcionar» (the English names Save and Cancel,
  not the dialog's Choose).

**Shared files (add-only).** `identical.json` gains 45 rows (Spanish's ES8b/ES9 reasons in Portuguese: the empty statuses, Bytes,
SHA-256, Zoom, Horizontal, Vertical (s), #, the dash, CR LF and LF, OK, the placeholder-only formats, the telescope apertures, the date
placeholder, the list's note numbers, Dec, V, UT, x, y, the cursor readout, «quasar (QSO)» and «pulsar»); `lint-allowed.json` gains
three: `objectXMeasure.colRole` (the noun «Marca», as PT5's receiver mark), `pulsar.nativeSave.problem.reserved` (the straight quote
listed among the characters a file name cannot contain) and `objectXLab.imageSkipped` (the lint's sample argument «A1» is iterated as
the reasons list, so its guillemets reach the output). No glossary row was added or changed. No existing entry of a shared file changed.

**Tests.**
- `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`: the seven maps in `ENGLISH_MAPS` and `COMPLETE_MAPS`, a PT6 coverage check (the 137 keys of
  `pulsarLab`, `plr` and `fits`, and every en.json key), and the profile lint over 3,567 keys, 31 maps and the originals (no errors).
- `tests/i18n/es-e06.spec.ts` → `e06.spec.ts` and `es-e08.spec.ts` → `e08.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over `TRANSLATED` as PT3–PT5
  did: the Spanish tests keep their titles, steps and assertions (the `expect(` count is unchanged; the Spanish identical words and
  "Panel {n}" moved to a per-language `SAME_IN`), with their text read from `languageText.ts` (`e06Maps`, `e08Maps`); pt-BR runs the
  same checks under its own titles in its preview, including, in E08, the identity check at every step before the reveal (the verdict
  pattern gains «É uma/um» and «Sua resposta está certa») and the star-versus-asteroid sameness test. qps runs once. Portuguese keeps
  Bytes, Zoom, Horizontal, Vertical (s) and OK in E06.
- `tests/i18n/exports-identity.spec.ts`: the ES8b family (E06) and the ES9 family (E08) also run in pt-BR, with the route's Portuguese
  `h1` checked; the E08 investigations before and after the reveal are byte-identical to English's (one documented mask), so the
  Portuguese run cannot put a target's name in the file either.
- `tests/fixtures/layoutProblems.ts`: one comment names `e06` instead of `es-e06`.
- `check-i18n-dist.mjs` finds a module's chunk by its markers, the single-quoted literals of its map files: the three pulsar maps are
  written with single quotes (PT5's `radioR1`, the only Portuguese map of pulsar+radio before PT6, uses double quotes, so the build
  warned that pt-BR's pulsar and pulsar+radio modules were found by chunk name only; the first batch output of this front did too). One string, `pulsar.nativeSave.problem.reserved`, stays double-quoted: the script reads a single-quoted
  literal's `\\` without unescaping it, so that marker could never match the chunk. No warning is left.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`; compared with main 0db2ca1 built the same way). pt-BR: 13 chunks, 234.4 KB gzip
(173.7 after PT5; 234.1 after the review's corrections) — core 99.4 KB gzip (317.9 KB raw; 99.3 after the review; the 82 `pulsarLab` keys and the guide maps live there), pulsar+radio 14.4 KB
(the `pulsar` map with `radioR1`), pulsar 3.9 KB (the saved-rows and load-save maps), fits 2.8 KB, radio 40.3 KB (unchanged); Spanish's
are 104.4, 14.3, 3.9, 2.8 and 40.3. Budgets: core 120/150, a module 60/90, the sum 300/360 KB. Spanish: all 13 chunks byte-identical
to main, names included. English: the same 2,092 files; once chunk hashes are normalised, six shared chunks (the field and exposure
spectrometers, the Hubble engine entry and targets, the Multi-Spec inputs) differed only in the minifier's local identifier names, with
the same sizes (two builds of main are identical, so the new pt-BR modules in the graph shift rolldown's naming); no source outside
`src/i18n/pt-br/` changed. The independent review could not reproduce those renames: its builds of main and of this branch (`vite
build` without the public folder) differ, outside the four pt-BR chunks this front fills, only in the hashed chunk file names that
the shared and English chunks import; with those names normalised every other file is byte-identical, so no English text,
behaviour or size changed and there was nothing to avoid.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pt6` from main 0db2ca1). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/seo`
and `src/components`: 760 tests in 33 files; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check (no warning from
`check-i18n-dist.mjs`); Playwright (dev server on port 5457, since 5449 was held by another session's server; Chromium, DejaVu

on `dist/` 51. Load flakes, each passing when rerun alone: the pt-BR 320 px E06 layout walk (a click on the saved-rows notice's OK

**Independent review (PT6).** Every string this front adds was read against the English (the 134 catalog keys and the 762 strings of
the seven maps), with Spanish beside it where a wording was in doubt. The translation holds: você throughout, Brazilian vocabulary
and post-1990 spelling, the IAU OAE pt-BR terms (pulsar, período, medida de dispersão, quasar, galáxia, estrela, asteroide,
astrometria, câmera CCD/IR), PT1's VIREO names with VIREO's English at the first mention, PT5's radio words (registro for a

- **Glosses:** `pulsarLab.recover.clock` and `pulsarLab.recover.catalog` glossed two web names («Cenário do relógio de rádio (Radio

- **Wording:** `fits.help` «uma imagem FITS … fornecida» (was «que você recebeu»; a supplied file may be the student's own save).

Judgments kept: `pulsar.save.fail.abandoned` «Salvar e Cancelar voltam a funcionar» names the actions as the English and Spanish do
(the dialog's buttons are «Escolher arquivo e salvar…» and «Cancelar»; «Salvar» is the action they share, and the radio dialogs,
