## The language select in WebKit (WEBKIT-SELECT, 2026-11-10)

**The bug.** PT0's keyboard test failed in WebKit, on main too: after Escape dropped a pending step, stepping back to the same option
and pressing Tab (or Enter) asked nothing. A probe in the local `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble` image logged the events:
the arrow changed the select's value but fired no `change` (nor `input`). WebKit fires `change` on a closed select only when the
selected option differs from the one of its last `change`, a record it also resets when the select gains focus; a value the page sets
(Escape's return to the current language, or the dialog's) does not move it. With no `change`, nothing was pending, so leaving the
select asked nothing. Blur and focus events arrived as in Chromium and Firefox; `document.hasFocus()` stayed true.

**The fix** (`src/components/LanguageChoice.tsx`). A key's step is also read from the select's own value: `sync` marks the shown
option pending (or clears it when it is the current language) on key up, and before Enter, Escape and leaving the select act. Escape
also puts the select's value back itself, since a pending state that ends where it began makes React skip the render. Closing the
dialog puts the value back before the focus returns to the select, so WebKit's record starts from the current language; the probe
showed that without it a later step or pick to the option just cancelled fired no `change` in WebKit, and with it `change` fires.
Chromium and Firefox fire `change` on every step, so `sync` finds nothing to do there and their behaviour is unchanged. Focus,
Escape, Cancel's focus and the focus return are as PT0 left them.

**A mouse pick of the dropped option (review).** The implementer inferred that a mouse pick of the option Escape just dropped would
fire no `change` in WebKit (Playwright's `selectOption` dispatches its own events and does not reach WebKit's check). The review's
probe with real mouse events in the same image showed otherwise: a mouse press on the select resets WebKit's record as focus does
(WebKit saves the last selection before it opens a menu list's popup), so after Escape, a press and then the dropped option fired
`change`. Headless WebKit and Firefox on Linux open no list after the press, so the arrow is a step that Enter asks for; headless
Chromium opens the list, where the arrow moves and Enter picks, which asks at once. The PT0 keyboard test in `language-selector.spec.ts` pins this in every engine. Safari's
native popup on macOS was not checked (no macOS here); if its pick fired no `change`, leaving the select would still ask (`sync` on blur).

**Verification.** `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/components` and `src/i18n/imeAudit.test.ts`, 45 tests (no pin changes); `npm run
build` with every check. Playwright (dev server): `language-selector.spec.ts` and `page-bar.spec.ts`, 24 tests in each of Chromium,
Firefox and WebKit (Docker, as above; the keyboard test failed there before the fix); `shell.spec.ts` in Chromium, 22.
Review (high effort): the same checks, 24 tests in each engine and `shell.spec.ts` 22, with the mouse step added to the PT0 keyboard
test; a scratch probe (removed) also passed in all three engines for a no-op step (away and back, then Enter, Tab or Shift+Tab ask
nothing, before and after an Escape), Shift+Tab and a mouse click elsewhere with a pending step (one dialog each), Cancel by keyboard
then the same step (asks again, focus on the select), Escape twice, and blocked storage (the not-remembered note, applied through the
address), with no console errors or warnings.

## Brazilian Portuguese: help files (PT-HELP)

The seventh Portuguese front, plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2 "PT-HELP", §3.5 and §1.2 #8 in the research root,
with ES-HELP as its Spanish model. Portuguese stays hidden (`released:false`): only `?lang=pt-BR` reaches it. VIREO's 38 bundled
help files and OpenVireo's own help are translated as web adaptations by OpenVireo, never CLEA's: the originals under
`public/original`, their bytes, SHA-256 and provenance are unchanged, and "Ver original (inglês)" shows VIREO's exact text.

**What is translated.**
- **The 38 help modules** `src/i18n/pt-br/help/<NAME>.ts` (`{language:'pt-BR',sourceName,sourceSha256,text}`), in VIREO's own
  file format exactly as ES-HELP describes: Latin-1, LF, every TXT line at 72 characters or fewer, titles at 65 or fewer, the same
  LST rows, kinds and file tokens (the unterminated `'Classificar espectros' 'begin` and `'Tipos de estrelas variáveis' 'begin`
  rows reproduced; HUBHLP and LSSHLP keep their blank lines and stay refused; MLTSPHLP and SPLSTHLP stay empty), each section header
  the row title it opens, SPECLNS.LNL equal to the original and SPECLNS.TXT's `*3933.68   Ca II (K line)*` keys VIREO's.
  About 18,600 English words, 20,600 Portuguese. Latin-1 has no “ ”, so the help files quote with VIREO's straight `"`, as §3.5
  says (the loader refuses a character above U+00FF).
- **The related-topics marker** «Tópicos relacionados:» (`help:{related:'TÓPICOS RELACIONADOS'}` in `src/i18n/languages.json`;
  `core/cleaHelp.ts` upper-cases the line, so «Tópicos» matches «TÓPICOS»). VIREO's "See also:" lines read «Veja também:».
- **Maps, complete:** `helpViewer` (the viewer's language switch, «Tradução: adaptação web do OpenVireo (não é do CLEA)» ·
  «Ver original (inglês)» · «Ver tradução», and the Spectral Line Table window) and `webHelp` (OpenVireo's own help, Ajuda ›
  Tópicos de ajuda…, with the two texts Front HEADER changed: the "Saving your work" item names Salvar investigação at the top of
  every page, Restaurar investigação (JSON) in the menu beside it and «O que um backup inclui»; the "Load Saved Data..." row names
  Restaurar investigação (JSON) in the menu Mais). With them all 33 maps are complete (`pt-br.test.ts` now checks that every map
  of `MAP_IDS` is in `COMPLETE_MAPS`).
- **The neutral English of §1.2 #8:** `HELP_VIEWER_EN.viewTranslation` is "View translation" (was "View translation (Spanish)"),
  `helpAdaptationTranslation` says "OpenVireo's translation" (was "Spanish translation") and `linkNoteTranslation` names "the
  translated "Related Topics" line" (was "Temas relacionados"). These texts are shown only in a language that translates the help
  files, so no English screen changes; the Spanish map is unchanged.

**How it was translated.** Six Sonnet 5.5 batches (`model: "sonnet"`), from the English, with the reviewed Spanish help only as a
meaning reference and a model of the format, under PT1–PT6's style brief (você and its commands, Brazilian vocabulary and
post-1990 spelling, the IAU OAE pt-BR terms, sentence case, VIREO names from `originals.json` and the glossary in Portuguese with
VIREO's English in parentheses at the first mention in each section): VIRMNHLP with the two reminders; PULSRHLP with PLSLSHLP and
PULSRLST; PLSANHLP, PULSRANL, SPECHLP and SPECLNS; PHOTOHLP, OBSRVHLP and CAMHLP (coupled: their rows point into ObsrvHlp.txt);
CLASSHLP and MEASHLP; HRANALHLP, VARTYPEHLP, WDGPLTHLP and the eight index-only files. The two maps were written by the Opus
implementer, every quoted label looked up in the pt-BR catalog, maps and originals. The implementer read every line of the 38
modules against the English and corrected, across batches:
- **One name per thing:** VIREO's Radio Pulsar Analysis Tool is «Ferramenta de análise de radiopulsares» everywhere (VIRMNHLP
  said «de pulsares de rádio»); slider switches are «interruptores deslizantes» and the photometer's Reading switch «o interruptor
  "Leitura"» (OBSRVHLP, CAMHLP and PHOTOHLP said «chave», while VIRMNHLP, PULSRHLP and the interface say «interruptor»); the
  optical slew buttons are "N","S","L","O" (and «os botões "NSLO"»), as PULSRHLP and the originals (`E` › «L», `W` › «O»)
  write them; the astrometry index's Blink rows use PT6's «piscagem» («Comparar imagens por piscagem», «Procedimento de
  piscagem», «Ajustes da piscagem», «Comparar uma sub-região por piscagem»; were «Alternar imagens (blink)»); the classification
  display's grayscale is «tons de cinza», as its page («Foto em tons de cinza», «Inverter tons de cinza fotográficos»).
- **Wording:** a stray English gloss removed («Pressione "OK" (Ok)» in PULSRLST); paragraphs rewrapped at 72 characters after
  these edits.
- **Kept from the batches:** «Dados a partir das observações» for Data from Observations (the glossary's and the cluster window's
  form), «ponto de saída» for the turnoff (as PT4), «Visão» for VIREO's View slider (as Spanish kept «Vista»), «Fazer login» and
  «Executar exercício» (as the helpMenu map), «Observatório virtual educacional» in sentence case. VIREO's NOTE label, which the
  batches wrote «NOTA» or «OBSERVAÇÃO», is «NOTA» everywhere after the review (below).

**Interface names rechecked against main** (4645853, unchanged during this front): a scan of every «"Português" (English)» pair in
the 38 modules found 220 that match `originals.json`/the glossary and one homonym (CLASSHLP's Open button «Abrir», not the wedge
plot's hollow point style «Vazado»); the 60 names with no entry (VIREO windows OpenVireo replaces with its own pages, the
photometer's and classification window's VIREO captions) were checked against the pt-BR catalog where the web page shows them
(«Filtro», «Integrações», «Aproximar»/«Afastar», «Taxa de deslocamento», «Aumentar zoom»/«Reduzir zoom», «Propriedades»). Every
label `webHelp` quotes was looked up in the pt-BR catalog, maps and originals, including the page bar's («Salvar investigação»,
«Mais», «Restaurar investigação (JSON)», «O que um backup inclui»). A later change to one of those names must also change the help
texts that cite it.

**Tests.**
- `src/help/helpTranslation.test.ts`: the structure checks run once per language whose registry entry has `help` (Spanish keeps its
  describe title and its 91 tests; Portuguese runs the same checks with its own marker), and a new block runs the pt-BR profile's
  text rules (its typography patterns: European, Spanish and English words, guillemets, title case, «estar a» + infinitive; the
  você register's patterns; the glossary's `forbidden`) over every paragraph of every Portuguese help text, with an empty
  allow-list.
- `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`: `helpViewer` and `webHelp` in `ENGLISH_MAPS` and `COMPLETE_MAPS`, the registry entry's `help`,
  and the profile lint over the two maps (one `identical.json` row: `map:helpViewer.spectralLines.ok`, OK as Windows pt-BR writes it).
- `tests/i18n/es-help.spec.ts` → `help.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over the translated languages with help: the Spanish tests keep
  their ten titles and every assertion (their Spanish literals now come from `originals.json`, the help viewer and web help maps,
  and `languageText.ts` `HELP_TEXT`, which holds the few help-file words the spec looks for); pt-BR runs the same ten in its
  `?lang=pt-BR` preview.
- `tests/release/pages-smoke.pages.ts`: the ES-HELP help-chunk smoke runs for es-419 and pt-BR and also checks that each fetched
  chunk names the language of the load (both sets have chunks of the same names).

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`, compared with main 4645853 built the same way). pt-BR: 38 help chunks, 150.7 KB
raw, 54.4 KB gzip in all (largest VIRMNHLP.TXT 22.1 KB raw / 7.0 KB gzip, PULSRHLP.TXT 20.6 / 6.7), none loaded or preloaded by
`index.html`; the core chunk 104.5 KB gzip (336.1 KB raw; 99.4 before: the `webHelp` and `helpViewer` maps), 13 chunks 239.5 KB
gzip in all (budgets: core 120/150, the sum 300/360 KB). Spanish: all 13 chunks and all 38 help chunks byte-identical to main,
names included. English: with chunk hashes normalised, two files differ: the help viewer chunk (`CleaHelp`, the neutral English
above and the 38 new loader entries) and the entry chunk (the registry's pt-BR `help` marker); every other file is identical.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-pthelp` from main 4645853). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`,
`src/seo`, `src/components` and `src/core/cleaHelp.test.ts`: 867 tests in 36 files; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run
build` with every check (no warning from `check-i18n-dist.mjs`); Playwright (dev server on port 5453, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig):
`help` 20 (10 Spanish, 10 pt-BR), and `shell`, `page-bar` and `exports-identity` 57; `npm run test:scripts` 59; `npm run test:pages`
on `dist/` 52. The pt-BR 320 px screenshots of the classification help topic and the Spectral Line Table were inspected.

**Independent review (PT-HELP).** All 38 modules were read in Portuguese, the exercise files (VIRMNHLP, PULSRHLP, PULSRANL,
PULSRLST, SPECHLP, SPECLNS, CLASSHLP, MEASHLP, PHOTOHLP, OBSRVHLP, CAMHLP, HRANALHLP, WDGPLTHLP) closely and against the English
where a wording was in doubt, and the index files against theirs; both maps were read and every label `webHelp` quotes was found in
the pt-BR catalog, maps or originals. The translation holds: você throughout, Brazilian vocabulary and post-1990 spelling, the IAU OAE
pt-BR terms (desvio para o vermelho, aglomerado, isócrona, sequência principal de idade zero, ascensão reta, radiotelescópio), VIREO's
names with their English at first mention (a re-scan found 212 pairs matching `originals.json`/the glossary and the rest VIREO window
names with no entry), VIREO's file conventions (72 columns, titles ≤ 65, Latin-1, LF), no claim to be CLEA's. Corrected: 40 lines in 8
modules:
- **NOTE:** VIREO's NOTE label is «NOTA» in every file (17 «OBSERVAÇÃO» changed, in CAMHLP, CLASSHLP, PHOTOHLP, PULSRANL and
  VIRMNHLP, with VIRMNHLP's two «veja a NOTA abaixo»): in an astronomy help, «OBSERVAÇÃO» also means an observation, the catalog's web
  notes say «Nota», and Spanish says «NOTA»; five of the changed paragraphs were rewrapped.
- **Meaning:** SPECLNS.TXT's "Si just visible at B3" and "Just visible at B3" read «O Si é pouco visível em B3» and «Pouco visível em
  B3.» (were «apenas visível», which in Portuguese means "only visible", a Spanish *apenas* carried over).
- **Topic names cited across files** now match the titles they cite: MEASHLP «"Salvar o espectro para análise"» (SPECHLP's title;
  was «Salvar espectro para análise») and OBSRVHLP «"Trocar de telescópio óptico"» (VIRMNHLP's title; was «Alterar o telescópio
  óptico»).
- **Spelling:** «radio-observatório» (post-1990 hyphen before the same vowel; was «radioobservatório»); the unit «angstroms» in lower
  case in CLASSHLP and MEASHLP, as SPECHLP writes it (three «Angstroms»); PHOTOHLP's «bem posicionado, .» (VIREO's own stray comma)
  ends with a full stop.
- **Spec:** `help.spec.ts` matches the no-break space as `\u00a0` again (the rename had turned the escape into a literal character).

Judgments kept: «Visão» for VIREO's View slider (the pt-BR catalog says «visão do telescópio», «Visão completa»); «ponto de saída»
for the turnoff (PT4); «estrelas de tipo precoce/tardio»; «2 400 000» in PULSRANL's property list, as the reviewed Spanish writes it
(the number-format sentence is the coordinator's, below). The neutral English of `HELP_VIEWER_EN` shows only where a language
translates the help (`helpTranslationOffered()` gates every `ViewSwitch` and translation note), so no English screen changes; the
Spanish map keeps its shape and text. `help.spec.ts` keeps every Spanish assertion: each literal it now reads from `originals.json`,
the help viewer and web help maps or `HELP_TEXT` equals the one it replaced. On the built `dist/`, an `en-US` and a `pt-BR` browser
load of the classification help fetch no translated help chunk (the pt-BR browser stays English before the release), an `es-MX` load
fetches only es-419 chunks, and the pages smoke checks each fetched chunk's language. Review verification on the reviewed head:
`npx tsc -b`; Vitest over the same files, 867 tests in 36 files; the pins refreshed with no change; `npm run build` with every check
(pt-BR help 38 chunks, 54.4 KB gzip; core 104.5 KB gzip); Playwright `help`, `shell`, `page-bar` and `clea-help` 67; `npm run
test:scripts` 59; `npm run test:pages` 52.

**Not done here.** The plan's §3.1 sentence for the web help, "Numbers use a decimal point and commas between thousands, as VIREO's
files do", would add a block to `webHelp` in every language, so it changes the public Spanish and English help; it is left to the
coordinator (PT-REL or a decision). PT-REL: `released:true` with the strict gates.

## RTL groundwork: direction, the qps-rtl pseudo-locale and test fonts (RT1–RT3)

Front RTL-1 (plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-rtl.md` §§ 5.1, 6.3, 7, 9). The user decided on 2026-11-10 to prepare the
right-to-left groundwork only: no Arabic, Hebrew or Persian translation and no place in the language order. Nothing an English,
Spanish or Portuguese visitor sees changes; the one difference in their pages is `<html dir="ltr">` (below). The plan's rule for
what follows: **mirror the page, never the data** (plots, sky and image geometry, numbers, file names and native mirrors stay
left-to-right islands; RT5).

- **RT1, the registry direction.** `LanguageEntry.direction` (`src/i18n/languages.ts`) is optional, `ltr` or `rtl`; absent means
  `ltr`, and no entry of `src/i18n/languages.json` sets it today (the file is unchanged). `dir` stays the folder name.
  `textDirection(tag)` reads it. `src/i18n/locale.ts` has `HTML_DIR` beside `HTML_LANG` (ltr for English, every registered language
  and every pseudo-locale but `qps-rtl`) and `htmlDir(language, registry)`; `src/i18n/index.ts` exports `uiDirection()` for code
  that places things by side (RT6). `settleLanguage` calls the page's optional `setDir` beside `setLang`, with the **activated**
  language, so a right-to-left load whose chunk failed is English and `ltr`; `src/boot.ts` sets `document.documentElement.dir`. No
  inline script, no CSP change, `index.html` unchanged. `src/i18n/direction.test.ts` requires each entry's direction (the registry,
  the planned languages, the test language) to equal the direction `Intl.Locale(htmlLang)` gives, so a wrong value fails; the test
  registry's new `RTL` list (`tests/fixtures/i18n/registry.ts`: `ar` for every region, `he` with `iw`, `fa`) checks detection,
  `<html dir="rtl">` and the English `ltr` after a failed chunk.
- **What `<html dir="ltr">` changes.** Measured in Chromium on `#spectrum` at 320 and 1440 px for `en`, `es-419` and `pt-BR`, every
  computed property and box of every element with and without the attribute: one difference, the root's `unicode-bidi` (`normal`
  → `isolate`, the HTML `[dir]` rule), with no geometry change. The production build differs from main only in the JavaScript that
  sets it (below).
- **RT2, `qps-rtl`.** A dev-only pseudo-locale in `src/i18n/pseudoRtl.ts`, registered beside `qps-cjk`, `qps-deva` and `qps-cyrl`
  (`PSEUDO_SCRIPT`), so it has the same gate: accepted only from `?lang=qps-rtl` in the dev server and tests, never stored, detected
  or offered, no selector, English plural and number rules, files unchanged. `<html lang="ar" dir="rtl">`. The transform: each English
  word of n letters becomes round(1.25 n) Arabic letters, word-stable, with a non-joiner (ا د ذ ر ز و) about one letter in four so
  every joining form occurs, and a haraka (fatha, kasra or shadda with fatha) in about one word in four. Kept in Latin, to stress
  bidi: `{params}` and `<tag>` placeholders, numbers with their decimal point and separators, ALL-CAPS tokens (`CCD`, `QSOs`), tokens
  with a digit or an inner `.`, `/` or `_` (`Hubble.HTL`, `km/s`, `M82`), and a whole parenthesised group (the «Arabic (English
  VIREO name)» shape). `,` `;` `?` become ، ؛ ؟; `.` and `…` stay. It writes no bidi control: isolation is RT4's runtime. The plan's
  0.9 letters per English letter drew the catalog 1.00× as wide as English in Chromium; 1.25 draws it 1.25× (Noto Sans Arabic named
  first) to 1.29× (the site's stack, where DejaVu Sans draws Arabic), the plan's 1.2–1.3× target. Checks: `src/i18n/pseudoRtl.test.ts`
  (properties over the catalog, the map leaves and the VIREO originals; the gate; settle; maps; `vireo()`; numbers and three export
  hashes) and `tests/scripts-pseudo.smoke.spec.ts` (the home page renders with `<html lang="ar" dir="rtl">`, `direction:rtl` on the
  body, Arabic text, no selector and no error; English has `dir="ltr"` and no Arabic). Today it shows that the site has no direction
  support yet: the text is right-to-left but the sidebar stays on the left (fixed with physical offsets) and many physical margins and
  alignments do not mirror. That is RT5; the route walk under `qps-rtl` is RT7.
- **RT3, test fonts.** `scripts/test-fonts.mjs` (profile `scripts`) also links Noto Sans Arabic (the non-UI face on purpose: its
  2.112 em line is the worst case) and Noto Sans Hebrew, Regular and Bold, with fontconfig rules `ar`, `fa`, `ur` → Noto Sans Arabic
  and `he` → Noto Sans Hebrew. Ubuntu 24.04's `fonts-noto-core`, which the CI job `scripts` already installs, ships the four files in
  `/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto` (checked in the noble file list), so the workflow is unchanged; Arch's `noto-fonts` has them in
  `/usr/share/fonts/noto`. `tests/scripts/fonts.scripts.ts` adds, measured 2026-11-10 with Chromium of `@playwright/test` 1.64.1:
  - A stack that names the Noto face first draws the script from it, with Arabic's ASCII digits, space and `, - . :`, while Latin
    letters and `/ ( )` come from DejaVu Sans; a named family reaches the Bold files at 550 and 700; Arabic is shaped (four joined
    beh are narrower than four isolated ones in Noto Sans Arabic and in DejaVu Sans).
  - **Characterization (d), which differs from the plan's expectation:** DejaVu Sans covers Arabic and Hebrew and DejaVu Sans Mono
    covers Arabic, so under `sans-serif`, `monospace`, the site's token stacks and even `"DejaVu Sans","Noto Sans Arabic"`, DejaVu draws
    them for `lang` ar, he and en: no fallback happens. The fontconfig rules cannot change that; **RT5's `:lang(ar)`, `:lang(fa)` and
    `:lang(he)` stacks must name the Noto face before `system-ui`**, not after the Latin fonts as SC3 does for CJK, and then Arabic's
    ASCII digits come from Noto Sans Arabic too.
  - **(e)** Where the first face lacks the script (DejaVu Serif has neither; DejaVu Sans Mono has no Hebrew), the per-character
    fallback follows the language through those rules (ar and fa → Noto Sans Arabic, he → Noto Sans Hebrew, en → DejaVu Sans), and
    stays on the Regular file at 700, as for CJK (c).
- **The SC3 flake.** "en at 320 px computes the same styles with and without scripts.css" once counted 1890 elements, then 1896, in
  CI. Reproduced here under `--repeat-each=3 --workers=2`, where it failed on most runs: the six elements are the Multi-Spec card
  (`src/multispec/MultiSpecLazy.tsx`), a chunk loaded in an effect and mounted in the observatory view every route keeps, which could
  arrive after the walk's two agreeing snapshots, on a busy dev server, and so appear only in the snapshot without the sheet (the
  language selector and View original option, the suspected cause, are another such chunk). The walk now waits for the three lazy
  shell parts (`ms-card`, `language-choice`, `language-originals-choice`, shown for English and Spanish on every route), fails if two
  snapshots in a row never agree, and accepts a comparison only when the page with the sheet is the same before and after it (the
  same `<style>` node is put back and the page snapshotted again; a change in between retries, up to five times). The comparison
  itself is unchanged.

**Deferred** (plan § 7, other sub-fronts): `dir` on the selector's `<option>` and on the own-language half of a failure notice
(`LanguageChoice.tsx`, `OpenVireo.tsx`; with the first RTL language); SEO `dir` (RT9); the bidi runtime (RT4); logical CSS, islands
and the Arabic stacks (RT5); direction-aware popup and tour placement (RT6); the `qps-rtl` route walk (RT7).

**Front RTL-1 review** (independent, 2026-11-10):
- **`<html dir="ltr">` kept for every load.** Re-measured: every computed property and box of every element on the home page,
  `#spectrum` and `#observatory`, at 320 and 1440 px, for `en`, `es-419` and `pt-BR`, with the attribute and after removing it
  (a stable page first): Chromium differs only in the root's `unicode-bidi` (`isolate` → `normal`), Firefox in nothing (18 of 18
  identical). `unicode-bidi` does not inherit and the root is a block, so the isolate has no effect on layout, selection or print;
  no CSS selector or script reads `dir`, `:dir()` or `document.dir`. Setting `dir` only for rtl would save nothing and leave the
  direction implicit for code that later reads `<html dir>`. WebKit could not be launched on the review machine (missing host
  libraries), so it is reasoned, not measured: its UA sheet has the same `[dir]` rule as Chromium's.
- **The pseudo-locale name rule** (deferred above): `scripts/check-i18n-dist.mjs` now also fails a build whose text files name a
  dev-only pseudo-locale (`qps-` followed by letters, not after a letter, digit, `-` or `_`, so a chunk hash holding `qps-` is no
  name; the production code's own `startsWith('qps-')` and `/^qps(?:-|$)/i` are allowed). `src/i18n/distPseudo.test.ts` fails without
  it. The real build passes (1477 text files): the only `qps` strings in `dist/` are those generic tests and `qps` itself.
- **CI fix**: the shaping test pinned Noto Sans Arabic's glyph count for four joined beh (8, Arch's noto-fonts); Ubuntu 24.04's
  fonts-noto-core 20201225 draws them in 4, which failed the CI job `Script fonts`. The test now checks that one face draws them
  (and the width), not the count.
- `htmlDir()` reads only HTML_DIR's own entries (`constructor` and other Object properties give `ltr`).

## Script groundwork: Unicode help (SC7)

Plan: `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-scripts.md` §1 and the SC7 row of §12. A translated help set may now be Unicode text in any
script (Russian, Hindi, Japanese, Chinese). VIREO's 38 bundled originals are read exactly as before: same bytes, hashes and provenance,
same parsers, same rendering.

**Text cores, byte wrappers.** `src/core/cleaHelp.ts`, `src/help/spectralLines.ts` and `src/help/mainHelp.ts` each have one string core
behind two entry points:
- the byte functions (`parseHelpList`, `helpTopicBody`, `parseSpectralLineList`, `spectralLineHelp`, `reminderBoxText`) keep their byte
  limits, decode Latin-1 and call the core with VIREO's `wrapHelpLine`, which still refuses an unbroken line over 72 characters;
- the new text functions (`parseHelpListText`, `helpTopicBodyText`, `parseSpectralLineListText`, `spectralLineHelpText`,
  `reminderBoxTextOf`) take a translation's text (the same limits, counted in UTF-16 units) and wrap with `wrapHelpText`.
The viewers (`CleaHelp.tsx`, `SpectralLineTable.tsx`, the Exit Observatory reminder in `HelpMenus.tsx`) hold a file as a `HelpSource`
(`Uint8Array` for an original, `string` for a translation) and pick the entry point by its type.

**Columns.** `src/core/textColumns.ts`: `displayColumns` counts East Asian Wide and Fullwidth characters as 2 columns, `\p{Mn}`,
`\p{Me}` and `\p{Cf}` as 0, and everything else as 1. Every character up to U+00FF counts 1, so Latin-1 text measures its length. The
W/F table is Unicode 16.0.0 (122 runs), generated from the local Python standard library (the command is in the module), not
downloaded. Departure from the plan: spacing marks (`\p{Mc}`, Devanagari ा ि ो) count 1, because they advance the pen; counting them 0
would let a Hindi line overflow. `cutColumns` cuts at whole grapheme clusters (`Intl.Segmenter`; code points without it) and is
`slice` on Latin-1 text. All of VIREO's limits use it: the 80-column LST line, titles ≤ 65, file tokens ≤ 71, the 72-column wrap,
SPECLNS's 35/12 fields, and the reminder's 255/1023. The reminder stops adding once a part does not fit (StrLCat on a full buffer),
which with Latin-1 text is the old cut exactly; CR LF is one cluster.

**Wrap.** `wrapHelpText` never refuses. A piece ends at the last break opportunity within 72 columns:
- after a space, by VIREO's rule (a space run dropped when the space ends exactly at the width);
- or between two clusters when either is wide (CJK), unless simplified kinsoku forbids it: no line starts with
  `、。，．・：；？！ー）」』】〕〉》｝］` (and similar), a small kana or a danda, and none ends with `（「『【〔〈《｛［`.
With no opportunity, it breaks at the last cluster that fits, stepping back for kinsoku; when the cluster just past the width is a space,
the piece ends there instead and the space run is dropped (review: this avoided a row of one space between two long Devanagari words,
and a CJK break before the last character that fits), and a single cluster wider than the width is a row of its own (review: it
left an empty last row). It is linear in the line's clusters (review: the first version re-measured the rest of the line for each
piece, 300 ms for a 12,000-character line). On Latin-1 text that `wrapHelpLine` wraps, the result is identical (a 2000-case property
test over words and spaces, and a 6000-case one over every Latin-1 character, added by the review). Translators still pre-wrap at 72 columns; the wrap is a safety net for CJK lines and
long Devanagari or Russian words.

**Search.** `foldHelpText` is the translation view's fold (Find Topic, Search Text, related-topic links), per script and with no
per-language registry field:
- **Latin:** as `foldLatin1Accents` (accents dropped, ñ kept, case folded).
- **Full-width/half-width forms:** NFKC (U+3000, U+FF01–U+FFEE: ＡＢＣ finds abc, ｶﾞ finds が).
- **Other scripts:** keep their marks (й, Devanagari vowel signs) and compare NFC, with case folded where the script has case.
- **Cyrillic:** ё reads as е.
- **Japanese:** katakana reads as hiragana.
- **Devanagari:** the nukta and ZWJ/ZWNJ are ignored.
- **Han:** compared as written (no Simplified↔Traditional conversion).
The text is composed (NFC) first. On any text of characters ≤ U+00FF it equals `foldLatin1Accents`; a test checks all 256 characters
and every line of the Spanish and Portuguese help sets and of the originals, so the Spanish and Portuguese rows fold as before. Only
a query typed with characters outside Latin-1 can fold differently, and only to find more: a decomposed ñ now stays ñ (it was read as
n, and so missed «año»), a decomposed accent or a full-width letter (ａ) now finds its Latin-1 letter, and a non-Latin mark (й) is
kept, which no Spanish or Portuguese row holds anyway (review, 2026-11-10). Search is a substring search with no word segmentation.
"Match case" compares exactly, as before; in the translation view the query is NFC-normalised first (so a decomposed é also finds é
with Match case on). The original view keeps VIREO's
`foldAsciiCase`. `HELP_VIEWER_EN.searchFoldNote` is unchanged; each new language's `helpViewer` map must say what the search folds in
its script.

**Loader.** `helpTranslationBytes` is now `helpTranslationText`: the same checks of language, source name and SHA-256, and a string
text that is NFC with no lone surrogate. The U+00FF limit is gone. The Spanish and Portuguese sets stay Latin-1 by their own test (the
`LATIN1_HELP` list in `helpTranslation.test.ts`). A Unicode set must be NFC with no CR or tab, and keep its titles within 65 columns and
its lines within 72. The generalised structure tests run on the text parsers, and the Latin-1 sets are also checked to read the same
through the text and byte parsers. The viewers take their set from `useHelpSet()`: the registry language's (`registryHelpSet()`:
`help.related`, `import.meta.glob('../i18n/*/help/*.ts')`), or a test fixture given through `HelpSetContext`. The cache key now names the
set's tag.

**Build check.** `scripts/check-i18n-dist.mjs` already finds help chunks by name, SHA-256 and the language tag they name (all ASCII),
so it accepts a Unicode help set. The one change: a help chunk's budget (warn 25 KB, fail 40 KB gzip) is multiplied by its language's
`budgetFactor`, as its other chunks' budgets are, because UTF-8 takes 2–3 bytes per Cyrillic, Devanagari or CJK character. Spanish and
Portuguese (factor 1) keep 40 KB. `src/i18n/distCheck.test.ts` checks 38 help chunks of Japanese, Devanagari and Cyrillic text, and an
incompressible 45 KB Han chunk that fails at factor 1 and only warns at 1.5.
Review (2026-11-10): the harness and the fixtures were kept out of the build only by the harness plugin's `apply:'serve'` and by no
production module importing `tests/`; nothing would have failed if one leaked. `check-i18n-dist.mjs` now also fails any `dist/` file
that holds a test-only text (`testOnlyMarkers`): the harness path `/__harness/`, each harness page title of
`tests/harness/vite-plugin.ts`, and each fixture language tag (`tag:'q…'` under `tests/fixtures`: qaa-Jpan, qab-Deva, qac-Cyrl,
qaa-XA) that the checked registry does not hold. `distCheck.test.ts` checks the markers and three leaks; the real build has none.

**Fixture, not a language.** `tests/fixtures/help-unicode/{ja,hi,ru}.ts` are test-only stand-ins (tags `qaa-Jpan`, `qab-Deva`,
`qac-Cyrl` from the local-use range; `htmlLang` ja, hi, ru). Each covers CAMHLP.LST, CAMHLP.TXT and Reminder.obs, with the original's
rows, kinds, file tokens and section order, and deliberately long lines: unbroken CJK, a surrogate pair (𠮷), nukta letters, an
unbroken Devanagari compound of more than 72 columns, ё. They are not reviewed and are never in the build. They are reached by:
- `src/help/unicodeHelp.test.ts` (structure parity with the originals, links, wrap, fold, reminder);
- the dev-only harness `/__harness/help/?set=ja|hi|ru` (`tests/harness/help.tsx`, docs/TEST-HARNESSES.md): the real `CleaHelp` and
  Exit Observatory dialog under `HelpSetContext`, with the app's stylesheets including SC3's `scripts.css`.
The harness is exercised by:
- `tests/help-unicode.spec.ts`: rows, wrapped topic, related link, Back, the original view with `lang="en"`, width/kana/nukta/case/ё
  search, Match case, Print Topic, fallback to the original for an untranslated file, the reminder;
- `tests/scripts/help-unicode.scripts.ts`, under the pinned test fonts: layout and vertical ink at 320 and 1440 px for the topic list,
  a wrapped topic, its print preview and the reminder, and the platform face of the help text (Noto Sans CJK JP, Noto Sans Devanagari,
  DejaVu Sans).
The SC2 pseudo-locales do not reach the help viewer (they have no help set, so it shows VIREO's originals). A pseudo help set would need
`src/i18n/pseudo*.ts` and boot, which RTL-1 edits now; the fixtures cover the same scripts.

**Not done here (later fronts).**
- **Translated column tables in Devanagari/CJK** (plan §1 item 9): only SPECLNS uses `.cluster-hr-columns`, and its names stay
  VIREO's.
- **The section seek and the Spectral Line seek** keep VIREO's `foldAsciiCase`. A translation's header must contain its row title as
  written; the structure tests check every seek.
- **`HelpViewerMessages`'s fold note** per language: each language's help front writes it.

**Verification (2026-11-10).** `npx tsc -b`. Vitest over `src/help`, `src/i18n`, `src/core/cleaHelp.test.ts` and
`src/core/textColumns.test.ts`: 835 tests in 30 files. The pins were refreshed with no change (`dynamic-key-sites.json` keeps the
loader's `load` site). The full Vitest suite timed out on 11 unrelated tests while the machine's load was about 25. All of them pass
when rerun alone; `cameraParameters` needed `--testTimeout=60000` and takes 11 s at that load.

`npm run build` passed every check. Its 76 Spanish and Portuguese help chunks are byte-identical, by name and SHA-256, to a build of
`main` b22a7d5 made in the same tree. Playwright `i18n/help`, `clea-help`, `cluster-hr-help`, `page-bar` and `help-unicode`: 58.
`npm run test:scripts`: 65, with `help-unicode.scripts.ts`.

**Originals unchanged.** The originals are untouched: `public/` is unchanged, and the Front H and V2 witnesses (`helpAll.test.ts`,
`cleaHelp.test.ts`) pass unchanged. A new test reads every original LST, every topic body, SPECLNS and both reminders through the text
cores and checks that they equal the byte parsers.

## Brazilian Portuguese: the release (PT-REL)

The plan's PT-REL item (`analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md` §2.2, §4 and §6 in the research root) after PT0–PT6 and PT-HELP
merged (main b22a7d5): the catalog (3,567 keys), all 33 maps, every VIREO original and the 38 help modules complete. User decision 5
of 2026-11-09 (AGENTS.md): Portuguese turns on once all of its sub-fronts are in. Front ES-REL is the model; SC8's registry made the
release one field.

**Detection and the selector.** `src/i18n/languages.json`: pt-BR has `"released":true`. One load decides once, unchanged: `?lang=en`,
`?lang=es-419` or `?lang=pt-BR` exactly (that load only, not stored), then the stored choice (`en`, `es-419` or `pt-BR`), then the
first browser language: `pt`, `pt-BR`, `pt-PT`, `pt-AO`, any `pt-*` in any case (`PT-br`) open pt-BR (plan §4: the selector names the
variant, «Português (Brasil)», and English is one click away); `gl` and a browser whose first language is another (`['en-US','pt-BR']`)
stay as they were; `['es-MX','pt-BR']` opens Spanish and `['pt-BR','es-MX']` Portuguese. `?lang=pt`, `?lang=pt-br` and `?lang=pt-PT`
are no tag. The selector lists English, Español and Português (Brasil) on every load, each in its own `lang`. A change from Portuguese
is stored as for Spanish (no longer a preview: the preview note no longer shows for pt-BR). `<html lang="pt-BR">`, the title and the
failure notices are PT0's.

**Strict catalog and maps.** As for Spanish (ES-REL): a whole released bundle builds strictly and a split one (every production
Portuguese load) keeps the English fallback its per-module catalog needs, so strictness is static. `src/i18n/pt-br/pt-br.test.ts`
now checks `createI18n(pt,'pt-BR')` without fallback, every map of `MAP_IDS` against its English shape without `partial`, and the
registry line (`released:true`, `thousands`). The planner reports a released language whose catalog does not cover en.json, and its
plan's `complete` is true (`split.test.ts` item 7); `check-i18n-dist.mjs` requires marker text in every pt-BR module and no empty
area. `node scripts/i18n-coverage.mjs --lang pt-BR`: 3567/3567 keys, 33/33 maps, 744 originals, "0 render sites or module literals
still pending".

**Thousands on screen (user decision of 2026-11-10, recorded in AGENTS.md).** Portuguese separates thousands with a narrow
no-break space (U+202F) on screen: «24 000», «50 000», «2 400 000», «31 147». The decimal point stays (decision 4); Spanish, English
and the pseudo-locales keep «24,000»; files the site writes never pass through it, so the exports identity families stay
byte-identical (they ran in pt-BR, below).
- **Per language, in the registry:** an optional `thousands` field (pt-BR: `" "`; `languages.ts` `thousandsSeparator`).
  `createI18n` takes it as an option, and its `formatNumber` then formats with en-US and replaces each `group` part of
  `formatToParts`; `activateLocale` and `extendLocale` pass the active language's separator. `src/i18n/index.ts` `formatNumber` now
  reads the active language when called (it was bound to the English instance); `fixed` and `scientific` stay English (they never
  group). Every grouping call site is a `formatNumber` call without `useGrouping:false` (counts in the hot-list editor, target picker,
  field and spectrum counts, the feature cursor's ticks, the Spectrum Save print's wavelengths, the optical telescope's figures); the
  sites that feed inputs or measured values already pass `useGrouping:false`. The bare `toLocaleString()` sites format print dates,
  not numbers.
- **The maps:** the variable-star map's two `toLocaleString('en-US')` counts (`typeOption`, `results`) group through a local
  `grouped()` with U+202F in `src/i18n/pt-br/maps/variableStars.ts` (Spanish and English unchanged).
- **Literals:** 28 numbers in 25 strings: `pt-br.json` (16 keys: the 1 000-measurement log, 24 000 replay events, 50 000 samples, the
  1 000 000 photon draws, 2 147 483 647 counts, 5 000 callbacks, 86 000 bytes / 1 000 records, 10 000 lines, JD − 2 400 000…), the maps
  `variableStars` (31 147), `radioAntenna` (86 000 / 1 000), `wedge` (10 000) and `pulsar` (JD − 2 400 000, twice), and three
  `originals.json` values (the keys stay VIREO's English). Two of them used an ordinary space already («10 000 linhas», «10 000
  registros»), now U+202F. A map source must hold the character itself, not a ` ` escape: `check-i18n-dist.mjs` reads map literals
  as written, and the escaped form made the first build miss one core marker.
- **The help files** are Latin-1 (`helpTranslationBytes` refuses a character above U+00FF until SC7's Unicode help), so PULSRANL.TXT's
  «2 400 000» uses U+00A0, a no-break space Latin-1 has; the Spanish help is unchanged.
- **The lint:** two pt-BR typography rules (`src/i18n/lint/profiles.ts`), so a new string cannot regress: «comma between thousands»
  (`\p{N}{1,3}(,\p{N}{3})+`) and «ordinary space between thousands» (U+0020; U+00A0 and U+202F pass), each with a mutation fixture
  (`fixtures.ts`, three rows), applied to the catalog, maps and originals (`pt-br.test.ts`) and to every paragraph of the 38 help
  texts (`helpTranslation.test.ts` runs the profile's typography patterns). `pt-br.test.ts` also checks the formatter (24000, 50000,
  2400000, 999, 1234.5, −31147, no grouping with `useGrouping:false`, `fixed`/`scientific` unchanged, English «24,000») and the
  variable-star counts.
- **The web help:** one sentence in the Portuguese «O que é o OpenVireo» topic (map `webHelp`, Portuguese only; English and Spanish
  help unchanged): «Na tela, os números usam ponto decimal e um espaço fino entre os milhares (por exemplo, 1.5 e 24 000); digite os
  decimais também com ponto. Os arquivos que o site grava ficam em inglês, como o VIREO os escreve.» This replaces PT-HELP's open
  item (the plan's §3.1 sentence for every language, which predated the decision).

**Tests.** The Portuguese specs open Portuguese by the browser language alone, as ES-REL did for Spanish.
- `tests/fixtures/language.ts`: `PORTUGUESE_LOCALE` (`pt-BR`); `langQuery(tag)` is empty for every released language (it was empty for
  Spanish only), and `contextLocale`/`contextQuery` (already written for the release) now give pt-BR its own browser. `languageText.ts`:
  `PORTUGUESE_TEXT.query` is empty.
- The language-parameterised specs (`shell`, `observatory`, `e01`–`e06`, `e08`, `radio`, `help`): each top-level `for(const L of
  TRANSLATED)` (or `HELP_LANGUAGES`) body is wrapped in an anonymous `test.describe` with `test.use({locale:contextLocale(L.tag)})`, so
  the Spanish tests still run in es-MX (titles and assertions unchanged; anonymous describes add no title) and the Portuguese ones in
  pt-BR; English references keep `?lang=en` and the pseudo-locale `?lang=qps`. The Portuguese titles lost "preview" («(pt-BR)»);
  `shell.spec.ts`'s "a ?lang=pt-BR preview fetches…" reads "a Portuguese browser fetches…", and its untranslated-text check, which
  opened both pages in an es-MX browser, uses the language's own.
- `exports-identity.spec.ts` already made each context with `contextLocale`/`contextQuery`: its pt-BR families now run in a pt-BR
  browser without `?lang=`.
- `areas.spec.ts`: PT0's "a partial language fetches only its core, and no chunk for an empty area" (an en-US browser with `?lang=pt-BR`)
  became "Brazilian Portuguese (released) fetches its core, and on each area route only that area's modules", in a pt-BR browser, with
  no empty area. The partial-language case stays covered by the qaa-XA fixture (`partialLanguage.test.ts`, `split.test.ts`).
- `language-selector.spec.ts`: three options with their `lang` on every load; PT0's preview test became the PT-REL detection test (pt,
  pt-BR, pt-PT, PT-br → pt-BR; `['pt-BR','es-MX']` → pt-BR; `['en-US','pt-BR']` → English; `['es-MX','pt-BR']` → Spanish; gl → English;
  a stored pt-BR beats an es-MX browser and a stored English a pt-BR one, `?lang=` beats both; a change from Portuguese to Spanish and
  back is stored with a clean address); the keyboard test runs in a pt-BR browser without `?lang=`. No end-to-end test of a preview
  remains for a real language (none is unreleased); the preview path keeps its Vitest coverage (`locale.test.ts`, `languages.test.ts`,
  `partialLanguage.test.ts`).
- `es-narrow.spec.ts` → `narrow.spec.ts` and `es-browsers.spec.ts` → `browsers.spec.ts` (`git mv`), looped over the released languages,
  each in its own browser: the Spanish titles unchanged, the Portuguese 320 px sweep (home, the 16 routes, the menu with "View original",
  the page bar's selector and its confirmation, OpenVireo's help and About) and the engine smoke (boot, a route area, a lazy dialog,
  the help viewer, the selector to English and back, home and a lab at 320 px), their text read from the language's sources.
- `tests/scripts/routes.scripts.ts`: the scripts.css computed-style walk gains pt-BR (320 and 1440 px).
- `tests/release/pages-smoke.pages.ts`: a language chunk is recognised for any registered language (`tags` records whose); every route
  of `dist/` in a pt-BR browser fetches the pt-BR core and exactly its area's modules and logs nothing; a pt-BR home shows the
  selector with three options under the release CSP; pt-PT and pt browsers open Portuguese and read «31 147» (the variable-star scope
  and the result line built by the map), es-MX and en-US read «31,147»; the 404 page is trilingual.
- Vitest: `locale.test.ts` (PT0's test became PT-REL's; `UNRELEASED` closes every language), `languages.test.ts` (both released, three
  selector options; the planned-languages table now has pt-BR released, so its gate rows use French), `partialLanguage.test.ts` (the
  fixture's selector lists pt-BR too), `tests/fixtures/i18n/registry.ts` (pt-BR as released).

**Release text.** `public/404.html` is trilingual (English, Spanish, then «Página não encontrada… Este endereço não faz parte do
OpenVireo. Abra o observatório.» under `lang="pt-BR"`, linking `/?lang=pt-BR`; the title adds «Não encontrada»). README and
THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt carry "Brazilian Portuguese translation: an OpenVireo web adaptation; CLEA's files and texts are unchanged."
(the notice paragraph mirrors Spanish's, with the same uncertainty about whether the recorded CLEA License.rtf terms cover a
translation). Help › About OpenVireo's "This site" paragraph already said so in Portuguese (PT1, map `polish`).

**Search engines.** Unchanged: nothing advertises a Portuguese URL yet. The sitemap, `hreflang` sets (en, es-419, x-default), the lab
pages, their JSON-LD and the static `index.html` are those of main (the build's `index.html` differs from main's only in chunk
hashes); `/pt-br/` and its alternates are PT-SEO's. As with Spanish, a crawler that renders the app with a Portuguese browser language
sees Portuguese at `/`, whose canonical and alternates still declare an English page.

**Sizes** (`npm run build`, `check-i18n-dist.mjs`, compared with main b22a7d5 built the same way, chunk hashes normalised). pt-BR: 13
chunks, 239.7 KB gzip in all (core 104.7 KB gzip, 336.4 raw; radio 40.4; classification 28.1; pulsar+radio 14.4; photometer 13.5;
lss 8.7; cluster 8.2; exposure 8.1; cluster+photometer 5.1; pulsar 3.9; fits 2.8; exposure+lss 1.3; classification+cluster 0.4),
none loaded by `index.html`; 38 help chunks, 54.4 KB gzip. Spanish: all 13 chunks and 38 help chunks byte-identical. English: only
the shared chunk with the i18n machinery changes (`jsx-runtime`, +228 B raw, +63 B gzip: the registry's pt-BR line and the
grouping formatter); `index.html` and every other file are identical but for chunk hashes. Deploy limits: 2,154 files.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-ptrel` from main b22a7d5). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n`, `src/help`,
`src/seo`, `src/components`, `src/core/cleaHelp.test.ts` and `src/deployHeaders.test.ts`: 881 tests in 37 files; the pins refreshed
with no change; `npm run build` with every check (the first build failed `check-i18n-dist.mjs` on the ` ` escape described
above). Playwright (dev server, Chromium, DejaVu fontconfig), every `tests/i18n/*.spec.ts`: 234 tests in 17 files (`e01` Spanish and
Portuguese and `e02` Spanish failed once in a three-file batch, a paused exposure's button still disabled and a chunk past its
timeout, and passed rerun alone: load flakes; `exports-identity` needed the two fixes above), with `page-bar`; English samples
(`app-shell`, `project-links`, `variable-stars`, the pseudo-locale smoke), 20 tests; `npm run test:scripts`, 61 tests (the English rows of the
scripts.css walk failed twice right after a dev-server start, a card appearing on the home page between the two snapshots, and passed

`mcr.microsoft.com/playwright:v1.64.1-noble` image, `--network host`, run as the user, removed after) ran `browsers`,
`language-selector` and `narrow`: WebKit 17 of 17; Firefox 15 of 17, the two 320 px sweeps finding the known 1 px overflow of the
analyser's x-axis title («Longitud de onda · Å», «Comprimento de onda · Å»), the fixed-y axis title ES-REL recorded for SC11.

**Left for later fronts.** PT-SEO: `/pt-br/`, `/pt-br/labs/`, `hreflang` (`pt-BR` and `pt`) and the Portuguese preview image. SC7: once
translated help is Unicode, PULSRANL's U+00A0 may become U+202F. SC11: the fixed-y plot axis titles. No end-to-end preview test
remains until the next language (French) is registered before its release.

**Independent review (PT-REL)** (2026-11-10, Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort, over 9c2927d; fixes in the same worktree). No
blocking finding. *English and Spanish numbers:* `formatNumber` now goes through `current()`; every caller is a render-time call
(none at module evaluation, none feeding an input value, a file or a parse), and a non-Portuguese instance builds the same

es-MX: every number token identical (en 3,321 distinct tokens, es 3,325; among them «4,627.5», «1,000», «1,000,000», «31,147» and the
variable-star type counts, unchanged). In pt-BR the same 17 pages show only U+202F groups («4 627.5», «1 000 000», «31 147», the variable-star type counts)
and no comma group. New Vitest (`infrastructure.test.ts`): through `index.ts`'s `formatNumber` with the Spanish, pseudo-locale and
Portuguese bundles activated, 14 values × 10 option sets (grouping, `useGrouping:false`, fraction digits, percent with sign,
scientific, compact) print exactly English's in Spanish and qps, and English's with only the digit-group comma swapped for U+202F

(«12000 eventos», «144000 bytes», «24000 eventos de replay») mirror English, which writes them ungrouped too, so they stay. One

error on the dev server's first WebKit load), 17 of 17 on the rerun and 5 of 5 alone.

## Brazilian Portuguese: search pages (PT-SEO)

User decision 8 of 2026-11-09 applied to pt-BR (the next-languages decision of the same day: each language follows the Spanish
architecture). Plan: `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-pt-br.md`, PT-SEO, and the ES-SEO note "For later languages" above. The pages
are described in `docs/SEO.md` ("Front PT-SEO"); this section records what touches the language rules.

**Data only, as ES-SEO designed.** A `pt-BR` row in `src/seo/languages.ts`, `src/seo/labs.pt-br.ts`, `'pt-BR':'/pt-br'` in
`SEO_PATH_PREFIX` (now checked equal to the registry's `pagePath`), a `/pt-br/*` header rule and `public/og-image-pt-br.png`. No
renderer, plugin, boot or `index.html` body change: `/pt-br/` is a static page like `/es/`, whose buttons open the app with
`?lang=pt-BR` (that load only, never stored), and every Portuguese page links its English and Spanish versions. The observatory
footer's «Sobre as sete práticas» links `/pt-br/labs/` through the same `labsIndexUrl(uiLanguage())`.

**`hreflang`: `pt-BR` and `pt`.** The ES-SEO review's rule (ISO 639-1 language, optional ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 region) admits `pt-BR`,
so it is listed first and names the Brazilian audience. `pt` is listed too, for the same URL: the app opens pt-BR for every `pt` and
`pt-*` browser (PT-REL, plan §4), so a searcher in Portugal, Angola or Mozambique is better served by the Portuguese page than by the
English x-default, as Spanish's `es` serves every `es-*` searcher. `<html lang>`, the JSON-LD `inLanguage` and the visible link's
`hreflang`/`lang` are `pt-BR`. When European Portuguese (pt-PT) pages exist, `pt` can move to whichever the project chooses as the
generic Portuguese page.

**Text rules.** The Portuguese texts are OpenVireo's web adaptation of its own English pages: Brazilian Portuguese with «você» and its
imperatives («Aponte», «Meça», «Registre», «Salve»), IAU OAE terms («desvio para o vermelho», «aglomerado estelar», «diagrama HR»,
«sequência principal», «largura equivalente», «radiotelescópio»), sentence case, the app's own pt-BR lab names («Classificação
estelar», «Fotometria das Plêiades», «Objeto X»), no claim to be CLEA's, and the footer sentence «Este texto em português é uma
adaptação web feita pelo OpenVireo, não um texto do CLEA.», with the credit to Project CLEA, Gettysburg College and the NSF as the
English pages have. The home page's «Como funciona» adds one line the Spanish one does not need: on screen, numbers use a decimal
point, as VIREO's files do, and separate thousands with a narrow space («24 000», U+202F; PT-REL's decision).

**Tests generalised per language.** `src/seo/languages.test.ts` no longer names Spanish in its checks:
- the texts of each language go through the **SC4 framework** (`src/i18n/lint/rules.ts`) with the language's profile and glossary
  (`es-419` with `glossary.json` field `es`, `pt-BR` with field `pt`): placeholders, html, glossary (terms, names, forbidden),
  register (including the pt-BR tu-imperative paired with an English imperative), claims, mark pairs, quotes, typography (the pt-BR
  European, Spanish and English words, title case, thousands), digits, NFC and invisible characters. Glyph parity and the length
  ratio are not applied: the pages are free prose (Spanish writes Hertzsprung-Russell with a hyphen). Strings with no English
  counterpart (the home texts, the open-lab note, the educational levels) are linted with an empty English, so terms and names do
  not apply and every pattern does. The Spanish texts passed the stricter framework unchanged; Spanish keeps its «cúmulo estelar»
  reading of "star cluster" as a per-language English adaptation;
- the dist-marker guard runs for **every registered language** (its catalog, maps and originals from `src/i18n/<dir>/`): its markers
  may appear only on its own `/<pagePath>/` pages, so a Portuguese text on an English or Spanish page fails, and vice versa;
- the alternates are listed explicitly (`en`, `es`, `pt-BR`, `pt`, `x-default`) and checked on all 27 pages and in the sitemap.
`tests/i18n/es-seo.spec.ts` became `tests/i18n/seo.spec.ts`, one loop over the table's translated languages (texts from the table
and `tests/i18n/languageText.ts`; the Spanish titles kept), with a new check that each translated index links the other translation.
`tests/release/pages-smoke.pages.ts`'s ES-SEO test is one loop too (the Spanish title kept).

**PULSRANL.TXT's thousands (the PT-REL note).** SC7 lifted the loader's U+00FF limit, but `helpTranslation.test.ts` still held the
Spanish and Portuguese help sets to Latin-1 by their own rule (`LATIN1_HELP`). The rule now lists, per set, the characters it may add
beyond Latin-1: none for Spanish, U+202F for Portuguese. PULSRANL's «2 400 000» uses U+202F, as the screen does elsewhere in
Portuguese. U+202F is a no-break space of one column (`textColumns.ts`), and `wrapHelpText` breaks only at U+0020, so the parsers treat
it exactly as U+00A0: the text-versus-byte parser test now compares the text parsers' output with U+202F read as U+00A0 against the
byte parsers on the same text with U+00A0, for every file. The pt-BR typography rule «ordinary space between thousands» now also fails
U+00A0 between thousands (one separator on screen; its fixture's U+00A0 case became the failing one), over the catalog, maps,
originals and the 38 help texts. No pt-BR text holds U+00A0 any more. The original help files are unchanged.

**Verification** (2026-11-10, worktree `claude/front-ptseo` from main b57a827). `npx tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/seo`, `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/deployHeaders.test.ts` and `src/core/cleaHelp.test.ts`, 901 tests in
35 files; `npm run build` with every check (`check-i18n-dist.mjs` passes unchanged; deploy limits 2,164 files, ten more than PT-REL:
nine pages and the image); the 27 `dist/` pages validated by a throwaway script (self canonical, the same five alternates in page
and sitemap, reciprocal, titles 44–64 and descriptions 145–160 characters, JSON-LD parses, `og:image` 1200×630) and `xmllint` on the
sitemap; Playwright (dev server, Chromium) `tests/i18n/seo.spec.ts` and `tests/seo-labs.spec.ts`, 30 tests, and
`tests/i18n/help.spec.ts`, 20 tests (PULSRANL's Portuguese help in the viewer); `npm run test:pages` on `dist/`, 74 tests (one
new: the PT-SEO loop iteration). The Portuguese home and an English lab page at 390 px were checked visually (three language links
wrap in the header; no overflow, as the 320 px tests also assert).

**Review (PT-SEO, 2026-11-10, independent reviewer).** The built `dist/` was checked against main b57a827 by rendering the SEO pages at
both revisions: the English and Spanish pages differ only by the `pt-BR`/`pt` alternates, `og:locale:alternate` `pt_BR` and the
«Português (Brasil)» link; the sitemap only by the nine Portuguese addresses and the two alternates per entry (`xmllint` passes).
`hreflang` `pt-BR` and `pt` on one URL is valid and consistent (both name the Portuguese page from every page; no other page claims
either). The preview image re-renders to the same SHA-256. PULSRANL's U+202F keeps «menos 2 400 000» on one line in the help viewer
at 320 px (checked in Chromium); the viewer's Find Topic matches titles only, so the body's separator does not affect search.
Corrections: the LSS and cluster-HR descriptions lacked the article («de prática» → «de uma prática»; the cluster one now «compare-a com
as suas Plêiades» to stay within 160 characters); «nada recebe nota» → «nada é avaliado»; the credit's «usa os seus arquivos de dados
originais» (whose files?) → «usa os arquivos de dados originais»; the home line on numbers no longer reads as if VIREO's files used the
thin space («usam ponto decimal, como os arquivos do VIREO, e separam os milhares com um espaço fino», the web help's own «espaço
fino»). `tests/i18n/seo.spec.ts` reads the Spanish texts from the table rather than as literals, so `src/seo/languages.test.ts` now
pins those nine Spanish texts to the literals `es-seo.spec.ts` asserted.

## Script groundwork: the deferred fixes (SC11)

Front SC11 (plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-scripts.md` §12 row SC11, and every place the plan, SC3, SC5, ES-REL and PT-REL
deferred to it). English, Spanish and Portuguese screens are unchanged except where the fix is the point (the axis titles below);
files the site writes are unchanged (exports identity).

- **Canvas font** (`src/components/canvasLabel.ts`, used by both canvases of `RadioReceiverWindows.tsx`). Canvas text names its own
  font, so the page's `:lang()` fallbacks never reached it. Under a language with a script stack (`--ov-font-script` of
  `scripts.css`: ja, zh, hi) the font is `bold 13px <that stack>,sans-serif`, so kanji, hanzi and Devanagari use the regional
  system face (system fonts only), and the label background grows to the text's measured ink. Without one (English, Spanish,
  Portuguese, Russian) the font is `bold 13px sans-serif` and the band 16 px, as before. Also fixed: the base display measured the
  untranslated text but drew the translated one, so a longer translation outran its background. Test: the scripts walk's
  canvas-label test (stacks per language; English and Russian exactly as before; the background covers the ink in ja and hi).
- **Print and screen dates** (`src/i18n/screenDate.ts`, decision 2026-11-09 (9)). The 13 `new Date().toLocaleString()` sites (print
  previews of the field list, hot list, cluster list and data, HR print, help print, variable stars ×2, Multi-Spec, spectrum print,
  web print; the cloud list's two, not deployed) call `screenDateTime(date)`: English keeps the browser's own format; other
  languages write the page language's (es-419, pt-BR…); always `-u-nu-latn-ca-gregory`, so an English page in an Egyptian or
  Persian browser no longer prints Arabic-Indic digits or the year 1405, nor a Japanese one an era year. All 13 are screen or print;
  no file uses it (the pulsar save header keeps its US Windows formats, AGENTS.md 2026-10-30 (2)). `screenDate.test.ts` also fails
  on any new bare `toLocaleString()` in `src/`. Visible change: a Spanish or Portuguese print in a browser of another region now
  shows the Spanish or Portuguese date order.
- **IME guards** (`imeComposing(e)` in `src/dialogKeyboard.ts`: `nativeEvent.isComposing||keyCode===229`). The 20 offenders SC5
  pinned (SampledFlux; PulsarAnalysis ×3; PulsarSavedRows ×2; RadioAccessOptions; RadioControlPanel ×2; RadioPulsarParameters ×2;
  RadioReceiverWindows ×2; RadioTelescopeRoute ×2; FieldInfoListWindow's Find; SiteDialogs ×4) ignore the Enter that confirms a
  conversion, and the four isComposing-only guards (RadioHotListSearchDialog, HotListEntryDialog, OpticalHotListWindow,
  OpticalSkyCoordsDialog) moved onto the helper. The audit now calls a guard with one test alone `half guarded`, an offender;
  `ime-enter-sites.json` has 0 offenders. Playwright: `observatory-site-time.spec.ts` checks Set Date/Time with isComposing,
  keyCode 229 and a plain Enter.
- **`padEnd` by display columns:** none needed. Every `padEnd`/`padStart`/`padRight` in `src/` pads digits or a native mirror
  (FITS cards, VIREO list lines, hot-list rows, LSS field items, catalog names and comments), which keep VIREO's format by character
  count; no translated screen text is padded. SC7's `displayColumns` already serves the help viewer.
- **The three silent replacements are visible** (scripts plan §10). Saving a VIREO-header FITS whose camera detector name has
  characters outside printable ASCII says that the DETECTOR card shows `?` for them (`camera.saveDetectorAscii`, after the save);
  the Object X spectrum's Save card says that the header writes `_` (and the file name `-`) for characters of the student's ID
  outside plain ASCII and for commas (`spectrumSave.asciiLabel`, before the reveal); Classify says when the ID read from a loaded
  file's name shows `?` (`class.observationIdReplaced`). The files are unchanged.
- **Axis titles** (`src/plotTextFit.ts`, `<g ref={keepInsideSvg}>`): the rotated y-axis titles of `PleiadesAnalysis.tsx` and
  `NativeClusterAnalysis.tsx` and the wavelength titles of `SpectrumInstrument.tsx`, `ClassificationLab.tsx` and `FeatureCursor.tsx`
  are measured as drawn (with the fonts they use) and moved back inside the SVG by what crosses its edge; up to half a pixel is
  left alone. The four SC3 `KNOWN` entries are gone (`KNOWN` is empty and the walk passes). Measured on the dev server: in
  Chromium no title moves in English, Spanish or Portuguese at 320 or 1440 px; in Firefox at 320 px the wavelength title moves
  up 4.4 units in the analyser and 2.8 in Classify in all three languages (English crossed too: Firefox's box for the 24 px
  phone title reaches below the SVG), which closes the 1 px overflow ES-REL and PT-REL recorded in the `narrow` sweeps.
- **Plurals.** `plural()` takes generic forms, and `pluralKey(count,forms)` (index.ts) picks a catalog key by the load's language.
  Converted: the `*Messages.ts` maps (camera peak count, wedge ×5, Object X movers, hot-list print rows, variable-star results)
  with their Spanish and Portuguese maps, and the Spanish and Portuguese naked-eye captions; the catalog pairs became CLDR names
  (`obsHr.*.many` → `.other`, `obsHr.kindWarning.{one,other}.{none,one,other}`, `sky.searchOne`/`sky.searchMatches` →
  `sky.searchMatches.one`/`.other` with `{count}`), so no English key spells "not one" as `many` (the test checks it). Portuguese:
  CLDR puts 0 and 1.5 in `one`, so `pluralCategory` keeps English's one/other for `pt` (and CLDR's `many`): «0 estrelas», as
  before. Three code sites stay pinned on purpose: `guideAvailability` (a sentence for the one-channel state, not a count), and
  VIREO's own hour/hours (`siteInformationLines`) and Minute./Minutes. (`requestDeniedText`) choices between two VIREO literals,
  kept as VIREO makes them (with one decimal VIREO prints «1.0 hour»; `minimumFractionDigits` would change that). The 86 counted
  messages stay for the language that needs forms (Russian adds `.few`/`.many` keys). Tests: the converted maps print what the
  `===1` choice printed for 0, 1, 2, 21 and 1 000 000 in English, Spanish and Portuguese.
- **Firefox `shell.spec.ts`** ("…does not arrive within 10 s…", Spanish and Portuguese): the test, not the app. Firefox holds the
  `load` event while a dynamic import started before it is pending, and the test held the language chunk, so `page.goto` (which
  waits for `load`) timed out; the app had already shown English. The test now waits for the navigation to commit; both pass in
  Firefox and Chromium.
- **Verification.** `tsc -b`; Vitest over `src/i18n` (601 tests, 23 files, before the last test additions; the four pinned files
  38 after them), `src/help`, `src/components` and `src/core` (4,257 in 327 files; one `cameraParameters` test hit its 5 s timeout
  under load and passed alone); pins refreshed and reviewed; `npm run build` with every check. Playwright (dev server, DejaVu
  fontconfig), Chromium: `tests/i18n` `radio`, `e01`, `e03`, `e04`, `e05`, `observatory`, `narrow`, `exports-identity` and `shell`,
  129; `observatory-site-time` (with the new IME test, which fails with the guard removed), `render-budget` and three radio
  receiver specs, 35; `npm run test:scripts`, 81 (the walk with `KNOWN` empty, and the new canvas-label test). Firefox: `narrow`
  2 of 2 (the 1 px axis-title overflow is gone; the first run's Spanish row failed on a cold dev server's first module load and
  passed rerun) and `shell` 22 of 22.

**Independent review (SC11)** (2026-11-10, Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort, over 883da06; fixes in the same worktree).
- **CI failure, fixed:** `src/theme/scripts.test.ts` ("imported once, from src/main.tsx") failed on the branch's first CI run
  because the `canvasLabel.ts` comment named the stylesheet's file; the comment now names the custom property only (the front's
  local verification had not run `src/theme`).
- **Notes shown only when a replacement happens, tightened:** the Object X Save note tested the whole label, so a character past the
  header's 24-character cut showed the note although the header kept no `_` for it; `objectXHeaderReplaces(label)`
  (`exposureSpectrum.ts`) compares the header written with the label, and `nativeFitsDetectorReplaces(name)` (`cameraFitsNative.ts`)
  does the same for the DETECTOR card. Unit tests for both; a Playwright test (`observation-metadata.spec.ts`) imports
  `star_星HD1.ssp`, sees Classify's note and the ID `?HD1`, and the existing test checks that a plain name shows no note.
- **No change proof.** The 11 renamed catalog keys have the same text in each of `en.json`, `es.json` and `pt-br.json` (`sky.searchMatches.one`
  prints «1» through `{count}` as before); no reference to an old key is left. Every converted map function (camera, wedge ×4,
  Object X, hot-list print, variable stars, naked eye; English, Spanish and Portuguese) was run against main's version for 0, 1, 2,
  3, 5, 11, 21, 100, 1 000, 1 000 000, 1 000 001 and 2 000 000: identical. `screenDateTime` is used only by print previews and the
  undeployed cloud list (its default upload name is an editable label, not a file); no writer imports it, so exports and the pulsar
  header's US formats are unchanged.
- **Print dates** (decision 2026-11-09 (9), and the scripts-plan decision that print dates follow the screen language for
  non-English languages): checked in Chromium and Firefox on an English page in en-US, en-GB, ar-EG, fa-IR, th-TH,
  ja-JP-u-ca-japanese and hi-IN browsers. en-US and en-GB print exactly what the bare call printed; ar-EG keeps its Arabic
  order and AM/PM marker with Western digits; fa-IR, th-TH and the Japanese era calendar print 2026; Spanish and Portuguese
  print their own order whatever the browser.
- **Verification (review).** `tsc -b`; Vitest `src/i18n`, `src/help`, `src/components` 888 in 38 files, `src/core` 3,971 in 312,
  `src/theme` 27; the pin refresh leaves the three pin files unchanged; `npm run build` with every check. Playwright, Chromium (dev

  its 5 s under load and passed alone), `e05`, `e08`, `observatory`, `narrow` 46, `exports-identity`, `shell`,
  `observatory-site-time`, `render-budget` and `observation-metadata` 53, four radio-receiver specs 38; `npm run test:scripts` 79 of
  81 (the two popup-menu placements timed out while another suite ran and passed alone). Firefox: `narrow` and `shell` 24 of 24.

## Front HOME: the welcome's keys (2026-11-10)

`home.*` (26 keys) holds the welcome page's text (`src/home/Welcome.tsx`; docs/SEO.md, "Front HOME"): `home.eyebrow`, `home.title`,
`home.lead`, `home.factsLabel`, the three facts `home.factInstall`, `home.factFree`, `home.factFaithful`, `home.explore` and
`home.exploreHint`, `home.labsHeading`, `home.labsHint`, the page name `home.pageName` (the page bar's
name for the welcome; not a `*.nav` key, so the per-language shell key counts are unchanged), and per lab a name and a one-line summary (`home.hubble`/`home.hubbleSummary`, `home.lss…`, `home.classification…`,
`home.pleiades…`, `home.clusterHr…`, `home.pulsars…`, `home.objectX…`). They were added to en.json, es.json and pt-br.json after
`sky.local` (and `home.pageName`, `home.explore`, `home.exploreHint` after `home.objectXSummary`) in the same order; no existing key
changed. They live in the core module (the welcome is not a lazy route area). The English text is also the pre-render of
`index.html` (`homeBoot`), which is English only, so no Spanish or Portuguese text reaches it.

- **es-419**: «tú» («Apuntas…, tomas…, mides el brillo…», as /es/ says); h1 «Las prácticas de astronomía de VIREO, de Project CLEA, reconstruidas para tu navegador» (review 1); the
  lab names are the `/es/` pages' names (`src/seo/labs.es.ts`); «computadora», «Chromebook» as «una Chromebook»; «cuásar»,
  «cúmulos de estrellas» (the es-419 lint requires «estrella» for "star clusters"),
  «radiotelescopio», «corrimiento al rojo» from the glossary.
- **pt-BR**: imperatives as the catalog addresses the reader («Aponte…, meça…, registre…»); h1 «As práticas de astronomia do VIREO,
  do Project CLEA, reconstruídas para o seu navegador» (review 1); «computador», «desvio para o vermelho», «aglomerados», «Plêiades», «Diagramas HR de
  aglomerados» as the pt-BR catalog writes HR.
- Implementation by a subagent (Claude Opus 5.5, medium effort); the translations were written in the same pass and checked by the
  catalog lints; the independent review is recorded in docs/SEO.md.

## RTL groundwork: the bidi runtime (RT4)

Front RT4 (plan `analysis/front-plans/front-i18n-rtl.md` § 3.2, § 4, § 5.2 and the RT4 row of § 9). Unicode isolates are added
**at runtime and only in a right-to-left load**; translators never type them (the lint's bidi-control rule still refuses them in
every catalog). English, Spanish and Portuguese run the same code as before: in a left-to-right load `t()`, `vireo()`, the maps and
the helpers return exactly what they returned on main, and files are unchanged (F1: no writer calls any of this).

- **Where the direction comes from.** `activateLocale(bundle,registry)` (`src/i18n/active.ts`) sets `Active.rtl` once per load from
  the language's `<html dir>` (`htmlDir`, RT1); `settleLanguage` passes the page's registry, so the runtime and `<html dir>` always
  agree (a test registry's Arabic is right-to-left in both; a right-to-left load whose chunk failed is English, `ltr` and isolates
  nothing). Today only the dev-only `qps-rtl` is right-to-left. RT1's `uiDirection()` now reads the same flag, so it agrees with
  `<html dir>` for a test registry too.
- **`src/i18n/isolates.ts`** (pure, no imports, so `catalog.ts` and `active.ts` can use it): FSI U+2068, LRI U+2066, PDI U+2069,
  `BIDI_CONTROLS`, `firstStrongIsolate`, `ltrIsolate` (leading and trailing white space stay outside the isolate; empty and
  white-space-only text is returned unchanged, so a falsy string stays falsy; so is text that is already one whole isolate, so
  `t()` or `vireo()` output passed to `t()` as a parameter keeps its one isolate instead of nesting), `hasRtlLetter` and
  `isolateMap` (a map's strings and its functions' string results, keeping keys, arrays, arity and frozenness).
- **`t()` parameters** (`src/i18n/catalog.ts`, the single substitution point; option `rtl`): each value is wrapped FSI…PDI, so
  "-12.5", "12h 30m", "+26° 42′" and "Hubble.HTL" keep their order and an Arabic name reads right to left.
- **Text with no right-to-left letter of its own is left to right as a whole** (`t()` and `vireo()`): LRI…PDI around an English
  fallback key, an untranslated VIREO original, every original under "View original (English)", and also a translated text whose
  template has no Arabic or Hebrew letter (a format such as "{band}: {catalog} {filter}", "{value}°", "{time} UT", or an acronym)
  when its values have none either. A translated format whose values bring right-to-left letters ("{name}: {value}" with an Arabic
  name) is left to the right-to-left paragraph, so it reads name first; an English fallback is left to right whatever its values.
  The plan named only the English fallbacks; the walk below showed the formats: their FSI parameters, alone in a right-to-left
  paragraph, were drawn in reverse order ("R NOMAD :NF").
- **`vireo()`, `vireoList()`, `vireoMenuItem()`** (`src/i18n/vireo.ts`): the lookup is unchanged; the menu item adds its ellipsis
  before isolating, so it is "Save As…" and never "…Save As". A `fragment` piece is never isolated alone: it is joined to other
  pieces and data in one sentence, and an isolate per piece would reorder that sentence in a right-to-left paragraph.
- **Maps** (`src/i18n/registry.ts` `resolveMessages`): the English a map falls back to (a missing map, area or leaf, or a map that
  did not match) is `isolateMap(english,{strings:true})`; a derived map (the pseudo-locales) gets `isolateMap(english,{strings:false})`
  before deriving, so its function results (English sentences with data) are left to right. A right-to-left language's own map
  strings and functions are the translator's: its functions wrap each `${…}` in `iso()` (lint rule: RT8).
- **`src/i18n/bidi.ts`** (load-aware): `iso(value)` for right-to-left map functions, `plotText(text)` for SVG `<text>` labels (FSI…PDI),
  `ltrText(text)` for English composed from several pieces (LRI…PDI); each returns its input unchanged in a left-to-right load.
  No site calls them yet: `plotText` at the ~40 label sites is RT12, `ltrText` at the composed sites RT7/RT12 list.
- **Numbers** (plan § 4): Western digits, the decimal point and en-US grouping in every language. `formatNumber`, `fixed` and
  `scientific` are unchanged; under `qps-rtl` and a test Arabic load they print exactly English's output for 14 values × 10 option
  sets and the three forms (`src/i18n/bidi.test.ts`), and the measurement and workbook export SHA-256 stay. A source rule (AST over
  `src/`) fails when any `new Intl.NumberFormat` or `toLocaleString` is given a first argument other than `locale`, `numberLocale`
  or `'en-US'` (screenDate.ts, SC11's date formatter with `-u-nu-latn-ca-gregory`, has its own test).
- **Chromium's ICU** (verified, the plan's [M] values were Node's): Chromium 156.0.8078.4 of `@playwright/test` gives the en-US
  formatter "12,345.678", "-12.5", "-0.5", "25%"; default numbering systems `ar` latn, `ar-EG` and `ar-SA` arab, `he` latn, `fa`
  arabext; `ar`, `he` and `ar-u-nu-latn` write a negative as U+200E "-12.5" and `fa` as U+200E U+2212 and Persian digits;
  `ar-u-nu-latn-ca-gregory` writes 2026-11-10 14:05:09 UTC as «10‏/11‏/2026، 2:05:09 م». So Chromium agrees with Node, and the site
  never asks an Arabic, Hebrew or Persian tag for a number (`tests/scripts/bidi.scripts.ts` records and checks these).
- **The bidi check** (`tests/fixtures/bidiProblems.ts` `ltrRunProblems`, the plan's RT7 check, written here so RT7's walk reuses it):
  rendered text that must read left to right is measured grapheme by grapheme with `Range` rectangles; on each line its characters
  must be drawn left to right in logical order (1 px tolerance; white space, controls, paired brackets, quotes and the pseudo
  markers are not placed by order). Kinds: `isolate` (the content of an isolate with no right-to-left letter: the runtime's own
  output), `node` (a whole text node with no right-to-left letter: a number, coordinate or English text rendered alone) and `run`
  (one Latin word in a node with right-to-left letters, with a sign before a leading digit and an ellipsis after a trailing
  letter: "-12.5", "As…"). Words are checked one by one in mixed text, since between two words of a right-to-left sentence the
  reading order is right to left; only a whole node or an isolate says several words form one value.
- **`tests/scripts/bidi.scripts.ts`** (under `npm run test:scripts`, 4 tests): (1) on controlled text in a right-to-left page the
  check finds each case the runtime exists for — a negative parameter, a lone number and coordinate, a sign with a unit, a format
  with no Arabic letter, "Save As…", "RA:", an English fallback in an Arabic sentence, English pieces composed with data, and a
  negative SVG tick label — and the runtime's isolate fixes each one; the Latin cases have no problem in a left-to-right page;
  (2) under `qps-rtl` on `observatory`, `spectrum`, `photometer`, `variable-stars`, `pulsar-analysis` and `lss-survey`, with and
  without View original, every page has FSI parameters (and LRI originals with View original), no `isolate` problem and no page
  error; (3) Chromium's ICU, above.
- **Outside the runtime, counted for RT7** (not asserted; measured with `qps-rtl` at 1440 px): `node`/`run` problems per route —
  observatory 8/3, spectrum 3/4, photometer 9/7, variable-stars 106/0, pulsar-analysis 0/0, lss-survey 1/3. They are numbers and
  coordinates rendered straight into elements (`-77.240556`, `+0.000000`, the variable-star declinations and type codes such as
  `EA/D:`), a list of formats joined with "; " (in a right-to-left reading the first item is at the right, which may be right for a
  list), hard-coded text such as " (.TXT)", Latin words with an ellipsis inside `qps-rtl` sentences, and ranges ("1900–2099",
  which plan § 3.4 says reads correctly right to left as "2099–1900"; RT7 decides whether the check counts them).

**LTR identity proof.**
- *Strings (Vitest):* in English, Spanish, Portuguese, `qps`, `qps-cjk`, `qps-deva` and `qps-cyrl`, every one of the 3,568 keys
  through `t()` with sample values (signs, coordinates, file names, an Arabic word, the empty string) equals the plain substitution
  of its catalog text; every picked map resolves to the same object (the bundle's own map for Spanish and Portuguese, the English map
  for English) with no bidi control in any leaf; every original of `es/originals.json` and `pt-br/originals.json` (754) through
